# CLI Reference

The CLI is the primary interface — the browser UI currently covers MLB and
soccer views only, so NFL and the cup competitions are operated entirely
from here. Every command: `python cli.py <command> [options]` from the
repo root with the venv active.

Conventions: soccer seasons are `"2026/27"`; MLB and NFL seasons are
`2026`. Competition codes: `PL`, `ELC`, `EL1`, `EL2`, `EFL` (League Cup),
`FAC`, `CL`, `UEL`, `CS`, `MLB`, `NFL`. Dates are `YYYY-MM-DD`.

---

## Setup & discovery

| Command | Options | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `init-db` | `--force` | Create the SQLite schema (`data/sports.db`). |
| `sync-competitions` | `--sport` | Register the curated competition list for a sport. |
| `sync-teams` | `--competition --season` | Pull all teams for a competition-season. **A historical-season listing never renames (ARCHITECT 2026-11-01, addendum 204 item 2, #764):** an existing team listed for a season OLDER than its newest stored membership season (any competition of the same sport, by the leading four-digit year: "2026/27" and "2026" both read 2026; a string with no leading year reads unknown) gets its provider ids and the membership written, and keeps its name and short_name; the run prints `names kept: N (a historical-season listing)` and the kept/listed pairs (at most 25, then `... and N more`). Equal, newer, unknown, no membership yet, or no `--season`: renames as today. **The DB lock (#569 RULED, ARCHITECT 2026-10-21, addendum 153 item 2: "a run holds the DB lock the host's chains hold (the laptop's syncs today take none)"):** run by hand, it takes the chains' own flock (`sp_common.db_lock`: `SP_LOCK`, else `logs/db.lock`) for the whole run and prints `… waiting for the DB lock <path> (a chain or closing run holds it)` while another run holds it; run as a step of a chain, closing or slate run that holds the lock (`SP_DB_LOCK_HELD` in its environment, `<pid>:<lock file>`, set by `db_lock` and inherited by every step, naming an ancestor's pid), it takes none. The same lock is taken at the ingestion service (`IngestionService.sync_teams` / `sync_matches`, around the prefetch and every write), so the admin UI's sync jobs hold it too, each job thread in turn (Codex on #650). **SP_SYNC_HOLD (#921; ARCHITECT 2026-11-10, addendum 332 item 3):** a code the setting names is held: the step prints `HELD <code> (SP_SYNC_HOLD)`, builds no adapter, writes nothing and exits 0 (docs/specs/hosting-h1.md section O). |
| `status` | | What's in the local DB (row counts per table). |
| `find-team` | `<name>` | Verify a team exists and see its ids. |
| `db-tune` | `--vacuum` | One-shot DB health pass: WAL, ANALYZE, composite indexes, probe timing. |
| `data-freshness` | `--sport --season` | Diagnose whether model inputs are current. |
| `model-versions` | | Trained model versions and their status (production / held / rejected / shelved). |
| `show-config` / `set-config` | `--sport --field --value --yes` | Inspect / edit the production model's frozen config (gated; logs manual overrides). |

## Core data syncs (all sports)

| Command | Options | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `sync-matches` | `--competition --season --seasons N --date-from --date-to` · `--match-ids` | Schedules, scores, statuses. **The DB lock (#569 RULED, ARCHITECT 2026-10-21, addendum 153 item 2: "a run holds the DB lock the host's chains hold (the laptop's syncs today take none)"):** run by hand, it takes the chains' own flock (`sp_common.db_lock`: `SP_LOCK`, else `logs/db.lock`) for the whole run and prints `… waiting for the DB lock <path> (a chain or closing run holds it)` while another run holds it; run as a step of a chain, closing or slate run that holds the lock (`SP_DB_LOCK_HELD` in its environment, `<pid>:<lock file>`, set by `db_lock` and inherited by every step, naming an ancestor's pid), it takes none. The same lock is taken at the ingestion service (`IngestionService.sync_teams` / `sync_matches`, around the prefetch and every write), so the admin UI's sync jobs hold it too, each job thread in turn (Codex on #650). **A statsapi gamePk is looked up across the sport's rows (#569 RULED: "The statsapi upsert looks a gamePk up across the sport's rows, not the competition's"):** a gamePk already stored on any MLB row is updated, never created again (`service.SPORT_WIDE_SOURCES`); every other source's lookup stays the competition's own. **NCAA and NFL (api_american_football) take one date, never a range (ARCHITECT 2026-10-17, addendum 106 item 3, #570):** with `--date-from` ≠ `--date-to` the range is not sent, the whole `--season` listing is read and written, and the run says so before the fetch (`RANGE A..B is not supported by this provider: reading and writing the whole <season> listing`) and in the STRICT line's label; `resync-diff` says `reading`. `--seasons N` backfills N seasons (sport-aware season strings). **Run `sync-teams` for the same competition-season first:** listings whose clubs are not in the DB are skipped, and the command prints `skipped N listing(s) of clubs not in the DB: <ids>` on its own line (ARCHITECT 2026-11-01, addendum 204 item 3(c); #728: never only a log warning; the exit stays 0, law 4: skipped, not faked). A chain step that prints it is named in the chain's summary line (`listings skipped (clubs not in the DB): step i/n CODE N`), in the chain receipt's `listings_skipped`, and in the receipts table's chain key line (`sp_run.py`). The host's chains run `sync-teams` immediately before `sync-matches` for every line of `SP_FULLSEASON_LIST` (`weekly-fullseason`, #765) and for each cup of `intl-daily` (EFL, CL, UEL, FAC, #728), under the chain's one DB lock; the chain receipt's `competitions` carries, per competition-season, sync-teams' created/updated and sync-matches' skipped count, one printed line each. A failed `sync-teams` step does not stop either chain (ARCHITECT 2026-11-01, addendum 207 item 4; not `continue_on_sync_failure`, which stays unset on both): that competition's `sync-matches` and every later step still run, its skipped-listings line says what the failed teams read cost, the chain's exit is the FIRST failure's, and the summary line names the step (`sync-teams failed (the chain ran on, addendum 207 item 4): step i/n CODE exit N`); a failed `sync-matches` still stops them as before. **MLB on a host whose `SP_SKIP_FAMILIES` names MLB** (PHASE A, 2026-09-29) syncs from the api-sports Baseball fallback instead of statsapi: teams first, then the season's `/games` in one call (`--season` required; a date window filters locally); stage stays NULL on rows it creates; prints an `MLB-FALLBACK-RECEIPT` line with the doubleheader-game-2 limitation. `sync-teams --competition MLB` routes the same way. `--match-ids 1,2` (opt-in, the closing runs' schedule read for NFL, SOCCER and MLB, addendum 23 A1 as amended by addendum 25 2(i)): exit 1 unless the provider's answer held every named game; the sync itself is unchanged. **The series fields (ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 130 item 2(b); #476, #478):** the laptop's statsapi MLB sync stores each listed game's own `seriesGameNumber`, `gamesInSeries`, `seriesDescription` and `rescheduledFrom` on its match row (`matches.series_game_number`, `games_in_series`, `series_description`, `rescheduled_from`; the last as ISO-8601 UTC `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`). A field the game's entry does not give is stored NULL; it is never taken from another game. A game listed twice in one answer (a postponed game on its old and new dates) keeps the fields of its last entry, the one its status and kickoff were last written from. Only once `migrate_match_series_fields.py` has added the columns (unmapped, guarded Core SQL, the ORDER LINE note): before it, the sync writes its rows as before and nothing else. The api-sports fallback writes none of them. Data only: no export, Desk, matcher or card reads them; the one reader is `retire-orphans`' receipt (2) (#568). Run order: any (backup first, by hand). No backfill: the next statsapi sync over a window writes the rows it lists. The migration's receipt counts, per competition, the rows carrying each field. **status_raw (ARCHITECT 2026-10-20, addendum 146 item 3(b); #568):** "The statsapi adapter stores codedGameState verbatim in status_raw on every row it writes, as the api-football, api-hockey and api-sports paths do": each listed game's `codedGameState` as given (`S`, `P`, `F`, `D`, an unmapped code too), read before the adapter's `S` default; an entry without one, or with one that is not a non-blank text, gives none, and an update never blanks a stored code with an absent one (an entry without a code keeps the stored one). The mapped status and the pre-game score nulling are unchanged. A row the provider no longer lists keeps what it had (NULL on rows last listed before this). Data only: no MLB reader gates on it (`retire-orphans` prints it on every series row). **status_raw on NFL and NCAA (api_american_football; ARCHITECT 2026-11-01, addendum 202 item 3; #763, #493):** the provider's status `short` verbatim (`NS`, `FT`, `AOT`, `CANC`, an unmapped code too), read before the adapter's `NS` default; an absent status (no block, `{}`, a null, empty or `"None"` short) stores none, never `NS`, and an update never blanks a stored code with an absent one. The status mapping of every present code is unchanged; the score-presence rule now fires as designed: a game with no status and both final totals is FINISHED (an explicit code, `NS` included, is never overridden). Rows take the code at their next sync, or at `refresh-by-id`'s next refresh of a stored SCHEDULED row (the same parse; it writes the answer's code and never blanks a stored one). **Scores by team (ARCHITECT 2026-11-08, addendum 292 item 3; #876):** an update of a stored row (by its provider id, or by a re-key) writes the listing's scores into the STORED orientation: the same pair as today; the pair REVERSED, every score pair (score, `_ht`, `_90`) swapped and `full_time_result` H<->A (D unchanged), the kickoff and status as served; neither the stored pair nor its reverse, the update REFUSED (scores, kickoff and status untouched; `provider_orientation` written `mismatch`, addendum 297 C(ii)) and counted in `skipped`. The stored home/away is never flipped (item 3(d)). Item 3(c): "two new counts on the sync result and its printed receipt, each with the match ids LISTED": every run prints `orientation_reversed N: <ids> · teams_mismatch_refused M: <ids>` on its own line, zero counts included (the tennis re-read prints it again for its own sync, `<CODE> re-read: ...`); with `--match-ids`, a named row whose listing was refused prints `✗ STRICT: N of M named game(s) REFUSED (the listing's teams are neither the stored pair nor its reverse; scores, kickoff and status untouched; provider_orientation mismatch written): match <id>` (the text of addendum 298 A item 3), never as not in the answer (exit 1). **The provider id is the season's (ARCHITECT 2026-11-09, addendum 310 item 3, #902):** for every source that is not SPORT_WIDE (every source but `mlb_stats_api`) a provider id finds a stored row only of the same competition AND the same season as the listed row (each listed row by its own season, compared by `season_year` on both sides, addendum 312 item 1: "2026/27", "2026-27" and "2026" read one season); the prefetch cache keys a lookup (id, season_year), and the re-key index (the natural-key pairs, the `<source>_prev` history, the NCAA placeholder window) is the season's too, so an annual rematch is never a re-key target. A stored row of ANOTHER season carrying the listed id is never touched: the listing creates its own row (its season, its home/away), and the run prints `provider_id_collision N: <stored id> (<season>) ← <listing id>, …` on its own line right after the summary, only when N > 0: N counts the NEW rows created because the listed id was held only by a row of another season (addendum 312 item 2; once the listing's own season has its row, the sync updates it and counts nothing). A listing whose season has no leading year (none at all: a sync without `--season` whose provider stamps none, e.g. api_american_football by date) or whose id is carried only by a stored row whose season has no leading year is refused, nothing written, counted in `skipped`, and printed as `provider_id_season_unknown N: <listing ids>` (law 4; only when N > 0). A reversed pair on a row of the SAME season is a reversal (the scores-by-team chain); across seasons it is a collision and never reaches the chain (item 3(e)). **SP_SYNC_HOLD (#921; ARCHITECT 2026-11-10, addendum 332 item 3):** a code the setting names is held: the step prints `HELD <code> (SP_SYNC_HOLD)`, builds no adapter, writes nothing and exits 0 (docs/specs/hosting-h1.md section O). |
| `sync-odds` | `--competition --season` · `--match-ids 1,2` | Book odds for upcoming games (soccer & MLB bulk path). `--match-ids` (soccer only; 2026-10-09, addendum 21 item 3 C3) prices only those stored games: the closing run's. Default unchanged. **Order and limit (addendum 31 item 1, amended by addendum 33 item 2):** "A named game is never dropped by the limit. With --match-ids every named game is priced and --limit does not apply. The order is kickoff, then id, in both modes." Until #402 the limit (default 20) cut a named list. **MLB, the provider's answer when books are 0 (ARCHITECT 2026-10-16, addendum 97 item 2(a), #550/#514):** the sync records each game it leaves with no complete book and the provider's answer, and the command names them on one plain line, printed just before its `✓ MLB odds` summary (inside a closing run's five-line step tail; the summary stays the step receipt's key line): `NO-BOOK-ODDS (MLB): N game(s) with no book odds: match <id> [<answer>] <away> @ <home> (game <id>); …`. These are the per-game fallback's games and any bulk-matched game stored with no complete book. The answers: `provider listed the game, no odds`; no game in the provider's listing matched it; the games listing read failed; a doubleheader id already taken; a failed odds read; a started game; no complete book. When the fallback never runs (an empty or failed odds window, no key), the line is `NO-BOOK-ODDS (MLB): run [<answer>]`. No line when the sync named no game and its odds window answered. **`source_updated_at` (ARCHITECT addendum 137 item 4, #615):** every odds row and every book-consensus snapshot it appends stores the adapter's quote time: soccer, api-football's `response[].update` (addendum 147 item 1); `--competition NFL|NCAA`, american football's `response[].update`; MLB (api-baseball, #628) and NHL (hockey), null, because their answers carry none. A snapshot takes the oldest over the rows its close priced (`close.priced_rows`), and one unknown row makes it unknown. Written only once `migrate_odds_source_updated_at.py` has added the columns (guarded Core SQL, addendum 35 item 2); before that the field reads null everywhere. No backfill. **By team (ARCHITECT 2026-11-08, addendum 295 item 5(b), #885; addendum 297 C):** `external_ids["provider_orientation"]` records what the provider's current listing of the row's id says about it: absent = same, `reversed`, `mismatch` (written by the sync-matches upsert and by `refresh-by-id`). On a `reversed` row the provider's HOME selection is stored as AWAY and the mirror (a spread's line travels with its price; draw and totals as served), and the 1X2 snapshot follows. A `mismatch` row is REFUSED: not fetched, no odds row or snapshot written, its stored prices as they were. The command prints `orientation_swapped N: <ids>` and then `orientation_mismatch_unpriced N: <ids>`, each on its own line before its summary (the summary stays the step receipt's key line), zeros included; a chain's receipt and summary line carry both counts (`sp_run`, addendum 297 C(iii)). |
| `sync-injuries` | `--competition --season` · `--kickoff-within-hours N` · `--match-ids` · `--strict` | Current injury/status report per team. NFL: positions joined from the roster endpoint. `--kickoff-within-hours N` (2026-09-29) scopes the sync to the teams in this competition's scheduled games kicking off within N hours. That is the window service's imminent-tier step. It makes no provider call when nothing is inside the window. `--strict` (2026-10-09, addendum 22, closing chains only): exits 1 when a read it needed failed (a team's fetch failed, a team with no provider id, the competition or a team missing), listing each (`✗ STRICT: …`). Without it the command behaves as before: a failed read is counted as skipped and it exits 0. `--match-ids 1,2` scopes to those games' teams (the closing chains; not with `--kickoff-within-hours`). `--strict`: exit 1 when a read it needed failed (a fetch, a team with no provider id, the competition or a named game missing; NFL: a failed or empty roster read, addendum 23 A2). **Empty answers (ARCHITECT 2026-10-11, addendum 40 item 4):** every team whose provider answer held no injury entry is named on one plain line, `EMPTY-ANSWER (<COMP>): <n> team(s) answered no injury entry, stored empty (read as unread): <team>; <team>`; its list is still replaced with what the provider answered, and `--strict` does not fail on it (the provider answered). A closing run records the names in its receipt (`injury_lists_empty`, and the step's `empty_answers`). **The position lookup (ARCHITECT 2026-10-12, addendum 44 item 4; NFL only: `--competition NCAA`, which shares the adapter, makes none):** a listed player whose position the roster does not give is asked for by his provider id (`/players?id=`); only the answer's `position` field is taken. The answer is kept in `player_position_answers` (created by `migrate_player_position_answers.py`; before it runs the lookup is still made and nothing is kept, so the player is asked again at the next read), so a player is asked once, not at every read. **Only an answer that gives a position is kept** (addendum 46 item 2): an answer that gives none, an empty answer included, leaves the player unresolved and counted; it is not a failed read, it is not kept, and he is asked again at the next read. A quarterback so found is a quarterback for the rule. **The entries of the read (ARCHITECT 2026-10-12, addendum 47 item 3, #454; it replaces #440's ambiguous rule):** an entry is told apart by its provider id. One id listed more than once is one entry, the first. Entries that share a name and differ in id are different players: each is kept and resolved by its own id (roster, then the lookup), never by a name. An entry with no id is resolved by name as today. It is not kept where an entry with an id has its name. Entries with no id that share a name are one entry, the first. (the fourth sentence as addendum 48 item 3 rewrote it) A name the roster holds more than once (by the join's name comparison: "Michael Carter" and "Michael Carter II" are one name) resolves nobody by name. **A group is not a position (addendum 47 item 4, #451):** in the roster join, as in the lookup, only the `position` field resolves a player; a group-only roster row leaves him unresolved, and the NFL read asks him by id. One plain line, before the empty-answers line: `POSITION-LOOKUP (<COMP>): asked by provider id <n> (answered a position <n>, answered none <n>, failed <n>) · from kept answers <n> · resolved by lookup <n> · kept <n> new` (`kept` counts positions only), then `lookup failed: …` lines. `--strict`: a lookup that fails (an error, or a provider answer with errors) is a failed read; an answer that holds no position is not. No cap on the lookups of a read. **The provider player id (ARCHITECT 2026-10-17, addendum 112 item 10, #458):** an NFL / NCAA row stores its entry's own provider id (`response[].player.id`) in `injuries.player_source_id`, null for an entry with none; only once `migrate_injury_player_source_id.py` has added the column (unmapped, guarded Core SQL, the ORDER LINE note): before it, the rows are written as before with no id. Run order: any (backup first, by hand). No backfill: the next sync rewrites each team's rows with their ids. |
| `capture-odds` | `--sport --competition --season` | Lightweight odds-only capture for close tracking. Runs the MLB odds sync, which appends the 1X2 book-consensus snapshot itself (2026-10-02), then snapshots TOTALS. Only games before first pitch are captured (#174). |
| `mlb-odds-timing` | `--start --end --only-missing` | Read-only: when api-sports first priced each MLB game (from odds_snapshots), with UTC-rollover/night flags and a per-game verdict (#155). |
| `wipe-injuries` | `--confirm` | Destructive reset of the injuries table. |

## MLB daily operation

Pre-slate chain, in order: `sync-matches --competition MLB --date-from <today> --date-to <today>` → `sync-bullpen-stats` →
`sync-pitchers` → `sync-pitcher-stats` → `sync-odds` → `sync-kalshi` →
`predict` → `sync-umpires --today` → `capture-weather` → `export-predictions`.

Morning chain (laptop), split into **two phases by network mode**
(architect 2026-10-01). The two modes conflict, so never run them together.

1. **Phase 1, VPN on (the statsapi steps).** Open with the backup line,
   then run the morning grading: `sync-matches` → `evaluate` → `improve` →
   `sync-appearances --recent` → `sync-umpires --recent` →
   `export-results` → `desk-unit` (the day's unit for the record,
   addendum 179 item 3: a Kalshi read, not statsapi, its own call; the
   laptop's pages size from its file, and an unread day writes no order; the pool and the unit print only when
   the shell itself carries `SP_EXPORTS_MIRROR_ROLE=laptop`, never from the checkout's .env: addendum 183 item
   2(1)). The statsapi calls are `sync-matches`,
   `sync-appearances` and `sync-umpires`. The rest is local, so it runs
   in this phase too. Then, after the MLB sync and never inside the watch,
   the retire step (ARCHITECT 2026-10-20, addendum 146 item 3(a), #568:
   "explicit, dry run first, in the morning block after the MLB sync"):
   the dry run `python cli.py retire-orphans --competition MLB --season 2026`
   (at most one statsapi call), pasted with the morning receipts; then, for
   the rows it planned `retire`, a fresh `.backup` and `python cli.py
   retire-orphans --competition MLB --season 2026 --apply --backup PATH
   --match-ids <the ids the dry run planned>`. Nothing planned, nothing to
   apply. Then the college kickoffs (ARCHITECT 2026-10-09,
   addendum 21 item 1: "at least once a day on the host and in the
   laptop's morning chain"): first the listing reads, `sync-matches
   --competition NCAA --season 2026 --date-from D --date-to D` for each of
   D = today .. today+7, one day per call; then `refresh-by-id --competition
   NCAA --days 7`. ARCHITECT 2026-10-09, addendum 30 item 4:
   "The laptop's morning chain reads NCAA by date for today to today+7, one day per call, and then runs refresh-by-id, as ncaa-schedule does on the host. After a re-key the listing reads carry the new ids and the refresh confirms them; in the other order every re-keyed game prints as not found."
   These are api-sports calls, not statsapi, like the pre-slate chain's
   `sync-odds`. Paste the `sync-matches` lines and the `REFRESH-BY-ID` lines
   with the morning receipts.
2. **Switch.** QUIT the VPN client; disconnecting is not enough, because
   it can keep its tunnel or DNS hooks (hosting-h1 field amendment
   2026-09-28). Then bring Tailscale up.
3. **Phase 2, Tailscale on (the host steps).** Run
   `python deploy/hosting/pull_exports.py`, then
   `python deploy/hosting/compare_exports.py exports exports/host`. The
   compare defaults to `--since 3`, so settled exhibits stop re-printing.
   Then paste the host receipts table
   (`ssh sp@sp-vps-1 'sudo -u sp venv/bin/python deploy/hosting/sp_receipts.py --since 24h'`).

The pre-slate chain's statsapi steps (`sync-matches`, `sync-bullpen-stats`,
`sync-pitchers`, `sync-pitcher-stats`, `sync-umpires --today`) also run
under the VPN.

| Command | Options | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `retire-orphans` | `--competition MLB --season 2026` · `--apply --backup PATH --match-ids ID,ID` | **RETIRE-ORPHANS FOR MLB (ARCHITECT 2026-10-20, addendum 146 item 3(a); #568).** The ruling: "a SCHEDULED MLB row becomes STALE_ORPHAN on TWO receipts, both printed per row: (1) the row's provider id is absent from the provider's answer for a window that covers its date (the strict read's own evidence, one call), and (2) the series arithmetic from the #605 fields shows the series decided before this row's game number: the pair's FINISHED games of the same season and stage, wins per side, against games_in_series of any row of that series (needed = ceil(gis/2)). Either receipt missing -> the row stays SCHEDULED (law 4). Never CANCELLED: the provider said nothing." Explicit, dry run first, in the morning block after the MLB sync (Phase 1, above), never inside the watch: no chain or closing step runs it. Candidates: the season's SCHEDULED rows with a postseason stage (F/D/L/W) and no score; every other SCHEDULED row of the season already past its start (stage R or none included) is printed `[not a candidate]` with why. **Receipt (1):** one GET for the run (none when there is no candidate), the season's postseason schedule (`{sportId 1, gameType F,D,L,W, season}`, no dates, as `mlb-time-audit` reads it), with its params, game count, dates and every gamePk printed: ABSENT when the answer lists one game or more, not the row's gamePk (read raw from every entry, so an entry the sync would skip still lists its game), and at least one stored gamePk of the row's own series (its other rows; the count is printed): an answer that lists no game of the series is no evidence about it; LISTED when it lists the row's gamePk; NO RECEIPT when the call fails, the answer lists no game, the row has no `mlb_stats_api` id, or the answer lists no game of the row's series (`the answer lists no game of this series`). **Receipt (2):** from the stored rows only. The series is the rows of the row's season and stage with its two teams either way round, of every status; `games_in_series` is the one value its rows carry, needed = (gis + 1) // 2; wins per side over its FINISHED games. DECIDED when a side has `needed` wins over the k FINISHED games and the series ended before this row's game: its own game number after the clinch's (the clinch's stored game number, which must equal k; with none stored the receipt says `after k FINISHED games` (with none stored, k stands in for it, and a FINISHED row storing a number of k or more is no receipt: a FINISHED game of the series is not stored; with no number stored on any FINISHED row, a FINISHED game not stored cannot be seen)), or, with its own number null, the clinch's start before its own (`game number: null (own); read by start`; at the same start, NOT DECIDED). NO RECEIPT for `games_in_series` null on every row or disagreeing, a `games_in_series` under 1 or even (a best-of-N is odd: an even N lets both sides reach `needed`, a tie), the series columns not migrated (`migrate_match_series_fields.py`), a LIVE game of the series, a FINISHED game without a decided score, two FINISHED rows of one gamePk or one game number, more FINISHED games than `games_in_series`, a side past `needed`, two FINISHED games sharing the latest start, two FINISHED games on one ET day (one game stored twice under two ids; #631 review), a clinch (the latest FINISHED game) the other side won, or a clinch whose stored game number is not k (`a FINISHED game of the series is not stored (clinch is game N, k FINISHED)`, or more FINISHED rows than its number). Two more guards, a FINISHED game or the row with no start, cannot fire on a current-schema DB (`matches.utc_date` is NOT NULL). `series_description` is printed, never a gate. Each series row is printed with its gamePk, game number, start, status, `status_raw` and score. The plan: `retire` only on ABSENT and DECIDED; every other row stays SCHEDULED, labelled `listed`, `not decided`, `no receipt 1` or `no receipt 2`, both receipts printed. **`--apply`** needs `--backup PATH` (a `.backup` taken just before: the `dedupe-matches` check, its matches count equal to the live DB's, so the morning's opening backup is behind once the sync has created a row) and `--match-ids` naming the rows the dry run planned `retire` (as `merge-teams` names its pairs; `--match-ids` without `--apply` is refused). It plans again in its own session (one call); a named row whose fresh plan is `retire` becomes `STALE_ORPHAN` with `external_ids.mlb_stats_api_orphaned_at` (nothing else on the row changes; nothing is CANCELLED or deleted); a named row planned otherwise is `REFUSED` and stays; a row planned `retire` but not named stays (`[not named]`). The state line counts the stale orphans, before and after. Refusals, each exit 2, no provider call, nothing written: a competition other than MLB; `SP_SKIP_FAMILIES` naming MLB (the host's api-sports fallback); a `--season` that is not digits; no database file at the configured path (law 5: never created, the migrations' shared guard, #524); `--apply` without `--backup` or `--match-ids`; the backup check; a DB behind the code. Exit 1, no provider call, when MLB is not stored (`competition MLB not in DB`). Exit 0 on a completed plan, whatever stays. A retired row is no game to the readers that void or skip it: `closing.VOID` (the closing watch, the slate stage, `coverage` and the Today tile), the Kalshi matcher, `export-fixtures`, and `predict` (SCHEDULED rows only). **Self-heal:** a later sync that lists the gamePk again finds the row by its id and sets its status from the listing; the `mlb_stats_api_orphaned_at` marker stays on the row. **Not changed here:** `export-predictions`' default status for today's range is all, so a row retired on its own game day stays in that day's file with status `stale_orphan` (a question for the architect on #568). |

The dry run lists every such gamePk with its class, the kept id and the reason: class 1 (both FINISHED, one score, one kickoff minute) and class 4 (both FINISHED, one score, two kickoffs) by (a), class 4 keeping the earlier row and taking the later row's date; class 2 (FINISHED and SCHEDULED) keeps the FINISHED row; class 3 (POSTPONED and its make-up) keeps the make-up. (a): the kept row is the one holding references (odds, odds_snapshots, predictions, prediction_history, kalshi_settled_markets); neither: the lower id; both: REFUSED and listed. Also REFUSED and listed: three rows on one gamePk, two FINISHED rows with different scores, any other pair of statuses, a class-4 later row holding references. Per group it prints both rows, the state the kept row takes (utc_date, status, status_raw, scores, full_time_result; a NULL never blanks), the rows re-pointed and the rows left on the retired row; then class 2's predictions of a game already played (the record line), the training set's FINISHED rows per season before and after, and the `--gamepks` line to apply. **`--apply`** needs `--backup PATH` (the `dedupe-matches` check) and `--gamepks` naming the groups; it plans again under the chains' DB lock, and a named gamePk the fresh plan does not merge is REFUSED and left. Each merged group: the kept row takes the state; the references, `pitcher_appearances` and `umpire_games` move to it, and any other table moves only where the kept row holds none (else the retired row keeps its own, counted); the other row becomes `STALE_ORPHAN`, its `mlb_stats_api` id moved to `mlb_stats_api_prev` with `mlb_stats_api_merged_into` and `mlb_stats_api_deduped_at` (so a sync finds the kept row only); the kept row records `mlb_stats_api_merged_from`. A class-2 prediction re-pointed carries `factor_breakdown.dedupe_569` and `evaluate_finished` never grades it. Nothing is deleted. Refusals, exit 2, nothing written: no database file at the configured path (never created); `--gamepks` without `--apply`, `--apply` without `--gamepks`, a gamePk that is not digits; the backup check; a DB behind the code. The readings: `src/ingestion/mlb_twins.py`. |

| `sync-kalshi` | `--date-from --date-to` | Kalshi MLB game markets (second market source). `--date-from D1 --date-to D2` keeps our stored kickoffs from D1 00:00Z through the whole UTC day D2, both inclusive (`--date-to` is the whole day since #205). The window chain's MLB step passes `{today}` and `{tomorrow}`, so its window ends at {tomorrow}+1 day (#175, ARCHITECT 2026-10-17, addendum 112 item 8). **Where a market is placed (the switch, addendum 50 6(b)):** `kalshi_match.place`: the event's two names, from its own markets, fit a game of the series' own competition one each, on the ticker's day (US Eastern); on this clock series the ticker's own date gives the game (addendum 52 item 4) and a G1/G2 event goes by its clock; the run's window is read last, on the one game chosen; every refusal is printed by its reason. **The pair (#413, ARCHITECT 2026-11-03, addendum 230 item 0(a)):** the event's two names come from its sibling markets (each market's `yes_sub_title`), as on the soccer path since 09-13; the yes-team fallback is gone (`KalshiAdapter.yes_team` removed). **The "A vs B" title fallback, NARROWED (ARCHITECT 2026-11-03 13:05 ET, addendum 234 item 2: "NARROWED, and it never overrides"):** two sibling names are the pair and the title is never read; three or more sibling names are refused `the event's markets do not name two teams` (NO_PAIR) and the title is never read (the override of conflicting siblings is removed); only where the siblings name fewer than two teams (one market listed, a market with no name) is the event's first priced market's title read (`kalshi_match._pair`, `_title_teams`: the "A vs B ..." form by `KalshiAdapter.title_teams`, unchanged; the question form kalshi-history's API reads carry, "Will <Y> win the <A> vs <B> <LEAGUE> game?", parsed strictly and case-insensitively, LEAGUE one of the closed `QUESTION_LEAGUES` ("Pro Football", the only phrase the repo evidences) and <Y> one of its two names, else no pair; <Y> must also be the market's own yes team (its non-empty `yes_sub_title`, case-folded), else TITLE_CONFLICT, never placed: addendum 237 item 2), and its two names are the pair only when every sibling's yes team is one of them (the market's `yes_sub_title`, compared ignoring case, the pair formed carrying the market's own spelling so place()'s side lookup holds: ARCHITECT 2026-11-03, addendum 238 item 2(b), "a refusal on letter case alone was never the intent") or the sibling names none (a draw leg, a blank name); any other sibling refuses the whole event under its own sentinel, `the title's two names do not agree with the event's markets` (TITLE_CONFLICT; counted among the ambiguous, its line names the title and the markets' names), never a placement. No "A vs B" title, or a title that does not name exactly two teams (one name empty, the same name twice in any case, or a name that itself carries " vs "/" vs. "/" v "/" v." in any case, as "A vs B vs C" and "A vs B VS C" do), is NO_PAIR. Never placed by one name. Every title today reads "<Team> wins", so on a live run the fallback reads nothing and such an event is refused NO_PAIR; kalshi-history's API reads carry the exchange's titles, and there the narrowing bites. With nothing placed and an unstarted game in the window the zero-match alarm prints. |
| `kalshi-history` | `--series S` (one per run) · `--since` / `--until YYYY-MM-DD` · `--dry-run` · `--max-calls N` (default 300) | KALSHI SETTLED-MARKET HISTORY (ARCHITECT 2026-10-14, addendum 67 item 1; #519). Reads the series' settled markets from the exchange's public endpoints (no key): the cutoff, then `/historical/markets` and `/markets?status=settled`, paged by cursor. Each event is placed by `kalshi_match.place` on the stored games of its days: the same fit, the same pins, the same refusals (two markets claiming one side of one game are refused across events too, and a market the run would write, placed on a side a stored row of the series holds under another ticker, is refused under the same reason, the stored row unmoved: on a clock series, on any series where a row placed by ruling, `placed by ruling:`, holds the side, and on any series where a placed stored row listed in the run holds it; a date-only series is otherwise placed as before, run by run, and a day-off twin across runs is the NCAA tiebreak's, addendum 130 item 3(b)). **A stored row that holds a placement is read by its stored match_id and side, and never placed or moved again** (ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 132 item 3(b)): only the run's new markets and the held rows it places are written placed; a refused stored row has no stored side and keeps its date claim across the events, as before (addendum 134 item 3, option (ii)), and is never written again; a placed stored row NOT placed by ruling claims both its stored side and the side its date names, and a row placed by ruling claims its stored side only (ARCHITECT 2026-10-19, addendum 137 item 1, option (c): "this head's refusals must be a superset of main's, the one designed exception being a ruled row"). The live sync's in-play, no-price, spread and window refusals are not read for a settled market. Each market's one-minute candles are read from six hours before kickoff to its close (the stored kickoff when matched, else the ticker's clock; with neither, no candles). It stores one row per market in `kalshi_settled_markets`: the last two-sided quote at or before kickoff (`last_ts`, `last_bid`, `last_ask`, `last_mid`) and `candles_6h`, `candles_two_sided`, `gaps`, `raw_first_candle`. A refused market is kept with match_id null and the reason in notes. **It writes that table and nothing else** (no odds snapshot, no grading close, no prediction), and nothing reads it as a close until a later ruling. Each candle shape is read by its own keys, and a null price is never filled. A stored ticker is skipped, so a run resumes by the table. At the cap the run stops cleanly and prints where. A list that does not reach its end (a failed page, a cursor that comes back) stores nothing. A series whose competition is not in the DB refuses before any call. A candle call refused for good (a 4xx other than 408/429) is stored with no quote; other failures are asked again by the next run. **A 429 (ARCHITECT 2026-10-17, addendum 104 item 2)** is asked again in the run, after 2 s and then 5 s, each retry a call against the cap; a third 429 counts the call failed. The receipt says `429 retries: N`. `--dry-run` makes every read and writes nothing. Needs `migrate_kalshi_settled_markets.py` (after the daily .backup) except under `--dry-run`: a run checks that migration's own marker (`kalshi_settled_markets_migration`), not the table, since any `init_db()` creates the mapped table. The operator's block runs it, after the architect's read. **THE IDENTITY HOLD (ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 127 item 3(c); it stands, addendum 130 item 2(c)):** on a clock series (a ticker that carries a clock: KXMLBGAME of the five; NFL, NHL, NCAA and soccer are unchanged), an event whose pair (its two names) has an earlier-dated event that is unsettled, or out of step on its own date, is not placed by date: its markets are kept with match_id and side null and the note `identity hold: earlier event <ticker> of the pair unsettled` / `... out of step`, and no candles are fetched for them. Out of step is the matcher's own rule (addendum 54 item 6), read by `kalshi_match.behind_out_of_step` itself with the clock before every game: no game of the pair's two teams on the event's date (US Eastern). Unsettled is listed open by the exchange: a run that lists a clock ticker also lists the series' open markets (`/markets?status=open`, the live sync's call), for the hold only, never placed or stored; the receipt counts them on the listed line. An event closed but not yet settled is in no list read, so it holds nothing and a later event of its pair is placed by date (ruled, addendum 132 item 4(e): listing the closed markets for the hold is its own follow-up PR, #603). The earlier events are every event of the pair the run knows of: both settled lists whole, not only --since/--until, the stored rows and the open list; **an earlier event binds only when its ticker date is within 10 days before the event's date** (ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 132 item 3(c): 10 days binds, 11 does not), so a regular-season rainout holds nothing a month on. As for the live rule's "behind" (addendum 55 item 3), a market the matcher refuses for a reason of its own keeps that reason. **The one exception to "inserted once":** a stored held row is read again by every run that lists it in range and placed in place when the hold has cleared and the matcher places it on a side no stored row holds (its candles fetched then, on the stored kickoff; `hold cleared <time>: placed by the ingest` appended to its notes); the write reads the row and its side again, as place-held does; held again, or cleared but refused by the matcher (a stored row holding its side included, named on the receipt), it is left as stored. A row with a match_id, and a row refused for any other reason, is never read again: nothing already placed is moved by the ingest. The receipt prints an `identity hold` line (new rows held, stored held rows held again, stored held rows placed) and one `⏸` line per held event naming the earlier event and the matcher's words. **The new-row write re-check (#599 item 1, ARCHITECT 2026-10-19, addendum 136 item 3):** a new placed row reads its side again in the write's own transaction, in the plan's scope (a clock ticker, or a side a row placed by ruling holds); a `place-held` that committed after the run read the table keeps the side, and the new row is written unplaced, refused by the matcher (`two claims`, the side and its holder in the note; no quote), named on the receipt as `✗ <ticker>: match N SIDE held by T at the write: written unplaced` (a ruling can place it). **Two stored rows of one pair on a date-only ticker** (addendum 136 item 3; ARCHITECT 2026-10-19, addendum 140 item 2, option (b)): "dedupe-matches has to merge them" is printed ONLY where rows share a kickoff minute, on every ticker form. Rows at different minutes suggest no merge: where the ticker carries the game number (G1, G2), the line reads "two games of the pair that day; the G1/G2 suffix is not read (#478)"; on a date-only ticker with none (a college placeholder beside the real kickoff, or two games), it reads "two games or a placeholder beside the real kickoff (<HH:MM> and <HH:MM> ET): dedupe-matches's dry run says which; not merged from here", the rows' kickoff minutes in US Eastern. Where both hold (two rows at one minute and a third at another), the line names each. The refusal and its reason do not change. |
| `kalshi-history place-held` | `--ticker TICKER` · `--match-id N` · `--ruling "<addendum>"` · `--backup PATH` (required unless `--dry-run`) · `--dry-run` | **THE RULED RE-PLACE (ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 130 item 2(c)):** "sets match_id and side on a row that has match_id null and the hold/refusal note, appends the ruling to its note, and fetches its candles then — a reviewed command, never a guess by the ingest." A subcommand of `kalshi-history`: the pull's own invocation (`kalshi-history --series S ...`) is unchanged, and the pull's options are refused in front of the subcommand. Refuses (exit 2, nothing written) unless the ruling is not empty, the row exists with match_id null and an identity hold or a `refused by the matcher:` note, the match exists in the series' own competition, its two teams are the event's two names one each (`kalshi_match.fit`, from the event's stored rows), the side can be told (the market's yes team against the match's home and away; a Tie leg is DRAW), and no other row of the series holds that side of that match. Then the backup check (`--backup PATH`: the dedupe-matches gate, its `kalshi_settled_markets` count equal to the live DB's), then the candles on the match's stored kickoff by the ingest's own call (a 429 asked again as the ingest asks it). **A candle call that fails for now (a 5xx, a 408, a transport error, a third 429, an answer with no list) writes nothing and exits 2** (ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 132 item 3(a): "the quote lands with the placement or the placement does not land"): one `REFUSED: the candle call <path> failed (<why>; 429 retries: N): nothing is written ...; run it again` line, dry or not; the operator re-runs. A candle call the exchange refuses for good (a 4xx other than 408/429, as the ingest reads it), or a window that cannot be read, leaves the placement with a null quote and says so in notes and on the console. Notes: the stored notes, then `placed by ruling: <ruling>`, then what the candles said; fetched_at becomes the placement's time. Prints the row before and after. Writes that one row and nothing else. `--dry-run` makes every read (the candle call included) and writes nothing. The first uses, by the ruling: KXMLBGAME-26OCT161800TBCLE-TB and -CLE → 50550; after its settlement, KXMLBGAME-26OCT172000TBCLE-TB / -CLE → 50548. **The candle endpoint by the cutoff (#599 item 2, ARCHITECT 2026-10-19, addendum 136 item 3):** the stored `source` is the list the market came from when it was fetched, and the cutoff moves on; place-held reads `/historical/cutoff` first (one call, with the two 429 retries; the call cap is 6) and asks the candles where the market is served now: `historical` when its `settled_time` is before the cutoff, else `live` (a null `settled_time` keeps the stored source); the source written is the one used, and the console prints the cutoff, the settled time and the source. A cutoff that cannot be read writes nothing and exits 2 (`REFUSED: the cutoff (historical/cutoff) could not be read ...; run it again`). **The event (#599 item 3):** the row's event is `event_ticker`, or the ticker's own event when the column is null, as the history path reads it; the event's names come from the series' stored rows of that event read the same way. |
| `kalshi-history place-chain` | `--anchor EVENT` · `--ruling "<the anchor's ruling>"` · `--backup PATH` (required unless `--dry-run`) · `--dry-run`; or `--list --series S` (read-only) | **THE ANCHORED CHAIN (ARCHITECT 2026-10-20, addendum 148 item 2, on the design of #630):** the ruling's change to the design, verbatim: "a shift-capable check IS wanted, because the shifted chain is the case the rule exists for (E17 on 50548 is the ALCS's own example, and every rainout-then-doubleheader pattern is a shift). A member PLACES on the count's game when EITHER check passes, and a result mismatch refuses under both: (a) the clock check as designed: \|kickoff − ticker clock\| ≤ MAX_TICK_DRIFT and no other unused game within 2h; or (b) the settlement bracket: the row's settled_time is after the count's game's kickoff and before the kickoff of the pair's next game in the list (or no next game within N1's window), the target is FINISHED with a stored winner, and the leg's kalshi_result agrees with that winner. The bracket is the shifted event's own evidence that it settled on that game and no other. A member that passes neither stays held and the chain STOPs (Q3). The dry run prints which check placed each row ("by clock 0.1h" or "by settlement +2.9h, before <next id> <ET>")." A subcommand of `kalshi-history` beside `place-held`, which is unchanged. **The anchor** A is an event out of step on its own date (the identity hold's own read) whose every row is placed by ruling on one match M (`place-held`, both legs), each carrying `placed by ruling: <--ruling>`. **The chain** is the pair's events after A in the hold's chain order (ticker date, then event ticker; a twin on A's date is next), from the table only (no exchange call), within N1 (1-10 days after A), up to the first that ENDs it: IN STEP (a row placed by the ingest: a match_id and no `placed by ruling:`), refused, mixed legs, held behind another event, or held naming A `unsettled`. A MEMBER has every row held naming A `out of step` (N2). **The count:** the list is the pair's games (the games A's two names fit one each, `kalshi_match.fit`, as place-held fits its match; rows that are games, any status) from A's ticker clock through A's date + 11 days; fitted games of more than one pair of teams, M with them, refuse (a second team row of one club: `merge-teams` first; a namesake club's game, whose team names differ: rule the events by `place-held`), as the matcher refuses `best fit shared by two pairs of teams`; M is used by A and a game a stored row outside the chain holds is used by its holder; each chain event in order takes the first unused game; a ruled event is counted on its own match, and the chain stops where that is not the count's game. A member is placed on the count's game when each row passes (a) or (b), the game SCHEDULED or FINISHED (LIVE stays held; (b) needs FINISHED with a stored winner: `full_time_result` and the scores, none when they disagree); one that passes neither stays held and the chain STOPs. Two games of the list at one kickoff minute refuse (dedupe first), and so do two rows the chain would place on one side of one game (an event with a third leg naming one of its two names). **The write** is place-held's, per row: its lines, the cutoff read once for the chain, the candles read then on the game's kickoff, notes `<stored notes>; placed by ruling: <the anchor's ruling>; placed by the chain of <A> [k]: <by clock N.Nh \| by settlement +N.Nh, before <next id> <ET> \| by settlement +N.Nh, no next game of the pair through <date>>; <the candles' note>` (the ruling's own example forms, one decimal) (the 2000-character cap); a candle call that fails for now on any row writes nothing and exits 2 (all or nothing); one transaction, each row read again as a held row and its side read again. Refusals exit 2 with nothing written; the backup check runs once, only when there is a row to write. `--dry-run` makes every read (the cutoff once and the candle call for each row it would place, as place-held's dry run makes its candle call), never runs the backup check, and writes nothing. A chain with no member prints its counted events and exits 0. The console names each row and its verdict (`PLACE <SIDE> by clock 0.0h`, `PLACE <SIDE> by settlement +3.6h, before 50551 <ET>`, `stays held: ...; the chain stops`, `counted: ruled on <id>; the count's game agrees`, `END: ...`), then `held rows naming <A> outside the chain` and the closing count. **`--list --series S`** (Q12): read-only from the table and the stored games, no network call, no `--ruling`, nothing written: each held row by the event its note names, that event's state (refused, ruled with its ruling, placed by the ingest, held, mixed legs, not stored) and clock, and the pair's games on the held event's day with kickoff, status, gap and the rows holding each. Anchors are ruled from it. |
| `sync-lineups-history` | `--competition CL\|UEL\|EFL\|FAC` · `--season 2025/26` (or `2025`, read as `2025/26`) · `--dry-run` · `--max-calls N` (default 100) · `--save DIR` · `--since YYYY-MM-DD` | CUP LINEUP HISTORY (ARCHITECT 2026-10-15, addendum 74 item 1; addendum 71's Step B; #106). For the FINISHED fixtures of one cup-season, the starting eleven and bench per side from `/fixtures/lineups` (the adapter's `list_lineup_confirmed`, its own pace and parser), stored in `lineups` as kind `history`: never `confirmed`, which the live lineup factor reads across competitions. A side maps to a stored team by its provider id (merged ids included), never by name. Idempotent: a fixture whose two teams each hold eleven history starters is skipped with no call; any other is asked, and the history rows of each side in the answer are replaced (other kinds are never touched). The receipt: fixtures with both elevens / fixtures, then one / none (by why) / failed / not asked / no fixture id, rows stored, calls and HTTP requests by status. **A nameless answer (ARCHITECT 2026-10-16, addendum 98 item 1):** a later answer whose block for a side has no named slot never replaces an eleven stored for it; the receipt counts such sides ("answered nameless, earlier eleven kept: N") with their fixture ids, and the fixture's completeness reads from the stored rows. Rows carry the ingest time only, each fixture's rows the time they were stored (no publication time). At the cap the run stops cleanly between fixtures and prints where; a payload error (quota or plan) or a 401/403/429 stops it; other failed calls are listed and asked again next run. `--dry-run` reads the DB mode=ro, prints the plan before any call, makes the reads up to the cap and writes nothing. `--save` keeps each answer whole as `<fixture id>.json`, and an HTTP error answer's decoded body as `<fixture id>.http<status>.json` (#565), never over a kept answer (never under `data/`). Refuses before any call: another competition, one not in the DB, a season with no finished fixture (the stored seasons listed), UEL rows stored under EL. The pulls are the operator's blocks, after the architect's read of a dry run. **`--since` (ARCHITECT 2026-10-16, addendum 83 item 5(b)):** only the fixtures whose stored kickoff's UTC date is on or after the date are planned and asked (one with no stored kickoff is left out); the receipt counts the left-out fixtures, keeps them out of the coverage line, and lists every one (match id, fixture id, date, teams). **The run list (addendum 83 item 5(a)):** CL 2026/27, EFL 2026/27, CL 2025/26, UEL 2025/26, EFL 2025/26, FAC 2025/26 (FAC `--since 2025-11-01`, the First Round Proper). |
| `observe-cup-lineups` | `--competition CL\|UEL\|EFL\|FAC` (repeatable; default all four) · `--loop` · `--dry-run` | CUPS STEP B, THE LIVE T-15 OBSERVATION STORE (ARCHITECT 2026-10-23, addendum 158 item 3(a); #106). It reads the stored cup fixtures kicking off within 3 hours, excluding cancelled, postponed and stale_orphan. Three polls per fixture and stored kickoff, each asked once when due: T-15 (the label poll, addendum 155 item 3(a)), and T-5 and T+10 (later reads that catch a correction). A fixture a schedule sync re-dates starts a fresh poll set; the old kickoff's rows are kept as they were. Each poll uses the adapter's `list_lineup_confirmed` and is APPENDED to `cup_lineup_observations`: scheduled and observed time (our clock), the team-mapped starters and bench per side, and the status. Statuses: `complete` (eleven mapped starters per side), `partial` (fewer, or a side mapped to neither team, never matched by name), `empty`, `failed` (the error kept) and `missed` (more than 30 minutes past its time; no call). A call more than 5 minutes late is flagged `late`. The first complete observation of a kickoff is marked and kept as it was; a later complete row says whether its elevens differ from that first. Nothing updates or deletes a row, and nothing writes `lineups` or any candidate input or forecast (the T-35 inputs live apart). It writes only after `migrate_cup_lineup_observations.py` (table and marker), and refuses a missing DB file without creating it. `--dry-run` reads mode=ro and prints the due polls with no call and no write. A real tick holds the chains' DB lock (`sp_common.sync_lock`, as sync-teams and sync-matches do) across the due-check, the calls and the inserts, waiting with one line if it is held. The API key is checked before any row is written, so a refusal writes nothing. A poll another run stored first is skipped and counted as already observed. `--loop` ticks every 30 s until no poll of the window remains; without it, one tick (a timer's run). **On the host (ARCHITECT 2026-10-27, addendum 169 item 4(i)):** `sp-cup-observe.timer` runs one tick (all four cups, no `--loop`) every five minutes, hours 00-03 and 06-23 UTC; a tick with no poll due reads mode=ro, refuses an unmigrated store as a real tick does, and ends without the DB lock. |
| `cup-exam` | `--key PATH` · `--detail` · `--cup-coeffs tuned\|base` · `--with-history-xi` · `--db PATH` | **The cup acceptance exam (spec frozen 2026-09-25; cup_exam's gate unchanged): report-only, nothing written.** It prices the answer key's finished fixtures with production soccer pricing (fix-v2's tuned cup coefficients by default) and scores them against the books' fair: mean |Δ_HOME| <= 8.0pp, <= 13 rows over 8pp, EFL round-2 favourite agreement >= 80%; INVALID on > 2 key rows missing or < 45 scored. `--with-history-xi` (**THE PERFECT-XI PRICE**, ARCHITECT 2026-11-05, addendum 256 item 3; 2026-11-11, addendum 343 items 4-5) prices the exam set again with each fixture's stored `history` XI (#538; post hoc, a bound, never an input) through the existing XI/lineup multipliers, as `confirmed` (0.7, the figure OF RECORD) and as it stands (the confidence lookup's fallback, 0.3, information only), and prints after the exam as today: the four exam figures of each reading, the history-XI coverage (a fixture side with none prices as today, counted), the history sides of other than eleven starters (not the actual XI: priced as today, counted), the history-XI sides whose multipliers do nothing (no player-season stats: no baseline, or no starter of the XI resolved), the figures on the subset with at least one side's stats (information, not a second verdict), the per-fixture deltas and the runtime; it judges nothing (the threshold and the declaration-or-close are the architect's, the 11-21 step). It requires `--db`: a .backup copy outside data/, opened mode=ro + immutable=1 for every session of the run (addendum 198 item 6(ii); never created, never the configured database). Exit 2 on a refusal. src/walters/cup_exam.py, src/walters/cup_perfect_xi.py. |
| `kalshi-replay` | `--from-file PATH` · `--series S` (repeatable) | READ-ONLY (ARCHITECT 2026-10-12, addendum 50 6(b)): fetches each series' open markets once (MLB, NFL, NHL, NCAA from `venue.KALSHI_SERIES_BY_COMPETITION`, and each soccer series that resolves; CL's `KXUCLGAME` since 2026-10-13, addendum 58), each stamped after its own fetch, and keeps them in `logs/kalshi_replay_<stamp>.json` (public market data). It places them with `kalshi_match.place` on the stored games, loaded as the syncs load them, through a mode=ro session; it stores nothing. Per series it prints what is placed and what is refused, by reason, with the module's lines, beside main's newest stored legs at or before that fetch (their age printed, flagged over 3h), then every leg that differs: on another game or side, stored by main and refused now, placed now and not stored by main. `--from-file` replays a kept file; `--series` narrows. The operator runs it in the morning block, straight after the day's Kalshi syncs. |
| `closing-run` (= `python deploy/hosting/closing.py run`) | `--family MLB\|NFL\|SOCCER` · `--start ISO` · `--dry-run` | **CLOSING RUN FOR ONE MODEL FAMILY (ARCHITECT 2026-10-09, addendum 21 item 3 and addendum 22, on #370's frame).** Families (C1): MLB, NFL, SOCCER (PL); a shadow or market-only league has none. **Refusals first, each receipted, exit 2:** a backup folder under `data/` (before anything else); `SP_SKIP_FAMILIES` naming the family (MLB: laptop only); `SP_EXPORTS_MIRROR_REMOTE` not set; `SP_EXPORTS_MIRROR_ROLE` not set or neither `host` nor `laptop` (addendum 45 P1: no default); `NTFY_CARD_TOPIC` not set or with whitespace (C6; its value is never printed); a malformed `--start`; nothing to close (default target: the next unstarted start within 90 minutes). A watch-started refusal is receipted and notified once per start time and reason (reading 7). **Then under the chain lock** (a run that finds it held waits; a watch-started run waits until T-5 at most, then records a miss). Once it holds the lock it reads the clock and its receipts again (380.4): a watch-started run whose start time by then has a success or three attempts ends as `superseded` (no step, push or page; neither an attempt nor a refusal); one that gets the lock inside T-5 is a miss (`kind: closing_miss`, notified). The attempt number and the run's start (for the price and starter checks) are taken under the lock. **The backup (380.3):** a `.backup` dated today (America/New_York) in `SP_BACKUP_DIR` (else `~/backups`); when none, one is taken: the copy (`.backup` API), integrity check, open, sha256 and the `.sha256` file must all succeed, or the run fails as `backup` before the first step and what it left is renamed aside (`.failed-<stamp>`), never deleted. **The chain (C3)** covers every game of the family starting within 10 minutes after the start time: MLB `mlb-closing` (#370's ten steps, the export with `--date <NY date> --desk`; its schedule read `sync-matches ... --match-ids <the covered games>`); NFL `nfl-closing` and SOCCER `soccer-closing`, derived from `freshen:NFL` / `freshen:SOCCER`, opening with the schedule read, `sync-injuries --match-ids <the covered games> --strict` (NFL: a failed or empty roster read is a failed read, A2), the book sync with `--match-ids <the covered games>`, the Kalshi sync, the model step and the export with `--desk`. **The schedule read (addendum 23 A1, as amended by addendum 25 2(i)):** every family reads `sync-matches` by date with `--match-ids` naming the covered games (exit 1 unless the provider's answer held every one); NFL and SOCCER one UTC day per call, each naming that day's covered games. A covered game the read moved off the run's start time is recorded as soon as the read has run, so an attempt that fails at a later step does not count it as covered (Codex 4232307577). It stops at the first failed step. **The checks (C4), when the steps have finished:** one started test (a started target fails the run, `target started`, never retried); a covered game the schedule read moved off its start time leaves the run (receipted with both times; it is a new start time for the watch), and a run left with no game fails as `start moved` (C); every unstarted covered game must have a Desk row (`missing from export`); every price the export shows for a covered game was captured at or after the run's start (`stale prices`; Kalshi with more than one leg: the legs the Desk read for the row, A5; a stale leg it did not read is listed in the receipt, `stale_legs_not_read`); MLB: every starter the export lists was refreshed at or after the run's start (`match_participants.refreshed_at`; `stale starters`, a failed attempt). The export step writes `logs/<run id>.<slate file name>` (`--out {export_out}`, addendum 40 item 2), never `exports/`, so those failures leave no call behind. **On success (B1, addendum 23):** the summary (the run's own games only), then ONE page (C5) as soon as the checks pass: on the phone through the ntfy card topic, tried three times ten seconds apart (B2; not accepted: the run fails as `page`, nothing pushed, the watch retries), then the laptop's screen (not posted: recorded, changes nothing). **ONE CLOSING, ONE PAGE, ONE FILE (ARCHITECT 2026-10-11, addendum 40 item 2):** once the phone has accepted the page and before the push, the run writes `exports/closing_<FAMILY>_<start>.json` (`<start>` the run's start in UTC, `2026-10-11T1700Z`): the games on its page and no other (its Desk rows, the PASS games included), each row as the export gave it, `count` the rows kept, and a `closing` block {family, start, run_id, match_ids, called_off}; everything else is the export's own. A run whose page did not go out writes none. A failed write is receipted (`closing_file_failed`) and notified, and the closing is not run again; **THE FILE OF A SECOND PAGE (ARCHITECT 2026-10-11, addendum 41 item 3):** where a start time is paged a second time (a manual rerun), the later page is its close: its file replaces the earlier one, the earlier is kept as `logs/<its run id>.<name>` (a hard link made before the rename), and the later receipt names it (`closing_file.replaced`: file, sha256, run_id, kept_as); an earlier file that cannot be moved (its kept name taken, no run id) refuses the write (`closing_file_failed`) and stands. A watch-started run is still superseded by a successful closing. A closing run never writes, replaces or moves the family's slate file; the slate chains write it. The receipt records both files and their sha256 (`export_written`, `closing_file`) and the page's time (`page.at`). Then the exports mirror push (`--role` from `SP_EXPORTS_MIRROR_ROLE`, `--label closing`); a failed push after the page is receipted (`push_failed`) and notified, and the closing is not run again (the next push from this machine carries the file). Page lines name their game (B4); a game whose last call was PLAY or LADDER and is now PASS has its own line, `<game> · PASS · was <call>`, and makes the page high priority (B3); **the last call** (reading 7 as amended, addendum 25 2(iii)): "The last call of a game is the call the operator was last shown for it: its call on the last closing page that covered it or, when no page has covered it, its call in the newest export that no closing run wrote. An export a closing run wrote never supplies the last call of a game that run did not page. C5's was and B3 read this." Each closing receipt records the export it wrote (`export_written`: file, sha256) and the last calls it found before its first step (`last_calls`, every game of the family found, start within the last 24 hours or later); a run whose newest file is a closing run's export takes that run's paged calls and, for every other game, that receipt's last calls; a file no receipt names keeps its own calls; an NFL PLAY beside injured players whose position did not resolve says `<n> injured, position unresolved` (B7); the 2026-10-07 hold applies to MLB and NFL PLAYs (B5). **Addendum 40 item 4:** an NFL PLAY beside an empty injury list says `injury list empty: <team>` on its line, after B7's count; THE HOLD OF 2026-10-11 (NFL only, a second `CLOSING_HOLDS` row): an NFL PLAY the Desk tagged `QB-flagged half units` or `QB unread half units` says `QB: paste to the architect before placing` on its line (the Desk's call and the file do not change; the hold stands until a quarterback term passes its gate, #427). One receipt line (`kind: closing`) either way. A watch-started run that does not succeed posts a notice on both channels (best effort): `Closing NFL 2026-10-11 13:00 ET: FAILED: stale prices · attempt 1/3`. It places nothing and never opens Kalshi's trading API (a closing reads the day's unit file only; the slate's `desk-unit` read is the autopilot's one trading-API read, GET only, addendum 179 item 3). **Unread unit (addendum 179 item 4):** a PLAY line whose order the export left unwritten for an unread day says `unit unread — no order` in place of the order, verbatim (never `no order: …`); `depth_note` (item 7), where an order carries one, follows the order on the page and in the summary. `--dry-run` prints the plan (games covered, resolved steps, backup, export) and touches nothing. **The MLB books line (ARCHITECT 2026-10-16, addendum 97 item 2(a)):** an MLB closing file's closing block holds `books`, one entry a row: `{match_id, n, why}`. `n` is the row's `market.bookmaker_count`, and `why` is null when n > 0. When n is 0, `why` is the sync-odds step's own answer from its `NO-BOOK-ODDS` line (e.g. `provider listed the game, no odds`), else the line's run-level answer, else `the sync step named no answer for the game`. The step's receipt records the answers (`no_book_odds`), and the rows stay as the export gave them. |
| `closing-watch` (= `python deploy/hosting/closing.py watch`) | `--family F` (repeatable) · `--dry-run` | **The tick, every minute, for the three families** (launchd: `bash scripts/setup_closing_watch.sh`). No network to decide. Per family, from the stored schedule and the receipts: the unstarted games no closing has finished with (a success or three attempts), grouped from the earliest: a start time covers the games within the 10 minutes after it, and the next start time is the first game after those. A start time 35 to 5 minutes away starts `closing-run` for it, earliest first (MLB: after checking the MLB feed answers; unreachable: `MLB feed unreachable: VPN on, Tailscale off`, one miss per first pitch, receipted and notified once; the watch keeps trying silently, B6). A family `SP_SKIP_FAMILIES` names is still planned: its due start time is refused, receipted and notified once per start time and reason. At most three attempts per start time; none inside T-5 (a start time first seen inside T-5 is a miss, notified once). Otherwise it exits 0 and prints nothing. **Exit (addendum 45 P3):** 0 success or nothing due; 2 a refusal; 3 a failed run or a miss, already receipted and notified; 1 what it could not report itself. Every page and notice it sends ends with `machine: host` or `machine: laptop`. A failure, miss or refusal neither the screen nor the card topic took goes once through the operator topic (`NTFY_TOPIC`), and its receipt names the channel that took it (addendum 47 item 7(a)). `--dry-run` lists every family's games, start times, receipt state and what would start, then each family's slate plan. **THE SLATE STAGE (ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 126 item 2(a), #590):** after its closings, each tick runs a slate stage per family for the tick's US Eastern day: due at the day's first kickoff minus 150 minutes, or at the first tick after 06:00 ET when that is later (a tick at 05:59 does nothing; a day with no game does nothing). **The slate floor (ARCHITECT 2026-10-24, addendum 163 item 3):** the 06:00 ET floor means a kickoff before 08:30 ET gets its slate less than 150 minutes ahead (a 07:30 ET PL kickoff prints "due 06:00 ET", T-90); that is the floor, not a miss, and the closing stays at T-35. One run per family per day, decided from its own receipts (kind `slate`): a success, or three attempts, and no more that day; a refusal or a superseded run is no attempt. **The closings first** (item 2(a): "The closing runs at T-35 are unchanged"; one watch process runs one thing at a time): a slate does not start while a closing the watch will still run, for any family of the tick, has its T-35 within 15 minutes (`SLATE_GUARD_MIN`, more than twice the longest any chain has held the lock, addendum 45 P6) or already past with its start more than five minutes off (`closing_ahead`; a family this machine refuses is none), and its wait for the lock ends 15 minutes before that T-35; so held, it runs on a later tick, after the closing, and each closing it waits for is receipted once (kind `slate`, `held`: that closing; neither an attempt nor a refusal), so the day's newest hold names the closing it waits for now. **THE HELD PAGE** (addendum 133 item 4, item 2): once per family and day, when a slate is still held with no 15-minute window left before its family's first UNSTARTED kickoff, the hold pages, card and phone, `SLATE <FAMILY> <date> HELD for closing <FAMILY> HH:MM ET (T-35 HH:MM ET) — RUN BY HAND: block_<MMDD>_<family>_slate.txt` (receipted on the hold's line as `held_page`; the tick exits 3). The stage's pages go out after the tick's whole slate stage, each against a clock read again (#613 P1-1: a page sent mid-stage reserved only its own send), the send cut at `NOTIFY_MAX_S` = 57 s of wall clock (the screen 15 s, the card topic 20 s, the operator topic 20 s, plus 2 s; the channels' timeouts are per socket operation, so the send runs in a thread joined for the bound, and a send still running then is receipted `cut_at_bound_s`: #613 P1-2; RULED over a detached sender, addendum 138 item 3(a): "a page whose outcome is never receipted is a page we cannot audit"; a page cut at the bound may not reach the phone, and the receipt names it), and only where that bound ends before the first tick that could start the closing the watch runs next (`closing_ahead`, an MLB closing the feed did not answer for included: ARCHITECT 2026-10-19, addendum 138 item 3(c), "The invariant is that no page costs a closing its T-35"; the page waits while the feed is down): a page never costs a closing its T-35; else a later tick pages it. **At most ONE slate RUN per tick** (ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 134 item 1(ii), #606 item 1): "a slate that holds, misses or is superseded does not count; the next family's slate runs on the next tick, a minute later", so a tick is bounded as before plus one chain (the host unit's `TimeoutStartSec=45min` does not move); a slate due after the tick's one run is receipted once a day as a hold (`held: one slate run per tick: the <FAMILY> slate ran this tick (a later tick runs it)`, `deferred`: that family; no step, no attempt); **THE DEFERRAL PAGE** (ARCHITECT 2026-10-19, addendum 137 item 2(5)): a slate deferred with no 15-minute window left before its family's first unstarted kickoff pages, by the HELD page's mechanism and bound, `SLATE <FAMILY> <date> HELD for the <FAMILY2> slate (one slate run per tick) — RUN BY HAND: block_<MMDD>_<family>_slate.txt` (`held_page` on the deferral's line; once per family and day with the HELD page); and `--dry-run` marks it `would RUN on a later tick (one slate run per tick: <FAMILY>'s first)`, adding `; on this tick if the MLB feed does not answer` after an MLB slate that checks its feed first (unanswered, it misses, which is no run). The tick's one run goes to the family whose first unstarted kickoff of the day is soonest when the slate stage starts (read after the tick's closings, #613 P1-3); a family that has failed an attempt that day comes last (its retry never takes a tick from a family that has not run). RULED (ARCHITECT 2026-10-19, addendum 138 item 3(b), option (a): the one-run bound and SLATE_GUARD_MIN stand): "the slate runs on a later tick unless its kickoff comes first, and then its page says so"; a deferred slate whose kickoff comes before a later tick can run is not run, and its deferral page is the operator's alert. A closing that cannot start holds no slate (`slate_ahead`): while the MLB feed does not answer, an MLB closing is a miss on every tick, never a run, so when an MLB closing would hold a slate the watch reads the feed's answer and, unanswered, the guard reads the other families' closings only. An MLB slate checks its own feed before the guard: unanswered, its miss pages RUN BY HAND at once. But a closing due by the next tick holds a slate first, with no probe, where a probe could not release it or could cost that closing its T-35 (`_hold_unprobed`): a closing with no feed; an MLB closing the tick's feed answer says starts; or, with no answer, a closing with no feed due by the next tick too, or one probe (the 8 s timeout plus 2 s) that could reach the first tick that could start that closing (`next_tick`: this machine's first firing at or after its T-35, by role: the host's whole minutes, or the laptop's launchd firings, which keep their load phase; a firing before the T-35 cannot start it, so a probe that costs one costs the closing nothing). Otherwise an MLB closing with no answer gets the one probe, so with the feed down it holds nothing (a no-probe hold for it had cost a slate a kickoff). The slate stage probes the feed at most once a tick, and not at all when the tick's closings did (their answer is the tick's). `--dry-run` leaves out of a slate's guard the closings its own tick runs first (`after this tick's closings: …`); a closing after them whose T-35 could be inside the guard by the time they end (within the guard plus 15 minutes per planned closing) makes it `would RUN if they end before HH:MM ET, else held: …`; with no network, it shows an MLB closing ahead both ways (`held if the MLB feed answers: …; if it does not, …`), and a closing due by the next tick, on a tick with no closing of its own, as a plain hold where it holds with no probe (else both ways); after closings that leave no MLB feed answer, an MLB closing ahead adds `(held with no probe if they end within 10 s before a tick at or after HH:MM ET)` (its T-35). It takes the closing's chain lock (a slate and a closing never run at once; held through the full 5-minute wait: a miss, the watch keeps trying), takes today's backup if none is there (as a closing), reads the day's unit (`python cli.py desk-unit`, ARCHITECT 2026-10-30, addendum 179 item 3: the ET day's first slate reads the pool, a later one finds the day's file; not one of the chain's steps, its exit never fails the slate, receipted `unit` {state, et_date, new, exit}, no figure; an unread day's page adds `unit unread — no order` on the line after the counts), and runs the family's closing chain steps exactly as `closing-run` resolves them for the day's games not started (`sync-matches` for the day's ids, odds, injuries / pitchers as the family has them, Kalshi, predict, the export `--desk`), word for word. **STAGING (ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 133 item 3(a), the closing's doctrine: a failed run leaves no call behind):** the export step keeps the closing's own `--out logs/<run id>.<slate file name>` as `resolve_steps` gives it (SOCCER's export adds `--start`/`--end`, nothing else). The family's slate file in `exports/`: `nfl_predictions_<date>.json`; `mlb_MLB_<date>.json` (`--date <date>`, the closing's own); `soccer_PL_<start>_to_<end>.json`, the window its `--start`/`--end` price: a Saturday to Tuesday day takes the window soccer-prematch prices for its round, the Saturday on or before it to the Tuesday; a Wednesday to Friday day takes the midweek override's form, the day to three days on, a name soccer-prematch does not write by default, since run on a Wednesday to Friday it prices the coming Saturday to Tuesday. Then the kept file must be this run's, carry the Desk's calls and hold a row for every game of the day not started when the steps finished; then it is linked into `exports/<slate file>` the way the closing writes its closing file (a hard link, then the rename over the name; receipted `slate_file` {file, sha256}; the `logs/` copy stays: one file under two names); then the mirror push (label `slate`); then ONE page, card topic (three tries) and screen: `SLATE <FAMILY> <YYYY-MM-DD>: <N> row(s) · PLAY <p> · PASS <q> · exports/<file>` (`1 row` on a one-game day, addendum 133 item 3(c)); N and the calls count **the ET day's games** (ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 133 item 4, item 8: "rows whose match id is a game of the ET day"), any other call kind, e.g. a soccer `LADDER`, following as `· LADDER <k>`, rows with no desk call as `· no desk call <n>`, and the rows beyond the day (the NFL 36-hour window, the soccer round) as `· +<k> beyond the day` when k > 0, before the file; the receipt keeps the day's `rows` and `calls` and `beyond_day` (k). Then **`LATE <m> min: due HH:MM ET, first tick HH:MM ET, run HH:MM ET`** (item 3) whenever the run's start (`ran_at`, in whole seconds) is more than `LATE_MIN` (five minutes) after the due time, whatever held it (ARCHITECT 2026-10-19, addendum 137 item 2(4): "a one-tick deferral or hold is not late"): `<m>` counts from the due time to the run's start (how late the FILE is), and `first tick` is the earliest `queued_at` of the day's `slate` and `slate_miss` receipts (a hold's, a miss's and a refusal's included), which says whether the laptop slept; the receipt's `late_minutes` is `<m>`, its `late_seconds` the exact seconds. Then the machine's line. A failed step or check pages `SLATE <FAMILY> FAILED: <step command> — RUN BY HAND: block_<MMDD>_<family>_slate.txt · attempt k/3` (the third adds `· no further attempt will run today`; the machine's line last), as a closing failure does; the checks after the steps name the kept export (`missing from export (logs/<run id>.<name>): match …`, `logs/<run id>.<name> carries no desk calls`, `export not written (logs/<run id>.<name>)`), and a link that fails names the slate file (`exports/<file> not linked (<error>)`).
