A failed attempt at a step, a check or the link leaves nothing in `exports/` and nothing reaches the mirror; an earlier good file of the same name (Saturday's soccer window file, on Sunday) is never replaced by a half-synced one; the attempt's export stays under `logs/` with its run id. A page that fails after the push: the file stands (it passed) and the attempt is failed (the retry re-runs the steps and replaces it again). A push that fails comes after the link: the checked file stands in `exports/` and the attempt is failed (RULED, ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 134 item 1(i): "the checked file stands in exports/ (it passed; the next push carries it; the retry replaces it) — no restore, as built"). A second slate whose kept export path already exists (two watch processes entering in one UTC second share the run id) is refused before the backup and every step, receipted and notified, no attempt (#442, addendum 44 item 8: nothing a run kept is opened for writing a second time). `--dry-run` ends a slate's plan with `then, once its checks pass: logs/RUN.<name> linked into exports/<slate file> · then the mirror push (--label slate) · then the page`. Misses (kind `slate_miss`, receipted and notified once per family, day and reason, no attempt): the MLB feed unreachable (`SLATE MLB <date> MISSED: MLB feed unreachable: VPN on, Tailscale off — RUN BY HAND: block_… · the watch keeps trying`), the chain lock held through the wait, every game of the day started before a slate ran. A family `SP_SKIP_FAMILIES` names writes no slate and pages nothing; it is receipted once a day when the watch is run for it (bare, or with `--family` naming it). The host's unit runs `--family NFL --family SOCCER`, so no MLB slate receipt is written there: `coverage`'s `MLB SLATE · NO SLATE FILE — run block block_<MMDD>_mlb_slate.txt` line is the alert. The closing readers (`closing_state`, `covered_ids`, `last_calls`) read kind `closing` only; `last_calls` reads the slate file as the morning file it is. The closing runs at T-35 are unchanged. |
| `mlb-closing-run` / `mlb-closing-watch` | `--first-pitch ISO` · `--dry-run` | #370's names, kept: `closing-run --family MLB` / `closing-watch --family MLB`. |

### Closing runs (operator)

The laptop closes every live-model family by itself (ARCHITECT 2026-10-09, addendum 21 item 3). It places nothing.

- **Install (on the architect's word, after the PR is merged):** `bash scripts/setup_closing_watch.sh [--family F ...]`
  (addendum 45 P5: the families, repeatable; none = MLB, NFL and SOCCER, named in the plist all the same; after the
  flip the laptop's is `--family MLB`, h2-cutover-runbook.md section 1 step 7). One LaunchAgent,
  `com.sportspredictor.closingwatch`, runs `python cli.py closing-watch --family F ...` every minute, logs in
  `logs/closing_watch.log` / `.err`; it replaces #370's MLB-only `com.sportspredictor.mlbclosingwatch` if present.
  Settings are read where the job finds them, the checkout's `.env` (or host.env), never your shell: put
  `SP_EXPORTS_MIRROR_REMOTE`, `SP_EXPORTS_MIRROR_ROLE=laptop` and `NTFY_CARD_TOPIC` in `.env`. A setting set only in your shell refuses the install,
  naming it; no value goes into the plist and none is printed. The script runs the run's own refusal checks in the
  job's environment and refuses on any. It sends one test page through the card topic ("Closing watch": "Test: the
  closing watch is installed …") and refuses to install if ntfy does not accept it, then posts the screen test
  notification and says what you should have seen on both.
- **The watch needs:** the laptop awake; and, for MLB only, VPN on with Tailscale off (the MLB feed). NFL and
  SOCCER need neither mode (ARCHITECT 2026-10-09, addendum 23 D2).
- **Off switch:** `bash scripts/setup_closing_watch.sh --uninstall`.
- **Check first:** `python cli.py closing-watch --dry-run` and `python cli.py closing-run --family NFL --dry-run`.
- **The host:** NFL and SOCCER run the same command under a timer from the cutover on (C7). Addendum 45 ships
  `sp-closing-watch.service` / `.timer` (`closing.py watch --family NFL --family SOCCER`, every minute, H0-3's one
  exemption); install.sh enables nothing. The host enabled the timer at the cutover, h2-cutover-runbook.md section 1
  step 5b (2026-10-26 13:47Z), and it is on T11's `TIMERS` from then (ARCHITECT 2026-10-26, addendum 168 item 5; the
  `HELD_FOR_CUTOVER` list that held it until then is retired). MLB stays on the laptop to the season's last game.
- **The page (C5)**, by T-30 on a clean run, high priority when it carries a PLAY:

  ```
  NFL 13:00 ET · T-35m
  PLAY Buffalo 1u · BUY YES KXNFLGAME-26OCT11-BUFH @ 0.55 × 10 · exec +5.3pp · was PASS
  value shadow Tampa Visitors 0.25u · edge +10.0pp · exec +7.5pp · shadow, not staked
  quarantine shadow Newark 0.5u · edge +20.0pp · exec +18.2pp · shadow, not staked
  PASS: 3 games
  ```

  A PLAY (or LADDER) line: the call, the order line as the export prints it, the exec edge, the hold text
  (`paste to the architect before placing`, a PLAY under 4.0pp exec or unknown: `CLOSING_HOLDS` in
  `deploy/hosting/closing.py`) and `was <earlier call>` where the last export file called the game differently. No
  line exceeds 100 characters: a longer entry continues on an indented line. An MLB game with a side whose starter is
  not listed gets its own line (`… no starter listed for X · the model shrinks for it`).
  **The would-be line (ARCHITECT 2026-11-02, addendum 217 item 4):** while the registry does not read the NFL model
  certified (`registry.certification_of` on `nfl_predict.MODEL_VERSION`; an unread registry, a missing entry or a
  failed read is never judged certified and never blocks the page), an NFL row the Desk called
  PASS market-only whose record-only would-be call (`desk.market_only_hold.would_call`) is PLAY gets one line in the
  closing summary block (the console), under its game: `would-be PLAY (uncertified, unstaked) <pick> <units>u · exec
  <edge> · <QB-flagged | QB list unread | QB inputs clean> · record only, not placed`, and the receipt's Desk row
  carries it (`would_be`, only on such a row). The QB class reads the QB hold's own tags (`CLOSING_HOLDS`). It is NOT
  on the phone page's text (ARCHITECT 2026-11-02 18:05 ET, addendum 223 item 3: "the per-game line comes OFF THE PHONE
  PAGE's text; it lives in the summary block and the receipt only"): the NFL closing and slate pages carry no PLAY
  (#778's pin). The row stays a PASS in the count and never makes the page high priority.
- **Notices:** `Closing <FAMILY> <start ET>: FAILED: <what> · attempt n/3` (the third says no further attempt will
  run), `… REFUSED: <why>` (once per start time and reason), `… MISSED: <why>` (the lock came inside T-5; a start
  time first seen inside T-5), `MLB feed unreachable: VPN on, Tailscale off`.
- **Receipts:** `logs/receipts.jsonl` (`SP_RECEIPTS`): `kind: closing` (`family`, `start`, `covers`, `attempt`,
  `run_start`, `queued_at`, steps, export, Desk rows, push, `page`, `failed` / `refused` / `superseded`) and
  `kind: closing_miss`.

## Soccer weekly operation

Pre-matchday chain: `sync-matches` → `sync-odds` → `sync-injuries` →
`sync-kalshi-soccer` → `predict` → `export-predictions --start --end
--status scheduled`. Morning-after: `sync-matches` → `evaluate` →
`export-results --date`. After the matchweek closes: `soccer-refresh`
(gated). Full routine: `docs/pl_weekly_routine.md`.

| Command | Options | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `sync-kalshi-soccer` | `--competition --max-spread --series` | Kalshi three-way markets (Home/Away/Tie), refuse-safe matching, in-play guard. PL uses the mapped `KXEPLGAME`. **UNL** is PINNED to `KXUEFANLGAME` (ARCHITECT 2026-10-05: discovery refused on KXUEFANLGAME + KXCONCACAFNLGAME; "the competition is UEFA's"), the adapter default, so the window chain passes no `--series`. **CL** is PINNED to `KXUCLGAME`, wired since 2026-10-13 (ARCHITECT 2026-10-13, addendum 58: "wired as PL is: the soccer path, three legs, the same spread guard, the same placement"; CL stays market-only, with no model and no call); the host's window chain takes it with the tag that carries it, and no CL sync goes into the operator's block before the architect has read one replay console with CL in it (item 3). `KXCONCACAFNLGAME` is reserved for **CNL** ("for later"): `--competition CNL` refuses, naming it, until CNL is wired. **soccer-expansion-v1 (ARCHITECT 2026-10-07, addendum 2):** PD `KXLALIGAGAME`, SA `KXSERIEAGAME`, BL1 `KXBUNDESLIGAGAME`, FL1 `KXLIGUE1GAME`, ELC `KXEFLCHAMPIONSHIPGAME` are pinned and synced on `soccer-prematch` as CAPTURE ONLY (not in `WINDOW_KALSHI`; "a pinned series never makes a league live"), and from ARCHITECT 2026-10-24, addendum 164 item 2(a), on the host's hourly window chain too, as a capture-only step (`chains.WINDOW_KALSHI_CAPTURE_ONLY`: one per league, near and imminent tiers only; no export, no call); EL1 `KXEFLL1GAME`, EFL `KXEFLCUPGAME`, CZE `KXCZEFLGAME` are recorded, not wired, and refused. Series discovery (`/series` listing, game-winner series only, exactly one or a refusal listing the candidates) remains for a future unpinned competition. `--series` still overrides. In the window chain for UNL and CL games. **Where a market is placed (the switch, addendum 50 6(b)):** `kalshi_match.place`: the event's two names, from its own markets, fit a game of the series' own competition one each, on the ticker's day (US Eastern); on a date-only series the own date before a day either side; the Tie leg is placed as DRAW on the event's game and a leg wider than `--max-spread` is refused; the run's window is read last, on the one game chosen; every refusal is printed by its reason. |
| `soccer-refresh` | `--max-drift` | Weekly Elo/context retrain behind sanity gates (drift + spread-compression guards). |
| `soccer-odds-history` | `--competition --season --csv --url` | Ingest historical closing 1X2 odds (football-data.co.uk). |
| `predict-worldcup` | `--season --competition --out` | Market-derived tournament sheet — not the club model. |

## NFL operation (live Week 3 2026-09-22 to 2026-11-02; MARKET-ONLY since 2026-11-02, addendum 214)

Weekly rhythm: `sync-odds-football` + `sync-kalshi-nfl` every day or two as
lines post; after game days `sync-matches --competition NFL --season 2026` then
`nfl-grade`. nfl_elo_v1 is UNCERTIFIED (ARCHITECT 2026-11-02, addendum 214:
it fails its frozen gate on the complete warm-up) and runs as a labelled
shadow: `predict-nfl` → `export-nfl-predictions` before each slate as before,
but the Desk calls every NFL row PASS, units 0, reason "nfl_elo_v1 uncertified
(addendum 214): market-only until the confirmation read"
(`desk_policy.NFL_MARKET_ONLY_RULE`); the quarantine and value shadows go on
unstaked, and the chain's own call rides in `desk.market_only_hold`, for the
record only. It returns live only by the architect's ruling on the forward
confirmation (addendum 214 item 3).

> **DOCTRINE (architect, 2026-09-29): the game-day T-60 closing freshen is
> MANDATORY for model sports (NFL, MLB, soccer) on the laptop while it is
> writer of record.** Run the family's freshen sequence (the same steps as
> `freshen:<family>` in `deploy/hosting/chains.py`: injuries/odds/Kalshi →
> predict → export) about 60 minutes before each game-day slate. Earned by
> Week 4 MNF: the provider's injury report did not list the Chicago starter
> at either pre-game sync, and no closing freshen ran. The host's
> window-service freshen is NOT a substitute: its T-90 check only sees
> injury changes some other chain already synced, and the line-move alarm
> only fires on a >= 6pp market move.

| Command | Options | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `sync-odds-football` | `--match-ids 1,2` | `--match-ids` (2026-10-09, addendum 21 item 3 C3) prices only those stored games: the closing run's; default unchanged (every upcoming game in 8 days). Per-game book odds for upcoming American-football games, NFL and NCAA (moneyline, spreads with sign, totals with lines). Old name `sync-odds-nfl` still works as an alias. Every odds row and every book consensus snapshot it appends stores `source_updated_at`, the provider's `response[].update` (ARCHITECT 2026-10-09, addendum 32 item 4; a snapshot built from rows that differ takes the oldest, and any unknown row makes it unknown). Written only once `migrate_odds_source_updated_at.py` has added the columns (unmapped, guarded Core SQL, addendum 35 item 2): before it, the sync writes its rows and snapshots and the field reads null everywhere; run order: any (backup first, by hand). **By team (ARCHITECT 2026-11-08, addendum 295 item 5(b), #885; addendum 297 C):** `external_ids["provider_orientation"]` records what the provider's current listing of the row's id says about it: absent = same, `reversed`, `mismatch` (written by the sync-matches upsert and by `refresh-by-id`). On a `reversed` row the provider's HOME selection is stored as AWAY and the mirror (a spread's line travels with its price; draw and totals as served), and the 1X2 snapshot follows. A `mismatch` row is REFUSED: not fetched, no odds row or snapshot written, its stored prices as they were. The command prints `orientation_swapped N: <ids>` and then `orientation_mismatch_unpriced N: <ids>`, each on its own line before its summary (the summary stays the step receipt's key line), zeros included; a chain's receipt and summary line carry both counts (`sp_run`, addendum 297 C(iii)). |
| `sync-kalshi-nfl` | | Kalshi `KXNFLGAME` markets via the shared two-sided matcher. **Where a market is placed (the switch, addendum 50 6(b)):** `kalshi_match.place`: the event's two names, from its own markets, fit a game of the series' own competition one each, on the ticker's day (US Eastern); on a date-only series the own date before a day either side; the run's window is read last, on the one game chosen; every refusal is printed by its reason. **The pair (#413, ARCHITECT 2026-11-03, addendum 230 item 0(a)):** the event's two names come from its sibling markets (each market's `yes_sub_title`), as on the soccer path since 09-13; the yes-team fallback is gone (`KalshiAdapter.yes_team` removed). **The "A vs B" title fallback, NARROWED (ARCHITECT 2026-11-03 13:05 ET, addendum 234 item 2: "NARROWED, and it never overrides"):** two sibling names are the pair and the title is never read; three or more sibling names are refused `the event's markets do not name two teams` (NO_PAIR) and the title is never read (the override of conflicting siblings is removed); only where the siblings name fewer than two teams (one market listed, a market with no name) is the event's first priced market's title read (`kalshi_match._pair`, `_title_teams`: the "A vs B ..." form by `KalshiAdapter.title_teams`, unchanged; the question form kalshi-history's API reads carry, "Will <Y> win the <A> vs <B> <LEAGUE> game?", parsed strictly and case-insensitively, LEAGUE one of the closed `QUESTION_LEAGUES` ("Pro Football", the only phrase the repo evidences) and <Y> one of its two names, else no pair; <Y> must also be the market's own yes team (its non-empty `yes_sub_title`, case-folded), else TITLE_CONFLICT, never placed: addendum 237 item 2), and its two names are the pair only when every sibling's yes team is one of them (the market's `yes_sub_title`, compared ignoring case, the pair formed carrying the market's own spelling so place()'s side lookup holds: ARCHITECT 2026-11-03, addendum 238 item 2(b), "a refusal on letter case alone was never the intent") or the sibling names none (a draw leg, a blank name); any other sibling refuses the whole event under its own sentinel, `the title's two names do not agree with the event's markets` (TITLE_CONFLICT; counted among the ambiguous, its line names the title and the markets' names), never a placement. No "A vs B" title, or a title that does not name exactly two teams (one name empty, the same name twice in any case, or a name that itself carries " vs "/" vs. "/" v "/" v." in any case, as "A vs B vs C" and "A vs B VS C" do), is NO_PAIR. Never placed by one name. Every title today reads "<Team> wins", so on a live run the fallback reads nothing and such an event is refused NO_PAIR; kalshi-history's API reads carry the exchange's titles, and there the narrowing bites. With nothing placed and an unstarted game in the window the zero-match alarm prints. |
| `capture-weather-nfl` | | Tracking-only weather snapshot for upcoming NFL games (team-keyed stadium map; roofed games stored as indoor). |
| `predict-nfl` | | Write v1 Elo predictions for upcoming games (match-only upsert). **The rehearsal arm (ARCHITECT 2026-11-03, addendum 230 item 1(a), as ruled by addendum 247 items 2-3; docs/specs/nfl-rehearsal-arm.md):** on the host, while the registry entry `nfl-elo-v2` reads the architect's verdict PASS, `status: "rehearsal"` and `rehearsal_window: {"opens_at": "<instant>Z", "closes_at": "<instant>Z"}` (read, never written here; the verdict-record PR writes it on PASS only), a game whose KICKOFF is in [opens_at, closes_at) also gets nfl_elo_v2's probability, priced by v1's own live walk under the declared term for the whole walk, from the walk floor (addendum 251 item 3(3): `nfl_rehearsal.WALK_FLOOR` = `nfl_backtest.WARMUP_SEASON`, 2024; a stored game before it is left out of the arm's walk and counted; v1's walk takes no floor, unchanged), at full precision, and stored on v1's row as `factor_breakdown.nfl_elo_v2_rehearsal` (a failed walk stores its error; v1's row is written as always), with one receipt line `rehearsal arm nfl-elo-v2 (<stamp>, <form> <value>): N of M covered rows priced (…); walk floor 2024; K stored games before it, excluded; rehearsal — not a call`. Dormant otherwise: nothing walked, stored or printed. |
| `export-nfl-predictions` | `--week` / `--days N` · `--out PATH` | NFL export (`rehearsal: false`; since addendum 214 the file carries `certification` "uncertified (addendum 214): …", additive, and its note says UNCERTIFIED; every row still reaches the Desk, which calls it PASS, market-only): quarantine contract (rows whose market_divergence_pp has an absolute value of 15 or more carry quarantine=true. The threshold is applied to the absolute value of market_divergence_pp as exported, rounded to one decimal: an unrounded divergence whose absolute value rounds to 15.0 (14.96 to 14.99 on either side of zero; exactly 14.95 goes by the float's binary representation) is quarantined, the conservative side of the boundary. Addendum 207 item 1), STALE-BOOK? venue check, Elo/rest "why" fields, QB status in input_quality. **Rows default to the current slate: kickoffs in the next 36h.** `--week` is the full 8-day look-ahead; `--days N` sets it explicitly. Prediction generation is unchanged; `predict-nfl` still stores the whole week for close grading. Prints venue-check lines plus a `window:` receipt line. The `line_move.venues.book` block carries `from_source_updated_at` / `to_source_updated_at` beside `from_at` / `to_at` (its `market` block shows no capture time, so carries neither). `source_updated_at` (ARCHITECT 2026-10-09, addendum 32 items 3-4) is the provider's own time for a game's odds (american football's and api-football's `response[].update`, naive UTC, the same form as the capture time beside it; api-football's since #615, addendum 147 item 1): the newest that any quote in the answer can be; a quote may be older; never the time of a quote. Where a session's rows differ, the oldest. null = unknown (every source whose answer carries no update time: MLB's api-baseball (#628) and hockey; rows written before `migrate_odds_source_updated_at.py`; a row without it), and unknown is never read as fresh. `--out PATH` writes that file instead of `exports/nfl_predictions_<UTC date>.json` (a closing run's export, addendum 40 item 2); a path under `data/` or onto the configured database is refused (law 5). **The rehearsal arm (addendum 230 item 1(a) as ruled by 247; GATE-CLASS, an export contract):** on the host, a row whose kickoff the registry's rehearsal window covers (verdict PASS, status `rehearsal`) carries six flat keys outside `prediction`: `rehearsal_version` ("nfl-elo-v2"), `rehearsal_probabilities` ({home_win, away_win} from the stored arm, presented at four places; the arm is stored at full precision, Codex on #825; or null), `rehearsal_term` ({form, value}), `rehearsal_verdict_stamp` ("nfl-elo-v2 PASS <verdict.at>"), `rehearsal_label` ("rehearsal — not a call") and `rehearsal_reason` (null, or why there are no probabilities). Absent on every other row and off the host (a dormant file is byte-identical); the Desk reads none of them; the top-level `rehearsal: false` is untouched. The closing run's summary block and receipt carry one arm line per armed row; the phone page carries none. |
| `nfl-backtest` | | Walk-forward backtest against the frozen phase-2 gate — in the live era a provenance/regression check; prints a scope line. |
| `nfl-v2-gate` | `--preflight` · `--architect-word TEXT` · `--stream-fingerprint HEX` · `--db PATH` · `--no-fetch` | nfl-elo-v2's ONE run (ARCHITECT 2026-11-02, addendum 215 items 2-3; GATE-CLASS): the FROZEN phase-2 protocol on the corrected stream (`nfl_backtest.walk_protocol`, the code `nfl-backtest` runs: warm-up 2024 update only, scored 2025 regular + post predict-then-update, preseason excluded, baseline the warm-up's realized home rate; PASS iff log-loss <= baseline − 0.010 AND every 10pp band with n >= 30 within 7pp), the candidate being nfl_elo_v1's walk with the ONE declared calibration term (registry `calibration_term`: `shrink` s, p' = 0.5 + s·(p − 0.5) at pricing only; or `home_advantage`, v1's 48 replaced for the whole walk). REFUSES (exit 2, nothing written, nothing recorded), in this order: not declared and unrun, or reserved; no word; no fingerprint; the term not declared, both forms set, or an invalid value; the fit not recorded (`calibration_term.fit` null, not an object, or without `warmup_fingerprint`; addendum 219 item 4); the entry's frozen `constants` not the walk's (no block, a key missing or extra against `NFLEloConfig`'s dataclass fields (a field added to the config later is walked but not frozen: refused), or any value not exactly `NFLEloConfig()`'s, home_advantage against v1's base 48.0; Codex P1 on #784: a default that drifts after the declaration cannot spend the one run on another model; nothing read, nothing reserved); no `--db`; a `--db` path under data/ (resolved, or by identity), the app's configured database, or missing (never created); then, the stream loaded and nothing scored, a season with no game, a stale fit (`fit.warmup_fingerprint` is not the loaded 2024 rows' warm-up fingerprint; 219 item 4), or a stream fingerprint that does not match. The STREAM FINGERPRINT is v2: it digests the rows AND the declared term (form and value as the run uses them), so a term edited after the architect's word changes it and the run refuses (219 item 4: "the word names the term"). `--db PATH` reads a .backup copy outside data/ through a mode=ro + immutable=1 URI (219 item 6; mlb-pitchers' `check_db` and `readonly_session`), no sidecar; the ONE run REFUSES without it (item 6 names the copy; the mlb-pitchers pattern), and a copy with a -wal, -shm or -journal beside it is refused (immutable=1 would ignore it); `--preflight` without it reads DATABASE_URL (215 item 3's 11-04 preflight on the live DB); the scope line names the database read. The reservation `docs/registry/nfl-elo-v2.started.json` (after the #329 cross-ref guard) carries the word, the fingerprints, the term and the database, and precedes the first scored game; it is whole or absent (serialised before the exclusive create, then written, fsynced and closed and its directory fsynced; a failed write, fsync, close or directory fsync removes the file and the run refuses, nothing scored, the read not spent; a removal that fails too names the file to inspect; Codex P1 on #784; a create that fails for any other reason refuses the same way, nothing created, and a removal is followed by a directory fsync, one that cannot be made durable said and the read not claimed unspent, #798, addendum 222 item 2); ONE run, recorded with its scored 2025 ids (`registry.record_run`). `--preflight` scores nothing: the scope line, the warm-up and scored counts beside 215's 285 (printed, never asserted), the counts by stage, the preseason and other seasons left out, the 2024 baseline, the declared term or NOT DECLARED, the entry's frozen constants against `NFLEloConfig()`'s (SAME, or DIFFERS: the run refuses), the warm-up fingerprint against the fit's (fit NOT RECORDED or CHANGED: the run refuses; SAME: bound); last line `STREAM FINGERPRINT <sha256>` (v2: the stream and the declared term; with no term declared it binds the undeclared term and the run refuses). No 2025 outcome is printed. |
| `soccer-v23-gate` | `--preflight` · `--architect-word TEXT` · `--stream-fingerprint HEX` · `--db PATH` · `--no-fetch` | soccer-v23's ONE run (ARCHITECT 2026-11-04, addendum 248 item 2(b); addendum 230 item 1(d); addendum 216 items 1-3; addendum 251 item 2; GATE-CLASS; lane soccer-v23, #656). REGISTERED, NEVER RUN BEFORE THE DECLARATION: the registry holds no soccer-v23 entry until the architect's declaration (11-20), and the run refuses without one; the one run is the operator's block on the architect's word (11-27). Both arms through the ONE prediction function `soccer_v23.predict(snapshot, fixture, arm)`: `v22` (v22 re-walked on this harness, production as of the snapshot: g and b from the competition's visible rows, the ladder, the real `estimate_strengths` with the bounded as-of backfill, the real `predict_match` with v22's stored config) and `soccer-v23` (the joint log-linear fit, src/models/joint_poisson.py, priced through the real `predict_match`); g and b (soccer-v23: μ and h) are AS-OF in the harness and in the production path `predict_live` alike (`CONTEXT_DEFINITION`, 251 item 2; the stored trained context is never read). THE BAR (`BAR_TEXT`, docs/specs/soccer-v23.md): the mean three-way log-loss at least 0.0050 BELOW BOTH v22's recorded figure (the declaration slot `v22_recorded_log_loss`, never recomputed) AND v22's re-walked figure on the identical scored set (a tie on either rejects; INVALID under 30 or on differing sets), the early-season matches INCLUDED (within-season skip zero); both figures printed and recorded side by side. REFUSES (exit 2, nothing reserved, nothing recorded), in order: no declared unrun entry or a reservation; a null or invalid declaration slot (`declaration_slots`: scope, first_season, visibility, cut, availability_on, v22_recorded_log_loss, v22_recorded_source); the entry's `frozen_constants` or `gate` not the code's; no word; no fingerprint (refused here, before any load, MATCHING nfl-v2-gate and ncaa-gate: soccer_v23.py:1954, nfl_v2_gate.py:642, ncaa_v1r_gate.py:792); no `--db` or a copy nfl-v2-gate's `check_db` refuses; the copy holding more than one soccer_elo_poisson row marked production (never the newest picked); the copy's availability record (addendum 252's two tables) half present, present and not migrated, or holding a soccer injury_status fact the reader cannot map (a fact of another kind is no input, addendum 263 item 25, never read; the record is read through `record.as_of`, the one read path, at every decision instant while the copy is open: addendum 263 item 23; no soccer fact in force is "no record", the term off); the copy's production version not v22; a fence refusing at the cut (pl-availability, the leagues' window, soccer-expansion); under scope LEAGUE_REGULAR (the `scope` slot's only value, addendum 263 item 2) a walked row's stage label that cannot be placed; a fingerprint that does not match (refused after the load, as in both precedents). Never refused: a club's arrival off the declared pyramid (`PYRAMIDS`: the outside-the-store prior, counted and labelled, addendum 263 item 15) or a thin season (no home or no away goal: excluded by cause from the scored set and soccer-v23's window, named by `--preflight` before the reservation, counted in the record, addendum 263 item 14). Then the #329 cross-ref guard and the reservation `docs/registry/soccer-v23.started.json` before any outcome is scored or priced (the stream with its goals is loaded and fingerprinted before it, as nfl-v2-gate and ncaa-gate do; the order ruled by addendum 254 item 3); then the walk, `soccer_backtest.compare_candidate`, the strata (reported only), the per-era AVAILABILITY line and addendum 254 item 3's two labels (the day rule's residual; "v22 re-walked" as v22 on this harness, the rows read at their 90-minute score counted), on every verdict, INVALID included, with addendum 263's three (the v22 arm's tie order; the thin seasons excluded; the off-pyramid arrivals, counted), and `registry.record_run`. A failure in the walk, after the reservation (the fit's `FitRefused`, or a walk guard), prints the REFUSED line with its reason, saying the reservation stands and the one run is spent, exit 2, nothing recorded (addendum 264 item 2(b); 263 item 13), never a traceback. `--preflight` fits nothing and prices nothing; with no declared slots it loads nothing; each competition's line names its first season IN SCOPE (addendum 264 item 2(a)) and outside the thin set, the thin seasons before it, and every later season priced as a first season because its S−1 is thin (addendum 263 item 14); its last line is `STREAM FINGERPRINT <sha256>`. |
| `nfl-v2-fit-warmup` | `--db PATH` | nfl-elo-v2's 2024-ONLY fit for the declaration proposal (addendum 215 item 2; read-only): selects ONLY the 2024 NFL rows (season `2024` in the query; any other season refuses), walks them predict-then-update from default ratings with nfl_elo_v1's constants, and prints for each form its 2024-fitted value and method — shrink s: the grid 0.001..1.500 (step 0.001) minimizing the 2024 mean log-loss, price only; home advantage h: the grid 0..120 (step 0.5) minimizing the 2024 mean log-loss of the re-run walk; ties to the value nearest v1's; a grid end flagged — with the 2024 bands stated vs realized before and after, and the 2024 warm-up fingerprint. `--db PATH`: a .backup copy, nfl-v2-gate's own `--db` (outside data/, never created, mode=ro + immutable=1); without it, DATABASE_URL. Never reads or prints a 2025 figure; writes nothing; records nothing. |
| `nfl-grade` | `--days N` | **The stated record (ARCHITECT 2026-10-06):** season to date by default, from live_since onward. Ties are pushes outside the hit denominator, and pre-live rows print under their own line, as in RESULTS.md and the results file. `--days N` is the rolling read. Grade predictions vs finished games + banked closer consensus (sides, log-loss, model-close divergence). Also prints value-side-vs-close beside pick-vs-close: the value side is where model − market was positive at the earliest pre-kickoff **book** snapshot (which `sync-odds-football` now appends every sync); games with no snapshot are counted as unanchored, never guessed. Each graded line records the anchor timestamp beside the prediction's (`anchor=… pred=…`); an anchor later than the prediction is flagged `⚠ ANCHOR AFTER PREDICTION` and counted. `export-nfl-results` carries the same per row (`value_side`, `value_side_clv`, `value_shadow`, `value_anchor_at`, `value_prediction_at`). **The record against the close (ARCHITECT 2026-10-13, addendum 63 item 3):** `vs_close` over the decided games that have a close: the games, the model's log-loss, the close's, close minus model, its bootstrap 95% interval (10,000 resamples, seed 20261013) and the count of games the model beat the close (one helper, `src/walters/vs_close.py`; "A win rate is never printed without it"), read as the results file reads its rows (the close's four-place fair), printed under the sides line; the pre-live line carries its own. The sides line's `log-loss` stays over every live game (a tie as a home loss). **Void: re-dated (addendum 106 item 4; #574):** a game void by its closing rows in `exports/` leaves every line and is named under `── void: re-dated N · placeholder kickoff M`, as the results file and RESULTS.md leave it out; a window whose games are all void still prints that line before `no finished NFL games`. A closing row recorded at the 04:00Z placeholder kickoff whose game moved more than 1h is the same void under its own label, "void: placeholder kickoff" (addendum 109 item 2; `src/ingestion/placeholder.py`), counted as M. |
| `nfl-qb-audit` | `--team NAME` · `--live` · `--season` | QB-feed audit: the team's stored injury rows, the pre-fix vs fixed `qb_listed` read, unresolved positions, and sync timing vs the last kickoff. Read-only on the DB. `--live` spends 2 provider requests (injuries + roster) and classifies each listed player: H1 not on the report / H2 position did not resolve / H3 dropped by the old 14-day filter. |
| `export-nfl-results` | `--days N` `--match ID…` | Graded NFL results file for the consumer (standard results shape; live era — no rehearsal flag). **SEASON TO DATE by default (ARCHITECT 2026-10-05):** the file was a rolling 8 days, so Week 3 TNF (GB–ATL, match 15073) aged out of the 10-05 file and a lifetime record summed from the files could not reconcile. The file now carries `window` ({kind: season_to_date, season}) and `record` (games, top-pick hits, by week). `--days N` keeps a rolling window; `--match ID` prints whether each id is in the file. **`close_minutes_before_start` (ARCHITECT addendum 31 item 3, #382; additive):** every graded row that carries `close_at` also carries it: whole minutes, rounded down, from the close's capture (`close_at`) to the stored start; null where there is no close. It is the age of OUR CAPTURE, not of the quote (#348). `graded.value_anchor_source_updated_at` sits beside `value_anchor_at`; the close's book stamp is carried as its age, `close_book_stamp_age_h` (below). **THE STALE BOOK CLOSE (ARCHITECT 2026-10-19, addendum 137 item 4, #614):** "FOR A GRADED GAME WHOSE BOOK QUOTE CARRIES A PROVIDER STAMP MORE THAN 24 HOURS BEFORE KICKOFF, THE RECORDED CLOSE IS THE KALSHI TWO-SIDED MID CAPTURED IN THE SAME LAST PRE-KICKOFF SESSION" (`close.stale_book_close`, one helper: the NFL grader, this file, `grading_close`, `clv-restate` and the NCAA shadow grade read it). The stamp is the oldest `source_updated_at` of the rows the book close priced; more than 24 h before the stored kickoff, the close is the HOME leg's (bid + ask) / 2 of the newest two-sided Kalshi HOME capture within 10 minutes of the book capture (`CAPTURE_SESSION`: one closing run writes the book, then Kalshi), under the 2c spread cap and the mismatched-legs guard; the row says `close_reference: kalshi`, `close_kalshi` {bid, ask, mid, spread_c, book_fair, book_at} (the book close it replaced) and `close_at` the Kalshi capture's. With no such quote in that session the close is UNPRICED (`close_fair` null, `close_stale_book` says why). A NULL or absent stamp keeps the book close ("unknown is not stale"); a three-way board keeps it too (`close_stale_unswitched`: no two-sided mid is derived for one). Every row carries `close_reference` (books | kalshi | null) and `close_book_stamp_age_h` (hours to two places; null: no close or the stamp unknown), so each `vs_close` block carries `kalshi_only_closes`, the closes a Kalshi mid priced, and its line names them.  `source_updated_at` (ARCHITECT 2026-10-09, addendum 32 items 3-4) is the provider's own time for a game's odds (american football's and api-football's `response[].update`, naive UTC, the same form as the capture time beside it; api-football's since #615, addendum 147 item 1): the newest that any quote in the answer can be; a quote may be older; never the time of a quote. Where a session's rows differ, the oldest. null = unknown (every source whose answer carries no update time: MLB's api-baseball (#628) and hockey; rows written before `migrate_odds_source_updated_at.py`; a row without it), and unknown is never read as fresh. **The record against the close (addendum 63 item 3):** `record.vs_close`, each `record.by_week[w].vs_close` and `pre_live.record.vs_close`, each over the decided games that have a close: the games, the model's log-loss, the close's, close minus model, its bootstrap 95% interval (10,000 resamples, seed 20261013) and the count of games the model beat the close (one helper, `src/walters/vs_close.py`; "A win rate is never printed without it"); the receipt prints each under its win rate (one line per week). **Void: re-dated (addendum 106 item 4; #574):** a row void by its closing rows (`redated.py`, read from the out dir) keeps its fields, gains `redated` and leaves the season and pre-live records; `record.redated` and `pre_live.record.redated` count them, and the receipt prints `void: re-dated N · placeholder kickoff M` and names each row with its label; the pre-live line's games are its record's, and `--match` reads such a row `IN the file (void: re-dated, out of every record)` (a placeholder row: `IN the file (void: placeholder kickoff, out of every record)`, addendum 109 item 2). **The graded export (#755, addendum 196 item 4(i)):** every row (live and pre-live) carries `graded_export` {kind, file, run_id, exported_at}, the export it stands behind (a closing file's row over a Desk slate file's, as `export-results`; `src/walters/graded_export.py`), and the file's `graded_exports` counts the rows by kind; read-side naming only, the grade is the database's. **`model_close_divergence` (ARCHITECT 2026-10-19, addendum 137 item 3, #612; an export-contract change):** every graded row carries `graded.model_close_divergence`: model p minus close fair on the model's pick, a fraction (0.0453 = +4.53pp), NOT a closing-line value (close minus entry is the Cockpit P&L's, on executed fills). **Its alias `graded.clv` is REMOVED (#621):** ruled to stay through v1.5.0 and go in the release after; it shipped through v1.6.2 and is gone from the next release (its own changelog line); a reader of the graded block reads `model_close_divergence`. Here it is computed at write on the row's close; the pick is HOME when p ≥ 0.5. **The rehearsal arm (addendum 230 item 1(a) as ruled by 247):** on the host, every row whose prediction stores nfl-elo-v2's arm carries `rehearsal_arm` ({version, model_version, term, verdict_stamp, label, p_home, home_win_prob, away_win_prob, top_pick, top_pick_hit, push, log_loss, brier_score, reason}; `p_home` is the stored full-precision probability every figure is computed from, `home_win_prob` / `away_win_prob` present it at four places, Codex on #825), whatever the registry reads now (written once, read for the record always), and `record.rehearsal_arm` carries both arms over the same games, in total and per game week (the row's `week`): log-loss (the frozen gate's arithmetic: a tie a home loss), Brier ((p - y)^2 on the same y), W-L-P and vs-close (a tie a push), each rule on its line in `rules`, with the precision on its own line (`rules.precision`, addendum 251 item 3: "v2 at full precision as stored; v1 at four places as stored; the difference is bounded at about 1e-4 of log-loss"). Absent when no row carries an arm. |

## Market-only competitions (cups, UNL, NCAA, NHL, NBA, ATP, WTA)

No model output ships for these: the cup and NHL model tracks are
SUSPENDED (2026-09-25; cups at a rotation floor, NHL at the
schedule-only floor), UNL and NCAA are market-only by doctrine, and the
NBA enters market-only with no model declared (ARCHITECT 2026-11-05,
addendum 261 item 1, lane basketball-market-only), as does tennis, ATP and
WTA (addendum 261 item 4; lane tennis-market-only, #838; Kalshi's own
KXATPMATCH / KXWTAMATCH events are its fixtures and their settlement its
results, addendum 269 items 1-3). Data
syncs run normally; the consumer receives market-only files:

| Command | Options | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `sync-kalshi-ncaa` | | Kalshi `KXNCAAFGAME` markets via the shared two-sided matcher (the primary college market source). **Where a market is placed (the switch, addendum 50 6(b)):** `kalshi_match.place`: the event's two names, from its own markets, fit a game of the series' own competition one each, on the ticker's day (US Eastern); on a date-only series the own date before a day either side; the run's window is read last, on the one game chosen; every refusal is printed by its reason. **The pair (#413, ARCHITECT 2026-11-03, addendum 230 item 0(a)):** the event's two names come from its sibling markets (each market's `yes_sub_title`), as on the soccer path since 09-13; the yes-team fallback is gone (`KalshiAdapter.yes_team` removed). **The "A vs B" title fallback, NARROWED (ARCHITECT 2026-11-03 13:05 ET, addendum 234 item 2: "NARROWED, and it never overrides"):** two sibling names are the pair and the title is never read; three or more sibling names are refused `the event's markets do not name two teams` (NO_PAIR) and the title is never read (the override of conflicting siblings is removed); only where the siblings name fewer than two teams (one market listed, a market with no name) is the event's first priced market's title read (`kalshi_match._pair`, `_title_teams`: the "A vs B ..." form by `KalshiAdapter.title_teams`, unchanged; the question form kalshi-history's API reads carry, "Will <Y> win the <A> vs <B> <LEAGUE> game?", parsed strictly and case-insensitively, LEAGUE one of the closed `QUESTION_LEAGUES` ("Pro Football", the only phrase the repo evidences) and <Y> one of its two names, else no pair; <Y> must also be the market's own yes team (its non-empty `yes_sub_title`, case-folded), else TITLE_CONFLICT, never placed: addendum 237 item 2), and its two names are the pair only when every sibling's yes team is one of them (the market's `yes_sub_title`, compared ignoring case, the pair formed carrying the market's own spelling so place()'s side lookup holds: ARCHITECT 2026-11-03, addendum 238 item 2(b), "a refusal on letter case alone was never the intent") or the sibling names none (a draw leg, a blank name); any other sibling refuses the whole event under its own sentinel, `the title's two names do not agree with the event's markets` (TITLE_CONFLICT; counted among the ambiguous, its line names the title and the markets' names), never a placement. No "A vs B" title, or a title that does not name exactly two teams (one name empty, the same name twice in any case, or a name that itself carries " vs "/" vs. "/" v "/" v." in any case, as "A vs B vs C" and "A vs B VS C" do), is NO_PAIR. Never placed by one name. Every title today reads "<Team> wins", so on a live run the fallback reads nothing and such an event is refused NO_PAIR; kalshi-history's API reads carry the exchange's titles, and there the narrowing bites. With nothing placed and an unstarted game in the window the zero-match alarm prints. |

on a date-only series the own date before a day either side; the run's window is read last, on the one game chosen; every refusal is printed by its reason. **The pair (#413, ARCHITECT 2026-11-03, addendum 230 item 0(a)):** the event's two names come from its sibling markets (each market's `yes_sub_title`), as on the soccer path since 09-13; | `sync-kalshi-nba` | | Kalshi `KXNBAGAME` markets via the shared two-sided matcher (ARCHITECT 2026-11-06, addendum 271 item 3(h); the ticker READ on #839's series listing, `venue.KALSHI_SERIES_BY_COMPETITION`). Placement exactly as `sync-kalshi-nhl`: the event's two names (its markets' `yes_sub_title`) fit one stored NBA game one each on the ticker's US Eastern day, read on our stored kickoff (the served timestamp, naive UTC), never the provider's date string (addendum 272 item 4); a tie (the two Los Angeles franchises named alike, two games of one pair) is refused by its sentinel and counted, never guessed. No NBA code table, pin or synonym is added: the matcher reads the stored names (the 30 served on #839). Its fee M is `KALSHI_FEE_M["KXNBAGAME"] = (1.0, 1.0)`, RULED (ARCHITECT 2026-11-06, addendum 274 item 1(a)): the taker M the published schedule's default (the schedule, "Last updated and effective: July 7, 2026", does not list KXNBAGAME), the maker M the #839 series listing's `fee_multiplier` 1 (`quadratic_with_maker_fees`), taken as the higher of the two readings; an NBA row prices both costs, both sides, and no NBA call exists to price (PAPER). The mapped-but-unlisted rule (`venue.kalshi_exec`: no M, no cost) stays for any future series. Refuses with a reason (exit 0, as NHL's) when competition NBA is not in the DB. Runs on `nba-daily` and the window chain's NBA capture tier (`chains.WINDOW_KALSHI`). |
| `sync-kalshi-ncaamb` | | Kalshi `KXNCAAMBGAME` markets (NCAA men's basketball, competition `NCAAMB`, never `NCAA`, which is football) via the shared two-sided matcher, exactly as `sync-kalshi-nba` (ARCHITECT 2026-11-12, addendum 352 item 2; addendum 353 item 2: the series READ from Kalshi's own listing by the operator, 2026-11-12T15:36Z, `venue.KALSHI_SERIES_BY_COMPETITION`; KXNCAABGAME is dormant and not mapped). The event's two names fit one stored NCAAMB game one each; the event's day is the date in its ticker read as a US Eastern day, and a game is on it when our stored kickoff, read in US Eastern, falls on that date or a day either side (addendum 44 item 1); `occurrence_datetime` places nothing (addendum 52 item 4). A game with a non-Division-I side has no market and is never paired (272 item 2); a tie is refused by its sentinel and counted, never guessed. Its fee M is `KALSHI_FEE_M["KXNCAAMBGAME"] = (1.0, 1.0)`, RULED on that listing's read (353 item 2: `quadratic_with_maker_fees`, `fee_multiplier` 1, as KXNBAGAME's). Market-only, PAPER, no call. Refuses with a reason (exit 0) when competition NCAAMB is not in the DB. Runs on `ncaamb-daily` and the window chain's NCAAMB capture tier (`chains.WINDOW_KALSHI`). |
| `sync-kalshi-atp` · `sync-kalshi-wta` | | Kalshi `KXATPMATCH` / `KXWTAMATCH` open markets (ARCHITECT 2026-11-06, addendum 269 item 1 K1: "the two tour series READ as served"; `venue.KALSHI_SERIES_BY_COMPETITION`), read TO THE LAST PAGE by the adapter's own pager (269 item 3(d): "a cursor left is a refusal, never a partial count"; a refusal stores nothing and prints its reason), placed by the shared matcher `kalshi_match.place` on the stored ATP / WTA rows (the event's two players are its markets' `yes_sub_title`, never the event title: 269 item 1 K2), then THE PLAYER CHECK (`src/ingestion/tennis_kalshi.py`, 269 item 3(a)): a market is kept only on its own event's row and when its `yes_sub_title` names exactly one of the row's two players, the one on its side; naming neither, naming both, naming the other side's, or another event's row are each REFUSED by its own sentinel, counted and printed, never guessed. Then THE KICKOFF CHECK (ARCHITECT 2026-11-08, addendum 288 item 1): a row whose stored kickoff source (`external_ids["kalshi_kickoff"]`) is START_UNREAD, carries none, or carries a refusal label (addendum 294 item 13(b): `KICKOFF_REFUSED_ROW`) is never captured (refused, counted and printed); then THE SERVED KICKOFF CHECK (288 item 1, `tennis_kalshi.served_kickoff_check`): the kickoff rule read again over the event's two open markets in hand, and a placement kept only when it reads a served start equal to the row's stored kickoff (the rule refusing the event, no start served, a moved start or one market in hand: refused, counted, both instants printed; the row is never moved here, the next sync re-reads it). One line counts the placements the kickoff rule refused, and the zero-placement alarm counts as due only a row whose stored kickoff source is a served key (addendum 294 item 9, the tennis caller only; the alarm's text and every other series unchanged). Stored as `OddsSnapshot(source="kalshi", market="ML")` HOME / AWAY; the fee M (1.0, 1.0) for both series (addendum 274 item 1(b), `venue.KALSHI_FEE_M`). UNMETERED; market-only, PAPER, no call. |
| `export-fixtures` | `--competition --start --end` | Market-only fixtures file: schedule, results, book consensus (latest pre-kickoff price per book), Kalshi presence; `contains_predictions: false`. Prints the odds (market, selection) labels it saw. **Kalshi leg guard (ARCHITECT 2026-10-10, addendum 37 item 3):** "A game's Kalshi legs are read as a set only when their tickers name one event and that event's current legs sit on no other game. Otherwise the row says mismatched and names the events, and nothing is computed from those legs: no price, no normalized probability, no cost, no venue row, no line move, no close, no order line. Legs with no stored ticker are read as before." A mismatched row reads `kalshi` {`status`: `mismatched`, `events`, `rule`}, `kalshi_legs` null, the exec fields null, `input_quality.kalshi` `mismatched` (`counts.kalshi_mismatched`); the predictions exports put the same block at `market.kalshi` (NFL: top-level `kalshi` and `input_quality.kalshi`); `desk_meta.kalshi_leg_guard` = true. One helper (`src/walters/kalshi_legs.py`) serves every reader of stored legs; nothing stored is deleted or rewritten; before `migrate_kalshi_ticker.py` it answers nothing. The `market` block (1X2 close and the dark spread-derived fallback) carries `source_updated_at` beside `captured_at`. `source_updated_at` (ARCHITECT 2026-10-09, addendum 32 items 3-4) is the provider's own time for a game's odds (american football's and api-football's `response[].update`, naive UTC, the same form as the capture time beside it; api-football's since #615, addendum 147 item 1): the newest that any quote in the answer can be; a quote may be older; never the time of a quote. Where a session's rows differ, the oldest. null = unknown (every source whose answer carries no update time: MLB's api-baseball (#628) and hockey; rows written before `migrate_odds_source_updated_at.py`; a row without it), and unknown is never read as fresh. |
| `export-nhl-predictions` | `--hours 36` | NHL SHADOW (2026-09-30; **v7 from 2026-10-02**): the best FAILED candidate, `nhl_elo_v7_xg_margin_no_na` (`gate_verdict: "FAILED 0.6885 vs 0.6866"`), as a greyed REFERENCE MODEL for every NHL game in the window, with a `reference` block carrying `nhl_elo_v1`'s probability. Every row carries `engine: model_shadow`. v7 updates on the xG margin (the frozen 2023-24 fit, no `na` level); a game without stored shots updates on goals, counted in the fit receipt (`nhl-daily` runs `nhl-shot-sync` yesterday..today first). **A walk that took that goal-margin fallback for any game is not v7** (ARCHITECT 2026-10-13, addendum 63 item 2): the header and every row read `nhl_elo_v7_goal_margin_fallback`, the fit block stays as it is, the file carries `not_v7` and the run prints it. Where the xG fit cannot be made at all, the file is `nhl_elo_v1` (addendum 49). It writes `exports/nhl_shadow_<stamp>.json` and nothing to the DB; the header carries `git_sha`, the producing checkout's short SHA (`provenance.git_sha()`, as the predictions, fixtures, results and window exports' headers do; null when git cannot say), beside `exported_at` (ARCHITECT 2026-11-04, addendum 244 item 4(a)). It is never a Desk call, never a venue input and never logged; the window card ignores it. On `nhl-daily`. |
| `nhl-shadow-grade` | `--days 30` | Live model-close divergence of the NHL shadow calls (pick-vs-close + value-side), from the last shadow export before each puck drop. v1's pick-vs-close is shown beside each call as the reference (2026-10-02). Each name is graded on its own and the grade says which it graded (addendum 63 item 2): v7 only from files whose fit was made with no goal-margin fallback, `nhl_elo_v7_goal_margin_fallback` on its own line (its file's fit block decides, so an older file named v7 whose walk took the fallback is graded there), never pooled. Read-only, with no hit rate (not a record of calls). **The record against the close (ARCHITECT 2026-10-13, addendum 63 item 3):** `vs_close`, one per name, never pooled (v7, the fallback, and the v1 reference over every graded call carrying v1's number), over the decided games (both scores stored, not level) with a priced close: the games, the model's log-loss, the close's, close minus model, its bootstrap 95% interval (10,000 resamples, seed 20261013) and the count of games the model beat the close (`src/walters/vs_close.py`); a graded call without a decided score is counted (`undecided`) and left out. `results-tally` writes the same numbers into a RESULTS.md shadow section. |
| `ncaa-backtest` | `--baselines-only` · `--candidate v1` | **CLOSED (ARCHITECT 2026-10-09, addendum 24 item 2(c)): REFUSES, exit 2, naming that ruling, with or without `--baselines-only`, before anything is read, for good: it does not return when the #368 fence lifts.** #79's protocol (v1, train 2025, test 2026, all divisions) is closed without a verdict of its own: its candidate is the model ncaa-elo-v1r ran, and v1r FAILED its gate. No command scores a college model on a 2026 game until the next college candidate's run is recorded. The module `ncaa_backtest` stays (the v1r gate, the confirmation code and the shadow import from it). History, until 2026-10-09: #79 NCAA v1 GATE (frozen 2026-09-30), read-only: train 2025 (warm-up), test = the FINISHED 2026 games at run time (n and date range printed), pre/postseason excluded by stage marker. Prints the stream receipts, both baselines (constant 0.5, 2025 home rate) and the frozen bar (`need <= X`). `--baselines-only` stops there, so the bar is recorded before any candidate is scored. Default scores `ncaa_elo_v1` (a-priori constants, no selection) against the three criteria (log-loss <= bar, 10pp bands with n >= 100 within ±5pp, final ratings 1000-2000). Fewer than 500 finished test games = INVALID. Writes nothing; NCAA stays market-only. **GATE SUSPENDED-PENDING-DATA (ruling 2026-10-01):** the 2025 home/away labels are unreliable, so v1's verdict is VOID. The command still runs, as a diagnostic, and prints the status first and last. **Labels (ARCHITECT 2026-10-07):** the stream reads orientation, scores and neutral from `ncaa_cfbd_labels` where a row exists (the matches row otherwise) and prints the uncovered share per season; the neutral flag is data only (no model change; the frozen criteria are unchanged). The #368 test-season fence (ARCHITECT 2026-10-08 addendum 14) refused it until the ncaa-elo-v1r run was recorded; the refusal above replaced it on 2026-10-09. |
| `ncaa-audit` | `--season YYYY` (repeatable) · `--limit 20` | #79 NCAA DATA AUDIT (architect 2026-09-30; v1 verdict PROVISIONAL), read-only: the gate's stream (Sport.NFL + code NCAA, FINISHED, both scores) WITHOUT its exclusions (rows the gate would drop are listed with the reason). Per season (default 2025 and 2026): home rate by verbatim stage and by UTC month (n, home wins, home rate over decided games, ties, mean home margin); repeated pairings (reversed A-hosts-B/B-hosts-A with dates, and same-host repeats); HEURISTIC (b) provider home had fewer prior-season games than the away team; HEURISTIC (c) home rate when both sides are 'established' (>= 8 games that season in the DB; plus a prior-season-count variant) vs not — a proxy, not a division; (d) field inventory of every Match/Team column that could indicate a neutral site or division (non-null/distinct/top values; says so when none exist); suspects (stage/month home rate < 0.52 with n >= 50) and the top teams by home share. Writes nothing. The #368 test-season fence (ARCHITECT 2026-10-08 addendum 14) refused it until the ncaa-elo-v1r run was recorded; lifted 2026-10-09 when the run record reached main (addendum 24 item 2(e)): the command runs. **The 2026 fence (ARCHITECT 2026-10-16, addendum 86 item 3, Q2, #561):** until the ncaa-elo-v2 run is recorded, 2026 among `--season` refuses, exit 2, naming the ruling (before anything is read), and the default seasons are 2025 alone. |
| `ncaa-cfbd-labels` | `--year YYYY` (repeatable) · `--from-file PATH` (`{year}` placeholder for several years) · `--dry-run` · `--save-dir exports/cfbd` · `--division fbs` · `--unmatched-names` · `--limit 15` | NCAA CFBD LABEL LANE (ARCHITECT 2026-10-07, data lane; spec `docs/specs/ncaa-cfbd-labels.md`). Operator-run on the laptop, and weekly on the host's weekly-fullseason chain (below); take the `.backup` first; needs `migrate_ncaa_cfbd_labels.py` (a `--dry-run` does not). Fetches CollegeFootballData `/games` per season (`CFBD_API_KEY` from `.env`, or the process environment (host.env on the host), never printed) and saves the raw payload, timestamped, under `exports/cfbd/` (gitignored; never committed). Joins each completed both-FBS game to OUR NCAA match (±12h, shared name normalization with OUR names html-unescaped first (J2); exact names before substring; either orientation; more than one fit = ambiguous, REFUSED; two source games on one match = both refused). **J1 dateshift (ARCHITECT 2026-10-08):** a game the first pass leaves unmatched is retried within ±36h by the same tiers and joins (`join_via dateshift`, listed with both kickoffs and the offset) only on exactly one (match, orientation), a match the first pass did not join, and agreeing scores; a name fit whose scores disagree stays unmatched, listed with both scores. Upserts `ncaa_cfbd_labels` (orientation same/swapped, neutral, both scores in OUR orientation, season, CFBD `seasonType` as served (J4, `season_type`), provenance). Needs `migrate_ncaa_cfbd_v2.py` too (a `--dry-run` does not). **D10 week (ARCHITECT 2026-10-23, addendum 158 item 1(a)):** CFBD's `week` as served rides on every row (`week`: an integer as served, anything else NULL and counted, NULL on every row of a payload with no week field); needs `migrate_ncaa_cfbd_week.py` (column plus marker; a `--dry-run` does not); the receipt counts the joined rows by season_type and week and the stored NCAA matches of the year by status (counts only). The 2023 ingest is `sync-teams` and `sync-matches --competition NCAA --season 2023` (the provider's listing), then `ncaa-cfbd-labels --year 2023`. **J5 (ARCHITECT 2026-10-08, addendum 11):** a substring-tier fit joins only when our match is scored and the scores agree in that orientation; a first pass whose substring tier gives no clean join goes to the J1 retry (exact tier first across 36h). **L1/L4:** every non-dry run writes one ingest record per season to `ncaa_cfbd_ingest_records` (zero joins included) and stamps every label it writes with the run's `fetched_at`; a non-dry run with any division other than `fbs` refuses (exit 2), a `--dry-run` may read another division. The matches table is never written. Receipt per season: records, both-FBS completed, joined, **the SCOPE coverage line (labelled / CFBD completed both-FBS, >= 95% YES/NO) and EVERY unlabelled in-scope game**, same/swapped, neutral, score-reversed (every corrected row listed with its reason), every unmatched game, ambiguous-refused, the dateshift rows, the J2 names changed by unescape, alias refusals, and the unmatched source names with counts (`--unmatched-names` lists them all: the input for the pinned alias map `src/ingestion/ncaa_cfbd_aliases.json`, filled only in a reviewed PR; J3 pinned App State, Massachusetts, Buffalo, Rice). `--from-file` replays a saved payload (no network, no key); one year per call unless the path carries `{year}`. `--dry-run` writes nothing (no row, no payload). **The pregame neutral flag (ARCHITECT 2026-11-03, addendum 236 item 3(1)):** each NOT-completed both-FBS game of the same payload is joined to our stored fixture by the exact name tier only (no scores before the game, so never the substring tier or the dateshift; ambiguous and duplicate targets refused), and CFBD's `neutralSite` as served goes to `ncaa_cfbd_pregame` with the run's `fetched_at`: never a label, the stream, the FBS team set or the coverage fact. The receipt adds a `PREGAME NEUTRAL FLAG` line (in scope, joined, same/swapped, neutral, not neutral, unknown, unmatched, refused). Written only with `migrate_ncaa_cfbd_pregame.py`'s marker; without it the labels are written as before and the receipt says the pregame rows were not. **On the host's weekly chain (ARCHITECT 2026-11-03, addendum 240 item 4(b)):** "a small ruled PR after #816 puts `ncaa-cfbd-labels --year 2026` on the host's weekly-fullseason chain (Sunday 06:00Z; metered, one read a week — the neutral flags of a week's games are known days ahead and seldom change), its failure a sync-step failure that stops nothing after it; the daily ncaa-schedule chain stays as it is." `weekly-fullseason` (`sp-weekly-fullseason.timer`, Sun 06:00 UTC) runs it LAST, after the `SP_FULLSEASON_LIST` syncs (`chains.NCAA_CFBD_STEP`): its joins read our stored NCAA fixtures, so that, when the host's list carries `NCAA|2026`, the week's full-season sync of them runs first (the host list's NCAA line is not verified here). It is METERED (not in `UNMETERED`: under `SP_PARALLEL_MODE=designated` it runs only on a designated day). The chain's `go_on` names it: a failed run is receipted (`continued`: addendum 240 item 4(b)), the run goes on to any later step, the chain's exit is the first failure's (so `OnFailure` pages) and the closing lines name the step. The read runs only when the list resolves (the setting is set, the file exists and it lists a competition), the daily backup succeeds and no earlier `sync-matches` fails; in every other case that week's read waits for a hand run or the next Sunday. One GET per run: sp_run's transient retry re-runs the step (15s, then 45s; at most 3 reads) only on a timeout, a connection reset or abort, a 429, or a 504 (by its `Gateway Timeout` text); a CFBD 500, 502 or 503 (urllib's `HTTP Error 50x`) is not retried: the step exits 1 with no ingest record and pages. Each Sunday's run writes what any non-dry run writes: the 2026 labels (`ncaa_cfbd_labels`, upserted and re-stamped), one 2026 ingest record (`ncaa_cfbd_ingest_records`, so the D6 coverage fact for 2026 becomes that run's), the pregame rows (`ncaa_cfbd_pregame`, behind its marker) and the raw payload under `exports/cfbd/` (never mirrored: the mirror copies exports/'s top level only). It reads the key from the unit's environment (`CFBD_API_KEY` in host.env, addendum 240 item 4(a)); without it it refuses with exit 2 before any read. An HTTP 200 with an empty list exits 0 and does not page, yet writes a 0-record 2026 ingest record that becomes 2026's latest: every 2026 label and pregame row then reads non-current (L3), D6's 2026 coverage fact reads not covered and the stream walks no 2026 label until a later non-empty read. A payload whose required fields are missing also writes such a record, but refuses with exit 2 and pages. |

| `ncaa-v1r-gate` | `--preflight` · `--architect-word TEXT` · `--stream-fingerprint HEX` · `--no-fetch` | ncaa-elo-v1r GATE (ARCHITECT 2026-10-08, addendum 11 item 3, D3-D6; GATE-CLASS; spec docs/specs/ncaa-elo-v1r.md "Commands"). Walks the D2 stream (`load_v1r_stream`) with the shared D1 wrapper (`NeutralRuleElo`): 2024 update only; the 2025 games whose season_type is exactly `regular` predicted, then updated (the test set); any other 2025 game walked, never scored; the walk ends after the last 2025 game, so no 2026 game is scored. D4 baseline = the stream's 2024 non-neutral `regular` home win rate (0.5 at a neutral site), computed before the walk. D5: PASS iff (1) log-loss < baseline − 0.010 (strict, unrounded; a tie rejects), (2) &#124;mean p − home rate&#124; <= 5pp, (3) &#124;b − 1&#124; <= 0.20 (ML logistic fit on logit(p), p clipped to [1e-6, 1 − 1e-6]; non-convergence fails), (4) every rating after the last 2025 game within 1000-2000; #79's bands, constant-0.5 log-loss, Brier, intercept, cold starts and the neutral / non-neutral split are reported, never gated. D5 (2)/(3) are `ncaa_backtest.level_gap / level_ok / logistic_slope / slope_ok`, the same functions the design receipt calls. REFUSES (exit 2; nothing scored, nothing written), BEFORE THE RESERVATION, unless declared and unrun, no reservation exists, `--architect-word` carries the word verbatim, `--stream-fingerprint` is given, and 2024 + 2025 are covered (L2 + L3, each missing season named); then, the stream loaded and nothing scored, unless its fingerprint equals `--stream-fingerprint` (addendum 17 item 1: the word refers to one stream; both printed on a mismatch) and the scored set (2025 `regular`, level scores skipped) numbers >= 500 (addendum 17 item 2: under 500 nothing is recorded; re-run later, the bar does not move) and the D4 baseline is defined (addendum 21 item 4 (2): 2024 without a non-neutral `regular` game refuses the same way; not an INVALID run, the read is not spent). No `OPEN_ITEMS` gate (addendum 17). Then the reservation `docs/registry/ncaa-elo-v1r.started.json` carrying the word and the fingerprint (after the #329 CROSS-REF GUARD, `registry.cross_ref_guard`; `--no-fetch` skips its fetch and says so), then the walk; ONE run, recorded with its scored ids and the word with the fingerprint beside it (`registry.record_run`; commit docs/registry/ in a PR). `--preflight` scores nothing: per season the stream by season_type, the neutral and no-flag counts, the level scores, the coverage, the D4 baseline and the test-set size; its LAST LINE is `STREAM FINGERPRINT <sha256>`, a sha256 over the 2024 + 2025 stream as the gate walks it (every game up to the last 2025 game, in order, with match id, kickoff, label season, season_type, merged home / away ids, scores, neutral flag, label source). `ncaa-backtest` (#79) refuses since 2026-10-09 (addendum 24 item 2(c)). **Run once, 2026-10-09T16:18:32Z; verdict FAIL (ARCHITECT 2026-10-09, addendum 24: the spread test, slope b 1.420537 outside 0.80 to 1.20); the entry is `closed`, so the command refuses (declared and unrun no longer holds). The test set is RETIRED (`registry.RETIRED_TEST_SETS`).** |
