| `dedupe-matches` | `--competition --source api_american_football --apply --backup PATH --sample 8 --orphans` | ARCHITECT 2026-10-03 (priority): duplicate fixtures left by a resync that missed RE-KEYED provider ids. A pair = same competition, same home AND away team, kickoffs within 12h, different source ids. The receipt shows how the rows differ (ext id, team ids, utc_date, status, score). `--apply` (with a verified `.backup`, matches count = live) merges the NEWER row into the OLDER one, which the ledger, odds and snapshots reference: it takes the fresh status, scores and kickoff and the new id (the old id is kept as `<source>_prev`), every referencing row is re-pointed (tables discovered from the schema), and the empty newer row is deleted. Clusters of 3+, swapped pairs and unique-table conflicts are reported, never merged. Since this fix `sync-matches` re-keys instead of duplicating (american-football family), and `export-fixtures` carries one row per fixture (the finished one) until the dedupe runs. Every summary also prints the STATE: rows, rows re-keyed in place (carrying `<source>_prev`), stale orphans, and re-keys by provenance (`<source>_rekeys` log: dedupe-merge / orphan-merge / orphan-relink / sync), so a 0-pair run says the rows were already re-keyed. Every run (the pair path and `--orphans`, dry run or apply) then prints one record line, ARCHITECT 2026-11-04 15:45 ET, addendum 254 item 2(4), verbatim: "dedupe-matches prints one receipt line, "record rows on retired match ids: N", and rewrites nothing." N counts the availability record's rows (both tables) on the newer row of each merge the run made (a relink or an orphan mark retires no id; a merge refused at apply is left out); a dry run counts the newer row of every planned merge, so its N can exceed the apply's. The other two forms are `record rows on retired match ids: n/a (availability tables absent)` (before the migration) and `record rows on retired match ids: n/a (the live DB's tables could not be read)`. The count is a SELECT only (docs/specs/availability-record.md section 6). **`--orphans`** (ARCHITECT 2026-10-03) takes SCHEDULED rows with no score that are stale (kickoff >6h past) or have a twin (same home AND away within 48h, a different id) and resolves each id at the provider (`GET /games?id=`). Outcomes: found → untouched; lookup error → UNRESOLVED, untouched (incomplete coverage anywhere in a candidate's set, whether a twin's lookup or any day of the date search, leaves the candidate AND its twins UNRESOLVED: no merge or relink without complete lookups; PR #266 review); NOT FOUND + one live twin → merged (the older row keeps the references); NOT FOUND, no twin → the provider's games on the row's date ±2 days are searched for the pair. If exactly one game is found and no row holds its id, the row is RELINKED in place; if nothing is found, the row is marked `STALE_ORPHAN` (never deleted, never in `export-fixtures`, never an odds target; `export-predictions` over today's range, whose default status is all, still carries a same-day one that holds a prediction, with status `stale_orphan`: the #568 D9 question). Ambiguous, swapped or held-elsewhere cases are refused and reported. Dry-run unless `--apply --backup PATH`. MLB is not this command's (its lookups are the american-football adapter's): MLB rows the provider drops go through `retire-orphans` (#568). |
| `merge-teams` | `--apply --pair KEPT:MERGED --backup PATH` | ARCHITECT 2026-10-12 (addendum 44 item 3, addendum 50 item 5; #426, #358): one program is one team row. Dry run by default: counts the stored team names carrying an HTML entity by sport, prints the references read from the live DB, and lists every group of rows whose names are equal once unescaped and reduced to letters and digits without case, as a `[candidate] --pair KEPT:MERGED` or `[left]` with why. A pair is a candidate only when the sport is the same, no stored game has the two playing each other, and no day (UTC or US Eastern) holds a game of each; three or more rows of one name are left. `--apply` merges only the pairs named with `--pair` (repeatable), each as the dry run printed it, and only with a `.backup` taken just before (`--backup`, as dedupe-matches checks it, on the teams count); `--apply` without a pair is refused before anything is opened, and a named pair that is not a candidate when the apply reads the DB is refused with its reason. The lower id keeps its row; every reference is re-pointed; the other row's provider ids are recorded on it (`<source>_also`, `merged_from`) so a listing carrying either id finds it; the emptied row is deleted. A same `competition_teams` membership is one membership: dropped and counted. Any other unique-table conflict, or a reference the command does not know, refuses the pair, naming the table. Nothing is applied before the architect has read the dry run; before a merge in a sport that stores lineups, #467 must be on main. |
| `refresh-by-id` | `--competition NCAA --days 7` | **ARCHITECT 2026-10-09, addendum 21 item 1.** The ruling: "For NCAA, the kickoff, status and score of every stored SCHEDULED game kicking off in the next seven days are refreshed from the provider by the game's own id." The provider's listing no longer carries college games that have not started, but they still answer by id. The command takes every stored SCHEDULED game of the competition with a kickoff on the UTC dates today .. today+`--days`. For each it makes ONE `GET /games?id=` call (the american-football adapter's `get_game`). From the answer it applies the kickoff, the status (the adapter's map; an unknown code stays SCHEDULED) and the score (a present score is never blanked); since #763 (addendum 202 item 3) also the answer's status code into `status_raw`, never blanking a stored one, and an answer with no status and both totals turns the row FINISHED (#493's score-presence rule). Rate limits follow the sync-odds-football rule: calls are paced at `SP_ODDS_FOOTBALL_RPM` (default 280/min); a game still answered 429 is deferred and retried after the window (Retry-After, else 60s) for up to 2 rounds, never dropped. A game the provider does not return by id is NOT FOUND: counted and listed, untouched, never guessed, never deleted. An answer for other teams is REFUSED and untouched; a lookup error is UNRESOLVED and untouched; a row with no provider id is listed. The receipt is printed and appended to the receipts log (`SP_RECEIPTS`, else `logs/receipts.jsonl`, kind `refresh_by_id`). It gives asked, unchanged, updated, kickoff moved (each with both times), status and score changes, and not found (each listed). The last line is `REFRESH-BY-ID-RECEIPT {counts}`. Exit 1 when a game stays unresolved or still rate limited; NOT FOUND is not a failure. Runs last on the host's `ncaa-schedule` chain and in the laptop morning chain (Phase 1). Order (ARCHITECT 2026-10-09, addendum 30 item 4): "The laptop's morning chain reads NCAA by date for today to today+7, one day per call, and then runs refresh-by-id, as ncaa-schedule does on the host. After a re-key the listing reads carry the new ids and the refresh confirms them; in the other order every re-keyed game prints as not found." **Scores by team (ARCHITECT 2026-11-08, addendum 292 item 3(b); #876):** the answer's teams are resolved to the stored row's own team ids and the shared helper decides: the same pair as before; the pair REVERSED is written by team (the score swapped into the stored orientation, the kickoff and status as served; REFUSED until this) and listed; neither is REFUSED as before. The head line carries `orientation reversed N · teams mismatch refused M`, each reversed row prints `orientation REVERSED (scores written by team): match <id> ...`, the `REFRESH-BY-ID-RECEIPT` line carries both counts and the receipts-log record both lists. **The provider's orientation (ARCHITECT 2026-11-08, addendum 297 C(i) and (ii)):** the same verdict is recorded in `external_ids["provider_orientation"]` through the sync-matches upsert's own helper (`score_orientation.with_orientation_key`): REVERSED writes `reversed`, SAME removes the key, neither writes `mismatch` (the row's score, kickoff and status still untouched). The odds writers read it (`sync-odds`, `sync-odds-football`). A key that changes counts the row as updated. **SP_SYNC_HOLD (#921; ARCHITECT 2026-11-10, addendum 332 item 3):** a code the setting names is held: the step prints `HELD <code> (SP_SYNC_HOLD)`, builds no adapter, writes nothing and exits 0 (docs/specs/hosting-h1.md section O). |
| `resync-diff` | `--competition --season [--date-from --date-to --sample 12]` | READ-ONLY (NCAA audit ruling, 2026-10-01). It fetches the provider's CURRENT listing and compares it to our stored rows by provider match id: home/away labels (same / swapped / different), scores, dates moved > 1h, rows on one side only, and the home win rate under OUR labels vs the PROVIDER's. It ends with one `RESYNC-DIFF VERDICT:` line. Use it INSTEAD of a re-sync to answer "bad copy or bad at source": `sync-matches` never rewrites home/away on existing rows. It refreshes only the scores, so on swapped labels a re-sync would flip stored results. **#368 fence:** with `--competition NCAA` and a listing that includes 2025 (`--season 2025` or none), the home win rate line is withheld until the ncaa-elo-v1r run is recorded. Lifted 2026-10-09: the run record is on main (`v1r_run_recorded`), so the line prints. **The 2026 fence (addendum 86 item 3, Q2, #561):** for NCAA, a listing that includes 2026 (no `--season`, or 2026) prints the home-rate line as withheld (n stays) until the ncaa-elo-v2 run is recorded; the single-game samples stay. |
| `nhl-goalie-sync` | `--start 2023-10-01 --end --sleep 0.25 --refresh --dry-run --verbose --tolerance-hours 12` | NHL-GOALIE (a), 2026-09-30. Maps our NHL matches to api-web.nhle.com games (date ± 12h + both team names; ambiguous = refused) and upserts per-game goalie appearances (starter flag, shots / saves / goals against, TOI, decision) into `nhl_goalie_appearances`. Games outside our DB (e.g. 2023-24) are kept with no match link, as goalie history. Fields are found by the probe's discovery rules, and the receipt prints the keys used (law 1). Creates the table if missing (additive). Idempotent; take the `.backup` first. Ends with the coverage receipt. |
| `nhl-goalie-coverage` | — | NHL-GOALIE (a) receipt, read-only: of our finished NHL games, how many have both starters identified, by season and overall. |
| `nhl-shot-sync` | `--start --end --sleep --refresh --dry-run --verbose --tolerance-hours` | NHL-xG (1) INGEST (#153): shot events (shot on goal / missed / blocked / goal) from api-web play-by-play into `nhl_shot_events`, linked through the goalie-sync mapping (else the matcher; ambiguity refused). Raw values, upsert, never deletes; a refetch never blanks a value. Prints the law-1 key receipt and the shooter-side vs play-owner agreement. Take the `.backup` first. Host-runnable. |
| `nhl-shot-coverage` | — | NHL-xG (1) receipt, read-only: shot-event coverage of our finished NHL games by season, field completeness by event type, and P1–P6 against the probe's frozen FEEDABLE bars. |
| `nhl-goalie-audit` | `--start 2023-10-01 --end --sleep 0.25 --limit 8 --list-ours YYYY` (repeatable) | Read-only (2026-09-30): classifies every NHL API game with no link to our match by cause — `utc_boundary` (same team pair, our clock 12–48h off), `home_away_swapped`, `ambiguous_*`, `name_mismatch`, `unlinked_within_12h` (matchable now), `not_in_our_db` — with the offset histogram, how many a wider window would link uniquely (18/24/36/48h), and our unlinked finished matches by season. Choose `nhl-goalie-sync --tolerance-hours` from this receipt. **`--list-ours 2024 --list-ours 2025` (ruling 2026-10-01):** lists EVERY one of our unlinked finished games by date, with the nearest API game (same pair → swapped → one team, ±7 days), its delta, and a cause named from that evidence: `api_preseason`, `api_game_not_synced`, `utc_offset_beyond_12h`, `linked_to_other_match`, `home_away_swapped`, `one_team_only` or `no_api_game`. The goalie mapping also carries the ruled alias "Utah Hockey Club" ↔ "Utah Mammoth". |
| `python3 scripts/nhl_pbp_probe.py` | `--per-season 20 --seasons 2023 2024 2025 --from-db --sleep --out` | NHL SHOT-QUALITY PROBE (2026-09-30), read-only, stdlib (runs on the host). Samples `/v1/gamecenter/<id>/play-by-play` per season and finds shot events, x/y location, shot type, shooter and situation by discovery (key paths dumped). FEEDABLE / NOT per need (P1–P8) and the reopening line (xG-class data). `--from-db` samples OUR linked games = coverage vs our games. |
| `nhl-backtest --candidate v5` | `--season-start` | NHL-GOALIE (b), 2026-09-30: v1 + the as-of starting-goalie term (a-priori constants in `src/models/nhl_goalie.py`), through the FROZEN NHL gate (train 2024, test 2025, bar = home-rate log-loss − 0.010). Also prints goalie coverage, v1 on the same stream, the goalie information (v1 − v5 log-loss) and RPS for all three. Writes nothing. Needs `nhl-goalie-sync` first. |
| `nhl-backtest --candidate v7` | `--season-start` | NHL-xG v7 (ARCHITECT 2026-10-02): v6 with the xG `na` shot-type level REMOVED (events without shot type take the baseline level), the label-leak correctness fix; nothing else changed. Same bar (0.6866), splits, bands, v1 beside it, RPS. Refused before any data load unless the registry holds `nhl-v7` declared and unrun; records its ONE run. `docs/specs/nhl-xg-v7.md`. |
| `nhl-backtest --candidate v8` | `--season-start` | NHL-xG v8 (ARCHITECT 2026-10-02), the LAST candidate on 2025: v7's xG and Elo with the logistic divisor and k FITTED BY MAXIMUM LIKELIHOOD on 2024 ONLY (walk-forward, declared grid; printed before any 2025 read); home_adv 45.6, regression 0.25; same bar/bands; one recorded run. `docs/specs/nhl-xg-v8.md`. After it, the 2025 test set is RETIRED (registry-enforced). |

**NHL daily (from the 2026-09-29 market-only launch: the 2026-27 opening day; on the host the start is `SP_NHL_ACTIVE_FROM` in host.env):**
`sync-matches --competition NHL --season 2026 --date-from D --date-to D` for
each of D = yesterday, today, tomorrow (single-day calls: the hockey adapter
only honours a date when from == to, so a range pulls the whole season) →
`sync-odds --competition NHL --season 2026` → `sync-kalshi-nhl` →
`export-fixtures --competition NHL`. The Cockpit renders the file as
books' fair bars with a market-only chip and the Kalshi status.

**NBA daily (host `sp-nba-daily`, daily 06:47 UTC; ARCHITECT 2026-11-05, addendum 261 item 1(b); enabled by the architect's block):**
`sync-matches --competition NBA --season 2026 --date-from D --date-to D` for each of D = yesterday, today, tomorrow
(UTC, each its own step, nhl-daily's shape; ARCHITECT 2026-11-06, addendum 274 item 2(3): "THE WINDOW — THREE UTC
DAYS, {yesterday, today, tomorrow}, nhl-daily's own", correcting 271 item 3(d); the basketball adapter honours a date
only when from == to, so a range pulls the whole season) → `sync-kalshi-nba`. The calls (274 item 2(9)): "/leagues +
/games, 6 calls a day, plus the window chain's 4 an hour while games are in its window". At 06:47 UTC, today holds
last night's US evening and yesterday the afternoon tips before it; tomorrow holds tonight's US evening, so its rows
exist for the window chain, which re-reads them hourly. No
coverage step: intl-daily's 07:20 UTC `coverage --json` is the day's one writer (addendum 225 item 5) and reads the
fresh NBA rows. The season's first load is the operator's laptop order (261 item 0): `sync-competitions --sport
basketball` → `sync-teams --competition NBA --season S` → `sync-matches --competition NBA --season S` for S = 2026,
2025, 2024, AFTER `scripts/nba_certification_receipt.py`'s served phase is posted on #839, its stored phase after.

**NCAA men's basketball daily (host `sp-ncaamb-daily`, daily 07:07 UTC; ARCHITECT 2026-11-12, addendum 352 item 3(iii): "A NEW CHAIN, `ncaamb-daily`"; on T11's `TIMERS_BY_BLOCK`, enabled by the architect's block):**
`sync-matches --competition NCAAMB --season 2026 --date-from D --date-to D` for each of D = yesterday, today, tomorrow
(UTC, each its own step, nba-daily's three single-day shape) → `sync-kalshi-ncaamb`. 6 provider calls a day, as
nba-daily's. No coverage step (intl-daily's 07:20 UTC write is the day's one). The season's first load is the
architect's block on the laptop (352 item 4): `sync-competitions --sport basketball` → `sync-teams --competition
NCAAMB --season 2026` → `sync-matches --competition NCAAMB --season 2026`, AFTER
`scripts/nba_certification_receipt.py --competition NCAAMB`'s served phase (exit 0, its team-id stop at 0), its
stored phase after. The 2026-27 season ONLY (352 item 3(iv)).

**Tennis daily (host `sp-tennis-daily`, daily 06:53 UTC; ARCHITECT 2026-11-05, addendum 261 item 2(c): "the chain tennis-daily"; enabled by the architect's block):**
`sync-matches --competition ATP --date-from {yesterday} --date-to {tomorrow}`, the same for WTA → `sync-kalshi-atp` →
`sync-kalshi-wta`. A tennis `sync-matches` is the adapter `kalshi_tennis` (src/adapters/kalshi_tennis.py): every
KXATPMATCH / KXWTAMATCH market under open, closed and settled read to its last page (269 item 3(d)), one fixture per
event (two markets naming two players; anything else refused and counted), the players upserted first from the same
read (players as teams, 261 item 2(a)), then the events dated inside the window written. The result map (269 item
3(b)): 'yes' / 'no' → FINISHED with the winner the 'yes' market's player when EITHER (a) expiration_value is that
player (as built) OR (b) the 'yes' market's expiration_value reads 'Yes' AND the 'no' market's reads 'No'
(case-insensitive after stripping, both must hold; counted RESULT_WORD_ECHO, addendum 296 item 2), any other pair
SCHEDULED and counted WINNER_MISMATCH (a name variant is never matched by spelling), 'scalar' →
SETTLED_SCALAR with no winner and the fraction kept under `external_ids["kalshi_settlement"]`, '' → not settled
(SCHEDULED); anything else SCHEDULED and counted. Scores, sets and a retirement's point are NULL AND LABELLED (269 item
2). THE KICKOFF (addendum 288 item 1, RULED): the event's `occurrence_datetime` as served, UTC, the same instant on both
markets; served on neither, `expected_expiration_time` by the same rule; a difference, a key served on one market only,
or a value that is no ISO instant with an offset REFUSES the event under its own label (counted, never a fixture);
served on no key of either market, the row is stored at 00:00 of the ticker's date at UTC+14 with the label
START_UNREAD (`external_ids["kalshi_kickoff"]`), never captured. The kickoff is rewritten on every sync that reads the
row. A re-served row takes its served state (addendum 288 item 5(b)): an unsettled or changed result replaces the stored
winner and the stored settlement (none served, none kept). The window is required and at most 7 ticker days; `--seasons` is refused (addendum 270 item 5: "no backfill is
scheduled"); `--season`, when given, is the ticker's year. Each run also re-reads, from the same listing, the stored
SCHEDULED, FINISHED and SETTLED_SCALAR rows dated in the 7 ticker days before `--date-from` (addendum 294 item 7: a
settlement that came after the window, or one the venue retracted or re-settled; rows only, none created, no further
call), each written as served and counted ("settled since", "retracted or changed"); with `--season`, a held row of
another ticker year is not re-read (the adapter writes that season's events only) and is named on the line ("not
re-read (outside --season)"), never counted re-read. A held row the re-read's sync does not write (its listing names
a player not stored, or a pair neither the stored one nor its reverse) is not counted re-read and never enters "settled
since" or "retracted or changed": the line names it with its reason, always printed last ("N not re-read (player not
stored / teams refused): <event> (<reason>)"; Codex on #887). An event the listing refuses (and does not also accept: an
event with one stray market outside the series is accepted from its two markets and never labelled) that a stored
row dated in the window or that lookback holds has its refusal label written to the row's
`external_ids["kalshi_kickoff"]` and nothing else (addendum 294 item 13(b): status, winner, settlement receipt and
`utc_date` stay), counted with the match ids; the capture never prices that row until a sync reads the event again.
When the rows labelled this run are every stored row of the tour dated in the window or that lookback, and there is
at least one, the receipt adds the line "EVERY held row refused this run — the venue's serving changed" (addendum
297 item B.11: a line, not a page; the exit is unchanged). No coverage step: intl-daily's 07:20 UTC `coverage --json`
is the one writer. The first load is the operator's laptop order: `sync-competitions --sport tennis`, then
`scripts/tennis_certification_receipt.py`'s served phase posted on #838 BEFORE the first `sync-matches`, its stored
phase after.

**NCAA market (Tue / Wed / Thu / Fri / Sat; laptop and host `sp-ncaa-market`; Tue + Wed added 2026-10-07 for midweek FBS games):**
`sync-matches --competition NCAA --season 2026 --date-from D --date-to D` for
each of D = yesterday, today, tomorrow (single-day calls, as for NHL;
tomorrow because kickoffs at 8pm ET or later fall on the next UTC date,
ARCHITECT 2026-10-07), then `sync-kalshi-ncaa`, then `export-fixtures --competition NCAA`. ARCHITECT
2026-10-02 (#254): the result sync comes first so that games finished since
the last run (Thursday's slate before Friday's export) leave the window
instead of reading SCHEDULED.

**NCAA kickoff freshness (host `sp-ncaa-schedule`, daily 10:35 + 22:35 UTC; ARCHITECT 2026-10-09):**
`sync-matches --competition NCAA --season 2026 --date-from D --date-to D` for each of D = today ..
today+7 (the listing reads: they find games we do not hold), then `refresh-by-id --competition NCAA
--days 7` (addendum 21 item 1: every stored SCHEDULED game in those days refreshed by its own id, because
the listing no longer carries games that have not started). The provider files an unannounced college
kickoff at 04:00Z (midnight US Eastern) and announces the real time days later; this keeps every stored
kickoff in the next seven days at most a day old ("On the host, the stored kickoff of every NCAA game in
the next seven days is at most one day old"). The laptop runs the same listing reads and then the same
`refresh-by-id` in its morning chain (Phase 1, above; addendum 30 item 4: the listing reads first, the refresh
after). **Placeholder re-key (ARCHITECT 2026-10-09, addendum 30 item 3):** "For NCAA, a stored SCHEDULED row at
the provider's TBD kickoff holds its natural key for its whole game day. An unknown incoming id for the same home
and away team, at a real kickoff within 24 hours of that placeholder, re-keys that row. Every refusal of the
12-hour rule applies as it stands: the stored id still in the listing, a row already claimed in the run, more than
one candidate, the pair swapped. A row at a real kickoff keeps the 12-hour window." **AMENDED (addendum 33 item
1):** "The placeholder window is consulted only where the 12-hour rule would create a row: no candidate within 12 hours, same pair or swapped. It never takes a match away from the 12-hour rule. There the same refusals apply among placeholder rows: the stored id still in the listing, a row already claimed in the run, more than one placeholder candidate, the pair swapped." So a doubled game (the placeholder row and a second row at the real kickoff) is
decided by the 12-hour rule, as before: the real-kickoff row is re-keyed, the placeholder row untouched. A forward-week listing read
that carries an announced time under a new id re-keys the 04:00Z row (`rekeyed=`, old id kept in
`<source>_prev` and the `<source>_rekeys` log, via `sync`) instead of creating a second row. The placeholder and
its resolution are one shared definition (`src/ingestion/placeholder.py`, also used by
`scripts/ncaa_rekey_receipt.py`). NFL keeps the 12-hour window. Noted, not ruled: the placeholder is 04:00Z only
until the clocks change on 2026-11-01.

## Prediction, evaluation, improvement

| Command | Options | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `roadmap` | `--today YYYY-MM-DD` | **THE ROADMAP (ARCHITECT 2026-10-13, addendum 59 item 5, #486); read-only, no database, no network.** Per model lane of `docs/roadmap.json`, in the file's order: title, Issue, state, earliest live and the record against the close, then its next step (its first step not done): what, owner, due date and `in N days`, `today` or `OVERDUE N days` (one day is singular: `in 1 day`, `OVERDUE 1 day`, `(+1 more step overdue)`; addendum 61 item 5). Then every milestone of `.github/ledger/taxonomy.json` that has a `due_on`, with the same count, and a last line `overdue: N` (every step not done whose due date is before today, and every milestone past its date). A milestone with no `due_on` key is refused, named (#494). **The base check (addendum 60 item 3, #490):** `python scripts/ledger.py check-roadmap --base REF` runs in CI's `fragments` job and fails a PR that moves a `due` or an `earliest_live` from its base (the file where the PR branched, as check-fragments reads it) without a moved line the head adds (`which` the step's `what` or `earliest_live`, `was` the base's date, `now` the head's; `now` null for a cleared earliest_live, addendum 61 item 4). A first earliest_live set needs none; a lane or step only one side holds needs nothing. Every moved line the base holds stays on the head, word for word and in the base's order; the head only appends, and a line changed or removed fails, named (addendum 62 item 3, #504). Within a lane no two steps share a `what`. **A lane may carry `competitions`** (ARCHITECT 2026-10-17, addendum 111 item 2, #575: "each lane gains `competitions: [codes]` (a data-only roadmap change; I rule it in — the lane text is unchanged)"): a non-empty list of competition codes as the DB stores them, each named once; optional in the validation, not printed, read by `coverage`; the base check compares dates and moved lines only. Today is the machine's local date; `--today` sets it for the tests. Exit 0 whatever is overdue (it never stops a chain); a file that does not validate exits 2 and names the entry. The operator's morning block runs it as its last step. A date is a commitment to the step, never to its outcome: no date moves a bar. |
| `coverage` | `--date YYYY-MM-DD` · `--json` · `--out PATH` | **THE DAY'S COVERAGE (ARCHITECT 2026-10-17 09:30 ET, addendum 107 item C7; amended by addendum 111 items 1-2, #575); read-only: the DB opened mode=ro, no row written, no export written except the --json file, no provider call (SQLite itself may leave its -wal/-shm sidecars beside a WAL file, as for every `ro_connect` reader). The first step of every morning block and the source of the morning page's coverage map** (`deploy/hosting/coverage_map.py`, whose `coverage()` returns the same map as data). It reads the DB, the roadmap and the registry, the watch's schedule inputs (`chains.py`, `closing.py`) and the watch's receipt log (`SP_RECEIPTS` or `logs/receipts.jsonl`), the family slate files in `exports/` (the SLATE line, #590), the shadow exports in `exports/` (each shadow module's `last_calls`; never `exports/host/`) and `odds_snapshots` on a second mode=ro connection (the PAPER line, addendum 146 item 5(a)), plus the settings `closing._family_refusal` and `sp_run.active_from` read (`SP_SKIP_FAMILIES`, a chain's `active_from_env`), nothing else. The day is `--date`, default today in US Eastern; a game belongs to it by its US Eastern date (`closing.ny_date`, as the closing watch dates a first pitch); a postponed, cancelled or stale-orphan game is not counted (`closing.VOID`) and is named as void; a competition whose every game of the day is void has no SLATE line, no NO SLATE FILE gap and no closing group, and its `slate_line` is null in the `--json` file, so the Today tile shows no slate due for it (ARCHITECT 2026-10-20, addendum 146 item 3(c), #568: already so in code, pinned by tests). One line per competition with games that day: the games; the status with where it was read, **in the ruled order (addendum 111 item 2)**: **LIVE** where a closing family covers it (`closing.FAMILIES[fam]['comps']`, the families of `chains.CLOSING_FAMILIES`, C1) and the model its closing chain's predict step writes with resolves (`predict --sport S`: the one production row of S's model family in `model_versions`, `training.MODEL_FAMILIES`, as `_current_production_version` reads it; `predict-nfl`: `nfl_predict.MODEL_VERSION`, which has no `model_versions` row), e.g. `LIVE (closing family SOCCER; production soccer_elo_poisson v22)`; no production row, or more than one, is not LIVE, the reason on the line, and the derivation goes on. **SHADOW** where a roadmap lane names it in its `competitions` (never its prose) and either the lane is `confirming` with its registry entry `confirming`, or, in any other state, a chain in `chains.py` runs a shadow export for it (a step whose command is one of `coverage_map.SHADOW_EXPORTS`, the four cli commands whose rows carry engine `model_shadow`, in a chain whose steps name the competition; an operator-only chain is no schedule, nor is a chain outside its season gate on the day: `sp_run.active_from`, its `active_from_env` setting such as `SP_NHL_ACTIVE_FROM`, else chains.py's date, sp_run skipping it before that date and failing it on a malformed setting; the line then names the gate, `no shadow export scheduled for NHL (chains.py: nhl-daily inactive until <date> [<source>])`): NHL by `nhl-daily`'s `export-nhl-predictions` (its lane is `to declare`), UNL by its confirming lane, SA, FL1, ELC and PD by `soccer-prematch`'s `export-soccer-expansion-shadow` under the declared `leagues` lane; NCAA's declared lane has no shadow export scheduled. The tag is the vN token of the shadow's id (the registry id, or the shadow module's model id), the full id where it has none; for a shadow export whose rows a registry entry per competition labels (`coverage_map.SHADOW_TAGS`: `export-soccer-expansion-shadow`), the tag is that entry's id and `declared <declared_at>` (ARCHITECT 2026-11-03 07:55 ET, addendum 226 item 4(b): "SA, FL1, ELC and PD tagged from the registry entry (the id and "declared 2026-11-02T18:10:00Z"); "shadow v1" goes"). A competition two lanes name (addendum 223 item 2(f) Q2: "a competition in two lanes is the truth") reads the first lane that makes it SHADOW, except that a confirming lane whose entry is not the one the scheduled export binds it to yields the tag to the lane the export's rows are read for and is named beside it on the line (`; beside it, the confirmation: roadmap lane pd: confirming; registry soccer-expansion-v1: confirming`). **MARKET-ONLY** where the code's own sets hold it, imported: `desk_policy.SHADOW_VENUE_COMPS` (the set `_refuse_shadow_league` refuses), `chains.EXPANSION_CODES`, `chains.KALSHI_CAPTURE_ONLY`, `export._MARKET_ONLY_REASON` (NHL, NCAA, UNL) and `cup_coeff.EXAM_COMPS` (CL, EFL, UEL and EL, the adapter's alias for UEL). Both SHADOW and MARKET-ONLY print `MARKET-ONLY · shadow <tag>` (NHL `MARKET-ONLY · shadow v7`, UNL `· shadow v2`, PD `· shadow leagues-pd-v1 · declared 2026-11-02T18:10:00Z`, SA, FL1 and ELC the same from their own entries). **UNKNOWN** only when none applies, with every reason (law 4); a roadmap or registry that cannot be read judges no shadow and the line says so. Then the stored prediction rows by `model_version` with their `computed_at` in ET (`latest …`, or `computed <earliest> to <latest>` when the rows differ, so an old row is never hidden behind a fresh one; the map holds each game's own) and the games without one (`rows: none (none expected: …)` for a SHADOW or MARKET-ONLY competition); the closing groups of the day: **a run done** (addendum 111 item 1: "a run that has run is a fact of the day") prints `T-35 HH:MM ET [match ids] done (run <run_id>, <exit>)`, the ruling's words, the receipt's own `run_id`, `exit` and covers (`closing._attempts`: a refusal or a superseded run is no run); where a start time has several, the latest success where there is one (`closing.closing_state`: a later failed run, an operator's re-run, never hides it), else the latest run; a done start time is never printed as due, and where the watch still forms a group at it the line says what the watch does with it, as the watch decides: `(attempt N of 3: the watch tries again)` only ahead of T-5 with no success and fewer than three attempts, `(inside T-5: no further attempt, the watch records a miss)` inside T-5 (`closing.due`'s inside, `closing.watch`), `(a successful closing: the watch runs it no more)` or `(3 attempts: …)` where `closing._under_lock` supersedes it, then `; not in its covers: <ids>` for the group's games the run's covers do not hold, then the export file the run landed in, the receipt's own `closing_file` (`; closing file exports/<name>`), or `; closing file NOT written (exports/<name>): receipted and notified, not run again` where closing.py's write failed after the page went out (`closing_file_failed`: the run is exit 0 and never run again; only the receipt is read, nothing under exports/); then the groups the watch will run (`closing.groups` on `closing.due`'s own inputs over the family's games of the day at now: the stored games plus the unpaged called-off ones, less the games a closing has finished with, `closing.covered_ids` from the watch's receipts; `started …` first, then `a closing finished with …` for a game whose run is no done line of the day, then by start time `T-35 HH:MM ET [match ids] → exports/closing_<FAMILY>_<start>.json`, `closing.closing_name`, marked `(due now)` from T-35 to T-5, or `inside T-5: no attempt, no file (the watch records a miss)`), `refused here (…)` where this machine refuses the family (`closing._family_refusal`: SP_SKIP_FAMILIES, MLB on the host), `not shown (…)` where the receipts cannot be read, or `none (no closing family covers X)`. Then the gaps in capitals: a LIVE competition with a game that has no row (none for a family this machine refuses), `PL: 4 GAMES, 0 PREDICTION ROWS — soccer-prematch has not run` (the hint names the chain whose `predict` / `predict-nfl` step matches its closing chain's word for word, neither a closing nor a freshen chain; none found, no hint), or `MLB: 2 GAMES, 1 PREDICTION ROW, NONE FOR 50549`; **the SLATE gap (addendum 126 item 2(b), #590)**: a LIVE competition with no slate file, `NFL: 12 GAMES, ROWS 12, NO SLATE FILE` (claimed for a family this machine refuses too: it is the alert to run the block by hand); then one line naming the UNKNOWN competitions, for which no gap is judged; `GAPS: none` when there is none. **THE SLATE LINE (ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 126 item 2(b), #590):** under each LIVE competition's line, `<CODE> SLATE · exports/<file> · exported <ET> · PLAY <p> · PASS <q>`: the file of the family's slate kind (`closing.FAMILIES` export_glob: `mlb_MLB_*.json`, `nfl_predictions_*.json`, `soccer_PL_*.json`; a closing file never matches) that carries the Desk's calls, a row for at least one game of the day, and a desk row for every game of the day UNSTARTED at its `exported_at` (its kickoff after it; ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 133 item 4, item 4: the games it has no row for, started before it, are named, `· started before it: <ids>`, and no gap is claimed for them), **exported on the ET day** (item 5: "An older file never stands as the day's slate, whatever it covers"; a file exported after the day is neither the day's nor an older one); of those, the one holding the most of the day's games, then the newest by its own `exported_at` (a re-export after a kickoff leaves the started game out, and the morning file still holds it); with the day's counts (item 8: the competition's games of the day; any other call kind named, as on the slate page; the rows beyond the day as `· +<k> beyond the day`, a row of a void game of the day counted in neither); else `<CODE> SLATE · NO SLATE FILE — slate run due HH:MM ET` (the slate stage's due time), or, where this machine refuses the family, `— run block block_<MMDD>_<family>_slate.txt`; an older file that would count but for its export day is named first in the brackets, `(newest exports/<file> exported <ET>, not today's)`, and the gap is claimed. With no file the line reads the watch's slate receipts (item 2(c): "the morning page reads them as it reads the closing lines"; kinds `slate` and `slate_miss`, as `closing.slate_state` reads them: a hold, a refusal or a superseded run is no attempt): where the watch runs no slate that day it names the block, `— run block block_<MMDD>_<family>_slate.txt (<why>: the watch runs it no more)`, `<why>` one of `3 attempts, the last failed (<failed>)`, `a successful slate at HH:MM ET (exports/<file>), but no file of the day holds every game of the day`, `missed: every game of the day had started` (only while no game of the day is still unstarted: the watch decides from the schedule, so a game re-dated later into the day is slated on its next tick, and the line keeps the due time with the miss in brackets); else the due time with what the receipts say in brackets, `(attempt k of 3 failed (<failed>): the watch tries again)`, `(missed (<reason>): the watch keeps trying)`, `(held since HH:MM ET (<the closing it waits for>): the slate runs after it)` when the day's newest slate receipt is a hold and the closing it names is one the watch will still run (`closing.closing_open`: a hold for a closing done, started, inside T-5 or out of attempts is stale and not shown; the watch receipts each closing a slate waits for once, so it names the closing waited for now, and `since` is the tick that began that wait), or `(the watch's receipts cannot be read: <error>)`. A file that cannot be parsed, or has no desk calls, is skipped with its reason on the line (`· skipped: <name> (<why>)`), never counted; a file of the glob with no `predictions` list is another kind (a results export, `mlb_MLB_results_<date>.json` or `soccer_PL_results_<date>.json`): no candidate, never listed as skipped (ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 133 item 3(b); `last_calls` passes such a file in silence too); a file whose name dates it more than 8 days before the day, or after the day after, is not read. `coverage()` carries it as `slate` on the competition (file, exported_at, rows, calls, beyond (k), started_before (ids), older ({file, exported_at}, null when a file stands), due, block, refused, skipped; from the receipts attempts, success, last_failed, succeeded, misses, held, done, unread). exports/ is read, never written. Exit 0 whatever the gaps (it never stops a chain); a `--date` that is not a real date or a DB that cannot be read (a missing file is refused, never created; a DATABASE_URL that is not SQLite is refused) exits 2. **The host's writer (#805, ARCHITECT 2026-11-03, addendum 225 item 5, verbatim: "`coverage --json` becomes the LAST step of one daily host chain (intl-daily's tail is the candidate: daily, early, after its syncs; read-only, reads the DB and the registry; its failure never blocks a sync step — Code names the chain and the step's exit handling in the PR body)"):** `coverage --json`, bare (its default `--out`), is the last step of `intl-daily` (`sp-intl-daily.timer`, daily 07:20 UTC), after the cups' `sync-teams` / `sync-matches` pairs, and on no other chain; UNMETERED (`chains.UNMETERED`: it reads, never calls a provider). Its exit is handled as sp_run handles every step's: no step follows it, so its failure blocks no sync and the chain's exit carries it (systemd's OnFailure pages); a failed step before it that stops the chain (a cup's `sync-matches`, an intl step), or a failed daily backup before any step (sp_run then runs no step), leaves that day without a host coverage file; a failed `sync-teams` does not (addendum 207 item 4). **`--json [--out PATH]` (ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 125 item 2(a), #589):** "writes the dict `deploy/hosting/coverage_map.coverage()` already returns (date, now, competitions[], gaps[]; datetimes ISO-8601 Z), under `kind: "coverage_v1"`, plus `roadmap`: the lanes of docs/roadmap.json with their competitions, status and dated ETAs, read as `cli.py roadmap` reads them (no new source of truth; if a lane carries no date it prints "no ETA")." The file: `{"kind": "coverage_v1", "date": "YYYY-MM-DD", "now": ISO Z, "competitions": [...], "gaps": [...], "now_et", "roadmap": [...] | null, "roadmap_unread": null | why}`. Each competition is coverage()'s own entry (`code`, `games`, `void` as [id, status], `status`, `status_label`, `status_from`, `shadow`, `family`, `refused`, `rows` by model with `earliest` / `latest` / `computed` {match id: ISO Z}, `without_rows`, `writers`, `groups` with `start` / `run_at` ISO Z, `state` and the done run's `run_id` / `exit` / `file`, `started`, `finished`, `closing_unread`), every datetime ISO-8601 Z (any field coverage() gains later rides along as it is), plus the line's own words so a reader computes nothing: `line` (its printed line), `games_text`, `rows_text`, `without_rows_text` (null where the line leaves it out), `closing_text`, `closing_tail` (the closing clause's words outside any group's text: `none left to run`, or null where the clause has none; #589 review), `slate_line` (its SLATE line as the console prints it, or null where it has none: neither LIVE nor MARKET-ONLY, a MARKET-ONLY competition's being its PAPER header, addendum 146 item 5(a); #606 item 2: the Today tile shows it first, NO SLATE FILE marked as the gap); per model `computed_et`; per group `run_lead` (`T-35`), `run_at_et` and `text`. Each roadmap lane (`src/roadmap.lanes_data`: `roadmap.load()` and the print's own lines, `when` counted from the coverage day): `lane`, `title`, `issue`, `competitions` (null where the lane carries none), `state`, `earliest_live`, `eta` (`earliest live <date>`, or `no ETA` where earliest_live is null), `next` {what, owner, due, when} (null when every step is done), `overdue`, and the print's `line` and `next_line`; a roadmap that does not validate gives `roadmap: null` and `roadmap_unread` with the print's refusal (law 4). Default `--out exports/coverage_<date>.json` (the coverage day, relative to the working directory as every export is; the exports mirror carries it dated and as `<role>/latest/coverage.json`; the daily compare (the mirror's Action, which compares the newest dated folder both writers hold, never latest/) lists the dated copy as machine state, by presence, never compared, never DIVERGENT, never counted toward or against criterion (a); the latest copy is the same kind and is listed the same way when latest/ folders are compared by hand: #592 RULED, ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 128 item 3); the file is written whole (beside its name, then moved into place), the console prints the same lines with the header saying `one export written (<path>, --json)` and a last line `COVERAGE JSON: <path> written (…)`. The DB is still opened mode=ro, no provider call, exit 0 whatever the gaps; `--out` without `--json` or an `--out` under data/ (the checkout's or the working directory's) exits 2 and writes nothing; so does an `--out` at the top of exports/ (the checkout's or the working directory's: the files the exports mirror pushes) whose mirror kind is not `coverage`, e.g. `exports/today.json` (kind `today`), since the compare's machine-state rule is a kind rule (#592; Codex review on #595): `coverage_<date>.json`, `coverage.json`, a subfolder or any path outside exports/ pass; an `--out` that names no file (`.`, `/`, `..`) exits 2, `COVERAGE REFUSED: --out <p> names no file` (#597 item 1); each rule is judged on the lexical path and on the resolved one, so a symlink already at the name (which the write would replace with a regular file there) never gets a file past the data/ or exports/-kind rule (#597 item 2); a file that cannot be written exits 2 and writes no file (a missing parent folder may be left behind). Without `--json` the output is unchanged, byte for byte (pinned). The Cockpit's Today tab reads the file (tools/cockpit.html, repo copy). **The PAPER line (ARCHITECT 2026-10-20, addendum 146 item 5(a)):** "coverage's SLATE line for a market-only competition carries, per game, the Kalshi mid (two-way, or the three-way mids) and, where a shadow export exists for the day, the shadow's pick and value side with their divergence against the mid, labelled PAPER and never a call." Every competition whose status is MARKET-ONLY (`MARKET-ONLY` or `MARKET-ONLY · shadow <tag>`; no competition is named in the code) with a game of the day that is not void gets, under its line, a header `<CODE> SLATE · PAPER, never a call (market-only: no ledger, no P&L, no position; ARCHITECT 2026-10-20, addendum 146 item 5(a)) · <n> game(s) · Kalshi mid <k> of <n> · shadow <s> of <n>` and one line per game `<CODE> SLATE · PAPER · <id> <away> @ <home> HH:MM ET[ (started)] · <Kalshi> · <shadow>`; LIVE, SHADOW-only and UNKNOWN competitions get none, nor does an all-void day. **The mid:** the board is read from the stored Kalshi market (ML two-way, 1X2 three-way); two-way, `Kalshi mid HOME m / AWAY 1-m (bid, ask, <c>c; captured <ET>)`, `close.close_from_kalshi`'s own (the HOME leg's last two-sided quote before kickoff within the 2c cap; an older quote is never substituted); three-way, `Kalshi mids HOME / DRAW / AWAY (raw per leg, not normalized, display only; sum s; captured …)`, each leg under the same filters, `—` for a leg with none (`sum —`), never a close or a reference; else `Kalshi: no quote stored`, `Kalshi: no mid (no two-sided pre-kickoff quote within the 2c cap)`, `Kalshi: cannot be read (a capture time cannot be read): not shown` (a game left with no mid where a stored quote's captured_at cannot be parsed: that quote is no quote before kickoff, and the line never says "no two-sided quote" of it), `Kalshi: mismatched legs (<events>): no mid (addendum 37 item 3)` (each side's newest legs, then the row(s) the mid reads judged together with them, `paper.close_row_mismatched`: the two-way mid's HOME row(s), each three-way leg's row(s); a row of another event, or of one whose current legs sit on another game, shows no mid; #641 item 1 RULED, ARCHITECT 2026-10-21, addendum 152 item 4(a)), `Kalshi: ML and 1X2 rows both stored: not read` or `Kalshi: not read (<why>)` (a DB with no odds_snapshots table). **The shadow:** the last row any shadow export wrote before kickoff (each shadow module's own `last_calls` over exports/, any export day; matched to the game by match id and teams, and its recorded kickoff (`utc_date`) to the game's current start by the re-dated rule, `redated.redated_label`: more than `desk_policy.DESK_CLOCK_DRIFT` apart is another kickoff; Codex round 1 on #640, a reading for the architect), `shadow <model_version> (<gate_verdict>; exported <ET>): pick SIDE p (±d pp vs mid); value side SIDE (±d pp)`: the value side is the side with the largest model p − mid above zero (the pick's own side eligible; `none` when no side is above its mid), judged at the printed precision (a side less than 0.05pp above its mid prints +0.0pp and is none: `value side none (no side above its mid by 0.05pp or more, the printed precision)`, a reading for the architect); on a three-way prediction judged only with all three legs priced; a prediction whose legs are not the board's (a two-way prediction on a three-way board, or the reverse) is never judged and carries no divergence: `pick SIDE p (a two-way prediction on a three-way board: no divergence); value side not judged (a two-way prediction on a three-way board)`; the divergence (model p − mid) × 100; `shadow: none (no shadow row before kickoff in exports/)`, a row whose teams are not the game's or a game two shadow exports hold is not shown; `shadow: the row is for another kickoff (re-dated): not shown`, `shadow: the row's kickoff cannot be read: not shown`, `shadow: the row carries no pick: not shown`, `shadow: its exported_at cannot be read: not shown`, `shadow: exported at or after the current kickoff (an in-play row): not shown` (a row `last_calls` keeps, written before its recorded kickoff, but exported at or after the game's current start, compared as UTC; Codex round 2 on #640) and `shadow: the row cannot be read: not shown` (a prediction that is not an object, a probability stored that is not a real finite number, or a top pick that is not text), and `shadow not read (<module>: <Error>)` in the header where a shadow module's files cannot be read, on that module's competitions only (`paper.shadow_comps`, read from each module's own code), never hiding another module's rows. Whatever one game's Kalshi or shadow part raises reads `Kalshi: cannot be read (<Error>): not shown` or `shadow: cannot be read (<Error>): not shown` for that game only; every other line and the GAPS print whole (exit 0). PAPER never claims a gap: `slate` stays null for these competitions; `coverage()` carries `paper` (label, ruling, kalshi_unread, shadow_unread, games[] with board, kalshi, shadow and why there is none), and the `--json` file adds `paper_lines` (the lines as printed) and `slate_line` = the header. The Kalshi quotes are read on a second mode=ro connection (SELECTs, plus the legs guard's read-only `PRAGMA table_info(odds_snapshots)`), only when a MARKET-ONLY competition has games; the header line now names `the slate and shadow files in exports/`. Nothing from the PAPER lane enters the ledger, the P&L, a Cockpit position, the Desk's call, or any prediction, fixture, shadow or closing export. 5(a) appears on coverage's console and in its coverage_v1 `--json` file, which the compare treats as machine state (`deploy/hosting/compare_exports.py:97`); the Cockpit's Today tile shows its header as `slate_line`. 5(b), the close's record, appears in RESULTS.md and on results-tally's console (the results-tally row). **The NBA's coverage basis (ARCHITECT 2026-11-06, addendum 272 item 3: "the coverage page's totals count SCHEDULED + FINISHED + POSTPONED, not CANCELLED"):** for a competition in `paper.COVERAGE_COUNTS_POSTPONED` (the NBA only) a POSTPONED game counts in its line's games, not among its void; CANCELLED stays void (`+N void, not counted`), the FT-twin CANC listings with it; every other competition keeps `closing.VOID` as it stands. The coverage day is the US Eastern day of the stored kickoff, the day a Kalshi ticker's date names (272 item 4). |

(6) Kalshi legs captured before 2026-10-14T04:00:00Z (2026-10-14 00:00 ET; #475's switch to the shared pair) never enter the grade (`legs_before_2026_10_14`), the close nor its legs guard: addendum 239 item 4's fallback, since the repo record names no deploy instant for the tag carrying #475 and v1.4.0's release Issue #556 names none (read: "Not deployed yet", no later deploy record; the console says why). The headline's pick-vs-close and value-side are labelled `reference (book close, never THE close)`. |

By sport it prints ladders, complete and two-sided counts (two-sided = 0 < bid ≤ ask < 1 on every leg), and spreads (median, p90, max, 1c share). It also prints the fee-clear rate (the Desk's K2 rule, (reference p − cost) ≥ 4pp) at taker and maker cost, from `venue.kalshi_exec`, on two references that are never pooled: the live model's latest prediction at or before the capture, on its pick leg (MLB/NFL/PL), and the venue engine's book reference (≥ 4 books, ≤ 3h old), on the best leg. **Fills** (`--ledger`, the Cockpit's "Export ledger (JSON)"): fill books, realised P&L and the opening-fee class. Executed-position CLV comes from the Cockpit's own executedPositions, ported line for line (`src/walters/ledger_fills.py`; parity `scripts/ledger_fills_parity_verify.py`). **Call-to-fill:** exactly one of UNAVAILABLE / UNATTEMPTED / ATTEMPTED_UNFILLED / MATCHED / UNKNOWN per eligible call (real straight/ladder, units > 0, in the window). The ledger carries no order records, so a recorded quote with no fill is UNKNOWN, never inferred. `--out` refuses `data/`. **Void: re-dated (addendum 106 item 4; #574):** a call the Cockpit voided as re-dated (`void_redated`) is never eligible and never an executed position; the reconciliation prints `void: re-dated N · placeholder kickoff M` (a call the Cockpit voided as a placeholder kickoff, addendum 109 item 2, is the same void) and names each call that would otherwise have been eligible (a void shadow or parlay leg was never in it), and a fill matched to one is counted apart, neither in nor outside the window (`on a void call N`). **Legacy join (addendum 109 item 2):** the line after the void calls, `legacy join N of the eligible calls`, counts the calls the Cockpit paired with their result by the ±1-day team join (`legacy_join`: no match id, logged before the field). Fills carry no match id, so a fill is still fitted to its call by game, side and day. |

Then the games' count, the files with no odds and any unreadable file. Read-only. |

| Desk ORDER LINE (`desk.order`) | the day's unit, `logs/desk_unit_<ET date>.json` (`desk-unit`); floor `SP_UNIT_FLOOR_USD` (set by hand) | **ARCHITECT 2026-10-04, no policy change.** Every PLAY / LADDER row, every eligible VENUE row (market-only) and every `desk-parlays` ticket leg carries `desk.order` {ticker, side, limit, limit_basis, contracts, unit, text, why}, e.g. `BUY YES KXNFLGAME-…-SEA @ 0.57 × 10`, so placing an order is copy-exact, never a lookup. Side: BUY YES on the pick's leg. LADDER (an away pick on a 3-way market) = BUY NO on HOME (NO bid = 1 − YES ask). A two-way market with no leg for the pick falls back to NO on the opponent; a three-way never does. A DRAW ladder is refused ("write it by hand"). Limit per doctrine (**#87 v1.1, ARCHITECT-RULE 2026-10-06**, superseding join-bid): TAKE at the ask; join at bid + 1c only when the spread is ≥ 3c; no ask = no limit (refused). The executable cost (ask + taker fee, per fill of the order's OWN contract count) sizes PLAYs and gates VENUE calls (`desk.exec`). Pre-addendum files and `base_v11()` keep the old rule (join the bid; take at ≤ 1c). **Contracts (Desk policy v1.2, ARCHITECT 2026-10-30, addendum 179, #732; the operator's ruling):** "a unit is 8% of the money pool, growing and shrinking with it": `UNIT_FRACTION = 0.08`, `HALF_UNIT_FRACTION = 0.04` (the half-unit cohort, 0.5u, by arithmetic; the architect advised 2–3% and the operator kept 8%). Contracts = floor(units × the day's unit in dollars / limit), "as today, on the day's unit". The day's unit is floor(UNIT_FRACTION × pool) in whole dollars, then the floor `SP_UNIT_FLOOR_USD` (the unit is never below it; set by hand in host.env and the laptop's `.env`, its value never in the repo; half of a unit must buy one contract at 0.99, else the day is unread; unset: no floor applied, said on the `desk-unit` line). It is read ONCE per ET day (`desk-unit`, below) and every page that day reads the file; `unit` stamps it as before, `1u = $<n>`, and `desk_meta.unit` carries {state, et_date, label, why, fraction, half_fraction, floor_name}. **Unread** (a failed read, a day with no file yet: yesterday's is never read, a file that cannot be read, a unit folder under `data/`): no order line, `why` = `unit unread — no order` verbatim, `unit` = `unit unread`; the exec gate then prices its fee at the reference fill (10 contracts per 1u). That is NOT neutral (#732 judgement J1, for the architect): the taker fee rounds per fill of the order's own count (#87 v1.1 (h)), so on a read day it rounds at units × the day's unit / limit, and a borderline row's exec edge, and with it its tier and units, can differ between a read day and an unread one (a 1u PLAY near 4pp exec can read as a 0.5u PLAY on the day's unit); which basis is right is the architect's ruling; the unread day's `desk.exec` / `desk.ladder_exec` contracts carry `contracts_basis` "reference fill, no order (unit unread)". `SP_UNIT_USD` is retired from the Desk's sizing (nothing reads it; a file exported before v1.2 carries its own basis in `unit`, which `desk-rescore` reads). **Depth (item 7, informational): UNWIRED.** No orderbook read exists (item 7's "the orderbook read the Desk already makes" does not hold), the `/markets` listing's level sizes are not stored, and no depth field's key name has been read from a real response (law 1), so `order_line` reads no depth from any leg and no field name is guessed. `desk_policy.depth_note` holds the line's text, "size over the ask's depth: N contracts" (N = the depth at the ask, TAKE orders), and the closing page passes an order's `depth_note` through if an export ever writes one; none does. Wiring it is for the architect on #732 (needs-ruling). Fewer than one contract is refused. The ticker is the one `sync-kalshi-*` resolved, stored in `odds_snapshots.market_ticker` (latest pre-kickoff snapshot per leg, carried on rows as `kalshi_legs`). **Run `migrate_kalshi_ticker.py` once, after a `.backup`**; until then, and until the next sync, the order says `order: no Kalshi ticker on file …`. A refusal is always a `why`, never a guess. The Cockpit renders the file's text verbatim. Regression: `tests/test_desk_order_line.py`, `python3 scripts/cockpit_order_line_verify.py`. |
| `desk-unit` | — | **THE DAY'S UNIT (ARCHITECT 2026-10-30, addendum 179 items 2-5, #732; Desk policy v1.2).** Reads the money pool, "the Kalshi account's value — cash balance plus open positions at cost", ONCE per ET day: floor(UNIT_FRACTION × pool) in whole dollars, then the floor `SP_UNIT_FLOOR_USD`, written once, by the first page whose read succeeds, to `logs/desk_unit_<ET date>.json` (folder `SP_DESK_UNIT_DIR`; a `data/` segment is refused, exit 2); a later call that day, a retried slate or another process reads the file and never the pool, and the unit never changes inside a day. Every slate and closing stage on the host runs it before its steps (`closing.py` `read_day_unit`; its exit never fails the slate, its receipt keeps `unit` {state, et_date, new, exit}, never a figure); the laptop's morning block runs it for the record (its own call), and the laptop's pages read its file. A read that fails (no Kalshi key, a non-200 answer, a timeout, a missing key, an amount that is not a fixed-point `_dollars` string, a missing cursor key, more than 20 pages, a pool of zero) leaves that page **unread**: it carries no order line and says `unit unread — no order` (the slate page on a line of its own; a closing page in place of each PLAY's order), and nothing is written, never a zero unit and never the floor (addendum 180 item 5). **Every page reads (addendum 180 item 4):** a slate or a closing that finds no day file runs `desk-unit` itself before its export; a failed read writes nothing, so that page carries no order and the next page reads again; the first successful read writes the day's file, which stands. **The floor (addendum 182 item 3):** a floor above the fraction's unit but within the pool applies as the operator's hand-set minimum and says `the floor binds: the unit is above UNIT_FRACTION of the pool` on the `desk-unit` line, by name and never a figure; a floor above floor(pool) leaves the page unread, `floor above the pool`: the Desk never sizes a whole pool on one call. **The pool (addenda 186 and 187, on #732's names):** `balance_dollars` (GET /portfolio/balance, no `exchange_index`: the total across the indexes) + Σ `market_positions[].market_exposure_dollars` ("Cost of the aggregate market position in dollars") over every page of GET /portfolio/positions (`count_filter=position`, `limit` 100, the cursor until empty; past 20 pages the day is unread). Every amount is a `_dollars` string read as Decimal; `portfolio_value`, `balance_breakdown` and `event_positions` are never read. No Kalshi key configured: unread. GET only, through `scripts/kalshi_trade_api_probe.py`'s client (`KALSHI_API_KEY_ID` / `KALSHI_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH`). Prints the fractions, the floor's name and the state; the pool and the unit in dollars only where the role as the process was started with it (`config.PROCESS_ENV_ROLE`, read before `.env` is loaded: addendum 183 item 2(1), "never from a file the code opens"; no role, no figure) is `laptop` (item 5: never in a host log, a receipt, an Issue or the repo). The day file keeps the day's unit (item 3) on every machine and the pool's dollar value only where the role is `laptop` (`pool_usd` null elsewhere). The laptop and the host each read their own pool at their own time, so on a read day their order lines' `unit`, `contracts` and `text` and `desk_meta.unit.label` can differ, and `compare_exports` prints those values: a compare pasted into an Issue or PR would carry a dollar unit (J6, ruled by addendum 182 item 4: each machine reads its own pool; compare_exports masks the unit-bearing fields in its own PR, merged before v1.6.2's compare morning). Ends `DESK-UNIT state=<read|unread> et_date=<date> new=<yes|no>`. Exit 0 otherwise. Regression: `tests/test_desk_unit_fraction.py`. Spec: `docs/specs/desk-policy-v1.2.md`. |
| Desk LADDER exec (`desk.ladder_exec`) | — | **ARCHITECT 2026-10-26 12:05 ET (#689), informational: "it changes no call, no units, no floor."** A LADDER row (away pick, three-way board) orders BUY NO on the HOME contract, and its `desk.exec` stays null by design. `desk.ladder_exec` prices that ordered leg: `model_p` (the model's draw + away), `market_p` (the books' fair draw + away), the NO `bid` / `ask` (mirrored from the HOME leg), `cost` (the NO ask plus the taker fee at the order's own contract count, basis taker), `contracts`, `edge_pp` = (model_p − cost) × 100 and `fee_clears`. A draw pick (1X or X2: no single contract), an unticketed HOME leg or a missing ask gives `why` and no price. Written only with the #87 addendum on, so the golden is untouched. `results-tally`'s EXEC COHORTS section counts the LADDER rows by this edge on a priced-but-ungraded line: no record and no units, never pooled with PLAY. |
| `mlb-time-audit` | `--season` (default 2026) | **#187, READ-ONLY, laptop** (statsapi 406s the host). For every postseason game (gameType F/D/L/W) it shows statsapi vs api-sports start times, statsapi's `startTimeTBD`, the delta, and the api-sports placeholder signature (UTC times of day among mismatches). Ends with an `MLB-TIME-AUDIT` JSON line. No DB, no writes. Since #187 the api-sports fallback never overwrites a statsapi-sourced start time (disagreements show in its receipt as `TIME CONFLICT`), and the window card flags MLB times that statsapi has not confirmed: "⚠ time unconfirmed". |
| `export-results` | `--sport --competition --date --days N` | Graded results file (top-pick, totals pulse annotations). **`--days N` (ARCHITECT 2026-11-08, addendum 294 item D, #883):** "For each slate day in the N days ending yesterday, it writes that day's `<sport>_<comp>_results_<day>.json` when the day has at least one graded game. Yesterday's file is always written, as today." Default 1, today's behaviour, byte for byte; each day is its own 08:00-08:00 UTC slate window and its own file name, rewritten in place; oldest first, yesterday last; a day of the window with no graded game other than yesterday writes nothing; the console ends `results window: ...` naming the days written, not written and failed. Above 1, `--date` and `--out` refuse (exit 2, nothing written). soccer-morning-after passes `--days 7` (its timer is Sun/Mon/Tue: a Friday game has no next-morning run); mlb-morning and soccer-refresh stay bare. **`close_minutes_before_start` (ARCHITECT addendum 31 item 3, #382; additive):** every graded row that carries `close_at` also carries it: whole minutes, rounded down, from the close's capture (`close_at`) to the stored start; null where there is no close. It is the age of OUR CAPTURE, not of the quote (#348): the provider's quote time is not stored. **The record block (addendum 63 item 3; additive):** `record` = `games`, `hits` (the file's own slate day; every row is decided) and `vs_close` over the decided games that have a close: the games, the model's log-loss, the close's, close minus model, its bootstrap 95% interval (10,000 resamples, seed 20261013) and the count of games the model beat the close (one helper, `src/walters/vs_close.py`; "A win rate is never printed without it"), plus `kalshi_only_closes` (how many of those closes were a Kalshi mid: the Kalshi-only close, or the stale book close's, ARCHITECT 2026-10-19, addendum 137 item 4: "the `kalshi_only_closes` count in the record block already exists for this"); the console prints it under the top-pick line. Every row also carries `close_book_stamp_age_h`: hours to two places from a soccer book close's provider stamp (`response[].update`, stored since #615, addendum 147 item 1; a stamp more than 24 h before kickoff keeps the three-way book close and adds `close_stale_unswitched`); null for MLB, whose odds answer carries no update field (#628), and wherever there is no close or the stamp is unknown (rows synced before #615 included, no backfill). **An unknown competition code refuses (addendum 64 item 3, #511):** "export-results with a competition code it does not know refuses, exit 2, naming the code; it never falls back to every row." Nothing is written. **Void: re-dated (ARCHITECT 2026-10-17, addendum 106 item 4; #574):** a row whose match is void by its closing rows in `exports/closing_*.json` (`src/walters/redated.py`: no closing row within `DESK_CLOCK_DRIFT`, 1h, of the match's current `utc_date`, and the latest more than 1h from it; exactly 1h is not void, a start with no clock is not compared) keeps every field and gains `redated`, "void: re-dated <old start> → <new start>", and leaves every tally: `record` (games, hits, `vs_close`) and `totals_pulse`. `record.redated` counts them; the console prints `void: re-dated N · placeholder kickoff M` and one line per row. A latest closing row recorded at the provider's 04:00Z placeholder (`src/ingestion/placeholder.py` `is_placeholder_kickoff`) reads "void: placeholder kickoff" instead, the same void (addendum 109 item 2 Q2); `redated` and `record.redated` carry either label. The closing rows are read by match id, never by a team or day join. The label is never under `status` or `void`, so the Cockpit still grades the fresh call from the row. A match no closing file names, or one with a closing at its current start, reads as before. **`model_close_divergence` (ARCHITECT 2026-10-19, addendum 137 item 3, #612; an export-contract change):** every graded row carries `graded.model_close_divergence`: model p minus close fair on the model's pick, a fraction (0.0453 = +4.53pp), NOT a closing-line value (close minus entry is the Cockpit P&L's, on executed fills). **Its alias `graded.clv` is REMOVED (#621):** ruled to stay through v1.5.0 and go in the release after; it shipped through v1.6.2 and is gone from the next release (its own changelog line); a reader of the graded block reads `model_close_divergence`. Here it is the STORED value (`prediction_outcomes.clv`), whose close is VERIFIED or RETAINED-LEGACY in RESULTS.md, not always this row's own arithmetic. **`graded_export` (ARCHITECT 2026-11-01, addendum 196 item 4(i), built by addendum 200 item 2, #755; additive, read-side naming only):** every row carries `graded_export` = `{kind, file, run_id, exported_at}`, the export file whose row the results row stands behind: the grade itself is the database's (prediction_outcomes), no export file is opened to grade it. The closing row stands over the slate row (#632's rule): `kind` `closing` = the match's row in `exports/closing_*.json` (`redated.closing_rows`; host/ never read), the newest run's row within `DESK_CLOCK_DRIFT` of the match's current start, else the newest (the one a re-dated label reads), `run_id` its closing block's `run_id`, `exported_at` its top-level `exported_at`; `slate` only where no closing file names the match = the newest Desk slate export (`exports/<sport>_*.json` with `predictions`, `desk_meta`, no `closing` block, its `sport` the results'), the same selection by its `exported_at`, `run_id` null (a slate file carries no run id: law 4); none = every key null. Values as the file holds them. The payload's `graded_exports` counts the rows by kind and names a file that could not be read; the console prints `graded export: closing N · slate M · none K`. No grade, threshold or Desk rule moves (`src/walters/graded_export.py`). |
