PAPER, never a call; one console line, each close kind apart. |
| `window-card` | `--hours --t90` | Next-24h consolidated card → `exports/window_24h.json`: every game in the window, kickoff-sorted, fixtures grammar + model p / edge / tier / quarantine / venue flag / engine. Model fields copied from the canonical exports (no model runs); market side repriced. Read-only against the DB. **Line-move alarm** (2026-09-29): inside T-3h, a net move ≥ 6pp in the home probability on the book consensus or Kalshi (from stored snapshots, no provider calls) sets `late_news_flag: "late-news?"` with the per-venue `line_move`; the window pager pages it and requests `freshen:<family>`. `export-nfl-predictions` carries the same two fields. Its rows carry the fixtures grammar's `market.source_updated_at` and the book `line_move` source times, as in `export-fixtures` / `export-nfl-predictions`. **QB news by provider id (ARCHITECT 2026-10-17, addendum 112 item 10, #458: "key by provider player id, name as display only"):** each row's `qb_news` item carries `player_id`, the injuries row's stored provider id (null where the row has none: stored before `migrate_injury_player_source_id.py` or the next `sync-injuries`, or an entry the feed listed with no id). The window pager keys a QB by his team id and it, `id:<team_id>|<id>` (ARCHITECT 2026-10-17, addendum 117 item 3: "the key is (team id, provider player id)"), so two same-name quarterbacks on one team are two players and a status change in either pages, and one id on two teams' reports in one window (a trade, a provider error) is two entries, each paged on his own team's games. An item with no id is keyed by team and name, as every item was before, and its page line says `(no provider id)`; items with no id that share a team and name are one entry holding every status (`(no provider id, N entries)`), never one overwriting another. A change of key is not news: a player keyed by name in the previous state and by id now is carried to his id; where two or more ids share the old name key, that name makes no delta on that run only when the ids' statuses are the name entry's (a baseline; receipt `qb_rekey`: `carried`, `baseline`); otherwise the ids fold into one name-level line, `(N entries)`, and it pages. The same holds the other way round. `source_updated_at` (ARCHITECT 2026-10-09, addendum 32 items 3-4) is the provider's own time for a game's odds (american football's and api-football's `response[].update`, naive UTC, the same form as the capture time beside it; api-football's since #615, addendum 147 item 1): the newest that any quote in the answer can be; a quote may be older; never the time of a quote. Where a session's rows differ, the oldest. null = unknown (every source whose answer carries no update time: MLB's api-baseball (#628) and hockey; rows written before `migrate_odds_source_updated_at.py`; a row without it), and unknown is never read as fresh. |

## Diagnostics & research

| Command | Options | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `availability` | `--sport S` · `--as-of INSTANT` · `--clock recorded\|source` · `--match-id N` \| `--team T` · `--kind K` · `--history` · `--db PATH` | **THE AVAILABILITY RECORD'S QUERY (ARCHITECT 2026-11-04 13:05 ET, addendum 252 item 2; lane availability-record); read-only: the DB opened mode=ro (`--db`: a .backup copy, mode=ro + immutable=1, the standing `--db` fence), no row written, no provider call.** It "prints the facts in force at the instant under the chosen clock, each with both stamps and its observation; `--history` prints every assertion for the key in time order so a flip shows" (252 item 2), through `src/availability/record.py` `as_of` / `history`, the one read path ("there is no second path"). `--as-of` is ISO-8601 with `Z` or an offset (a time with neither is refused). `--clock recorded` (the default) is our fetch time, what we had on disk: a backfilled row never shows before its fetch; `--clock source` is the source's own stamp, and an assertion with none (every NHL landing row) is not read and is counted. A fact is in force from its assertion until a later assertion of the same source asserts the same (kind, player, team[, match]) differently or gives its slot to someone else (the derived interval, 252 item 1(b)), and a load of the nflverse file closes every fact of each team it holds that it does not restate, a partial team group (fewer than half the team's previous complete group) closing nothing (the snapshot closure, 259 item 2(a)-(b)); the player and team are the source's own identifiers (its id, else the name; its team text), so a mapping that lands between fetches never splits a fact. Every line, the as-of lines and the `--history` lines alike, carries `BACKFILLED` (recorded more than the source's cadence after its stamp: source-stamped backtests only) and `POST HOC` (recorded at or after its game's stored kickoff; `eventual_starter` always) where they apply: a `--history` line labels its own assertion; an as-of fact reads `BACKFILLED` only while every row of its interval is backfilled, and a fact first seen in backfilled history and restated by a fresh row prints `live-era from <recorded_at>` instead; an unmapped player or team is printed `UNMAPPED` with the source's id and text (law 4). `--team` is our team id (digits) or the source's team text verbatim; `--match-id` and `--team` are exclusive. Prints "Depth charts: nflverse (CC BY 4.0)" whenever an nflverse fact is shown (252 item 3(a)). Exit 0 ("no facts" included); a missing DB, an unmigrated store (`migrate_availability_record.py` not run) or a refused argument exits 2. The store itself: `availability_observations` and `availability_assertions`, append-only (SQLite triggers abort every UPDATE and DELETE), written only by the feeds' ingest commands (`availability-nflverse`; `availability-nhl-observe`); spec `docs/specs/availability-record.md`. |
| `availability-graph-rebuild` | `--backup-dir DIR` \| `--db PATH` · `--out PATH` · `--max-age-hours H` | **THE AVAILABILITY RECORD'S GRAPH PROJECTION (ARCHITECT 2026-11-04 13:05 ET, addendum 252 item 4; 2026-11-11 19:50 ET, addendum 347 item 5: LadybugDB, the package `ladybug` 0.22.3, HOST-ONLY; lane availability-record): rebuilt whole from a .backup copy opened mode=ro + immutable=1, never the live file; read by nothing (347 item 5(c)).** `--backup-dir`: the newest daily copy (`sports_YYYY-MM-DD[_HHMMSS].db`) whose `.sha256` sidecar matches, refused when older than `--max-age-hours` (26); `--db`: that copy (the standing `--db` fence). The read is `record.walk`, the record's own derivation (`history`'s partitions and `_derive`) under both clocks. Nodes `Player`, `Team`, `Game`, `Observation` (keyed by the record's own identities, our ids as columns) and one `Build`; an edge `FACT` (Player to Team) per assertion carrying its kind, `recorded_at`, `source_stamp`, its game and attrs, and its derived interval under each clock (`rec_*`; `src_*` NULL without a source stamp), `backfilled` and `post_hoc`; `ASSERTED` (Observation to Player), `IN_GAME` (Team to Game), `ABOUT` (Observation to Game). `--out` (default `logs/availability/graph/availability.lbug`) is a `.lbug` file in an existing, writable directory, never under data/; the graph is built beside it, counted read-only and moved onto it whole, one run at a time, and one line of `rebuild.jsonl` beside it records every run (ok, refused or error; a refused `--out` writes nothing at all). The engine is installed on the host only, from `requirements-graph.txt` (`--require-hashes`); without it the command refuses. Exit 0 built; 2 refused (no engine, not 0.22.3, or its native module not importing; no fresh verified copy; the fence; a copy not readable as SQLite; the store not migrated; a bad `--out`; another rebuild running), nothing written but that line; 1 a failed build, the previous graph untouched. The host runs it daily at 10:20 UTC as its own sandboxed unit `sp-availability-graph` (enabled only by the architect's block). Spec: `docs/specs/availability-record.md` section 11. |
| `availability-nflverse` | `--season YEAR` · `--file PATH` | **THE NFLVERSE DEPTH-CHART FEED (ARCHITECT 2026-11-04 13:05 ET, addendum 252 item 3(a); 259 item 2; lane availability-record): one observation of source `nflverse_depth_charts` written to the availability record; the host runs it daily at 08:45 UTC as the chain `availability-daily` (`sp-availability-daily.timer`, enabled only by the architect's block, 252 item 7).** It fetches the season's release file (`https://github.com/nflverse/nflverse-data/releases/download/depth_charts/depth_charts_<season>.csv`; redirects followed; timeouts 10 s connect, 120 s per read; `If-None-Match` with the ETag stored beside the last kept payload, 259 item 2(e)) or reads `--file` (a hand-carried release file; `--season` is not read). The header must be exactly the twelve columns of the 2025+ dictionary (`dt, team, player_name, espn_id, gsis_id, pos_grp_id, pos_grp, pos_id, pos_name, pos_abb, pos_slot, pos_rank`), read by name; anything else is refused with its reason, the 2023-2024 weekly shape named (and `--season` below 2025 refused). Each row whose `dt` is later than the newest `source_stamp` the record holds for the source becomes one `depth_rank` assertion (attrs `pos_grp`, `pos_abb`, `pos_slot`, `pos_rank` verbatim, plus `dt_raw`, `espn_id`, `gsis_id`; `source_player_id` = `espn_id` ONLY, one id that does not move so a `gsis_id` filled in later is never a flip, and a row with a blank `espn_id` is never asserted (never keyed by `gsis_id` or name): counted, its (dt, team) group `partial`, closing nothing (the observation's status keeps 254 item 2(7)'s meaning: a team or match unmapped) (addendum 262 item 5, #840); `player_id` NULL, counted; `source_stamp` = `dt`); older rows are counted, never asserted again, so the 2025 file is read BEFORE the first 2026 read (259 item 2(c): in scope; hosting-h1.md's recipe requires it first). A file whose every load is older than that watermark is REFUSED naming both instants (a `--file` exits 2, nothing written; a fetch is `failed`, exit 1), one whose newest load is the watermark says "no new load", and a row stamped later than our fetch plus one hour refuses its file naming the line (within the hour it is accepted and the receipt prints the skew, 262 item 3). A `--file` is copied ONCE to a temporary file, and that copy is hashed, parsed and kept (262 item 6), so a file changed during the run cannot split the hash, the rows and the kept bytes. The team maps to our `teams.id` where exactly one team of the NFL competition holds the file's code as its `tla`; else NULL with the text, counted, and the observation is `partial`. A 304, or a byte-identical file, is `complete` with 0 rows and the previous payload's sha256; a byte-identical 200 carrying a new ETag stores it beside the kept payload; a changed file is kept under `logs/availability/nflverse_depth_charts/<date>/` (never data/); a raised call, another status or a refused fetched file is `failed` (error redacted: every refusal, a fetched file's or one that ends the run, scrubbed before its 256-character cut, before it is printed or kept, Codex on #841 round 2; exit 1; a 5xx or 429 worded as requests words it, so the chain's runner retries it like a connection error, each attempt its own observation). The receipt: observation id, status, `fetched_at` (a 200's is our clock AFTER its body has streamed and been kept, never the headers' arrival; a 304's the answer's; a failure's the raise or the headers' arrival: Codex on #841 round 2), rows read, loads (new), new rows, rows asserted, older rows with the watermark, `partial team groups: N, closing nothing` (259 item 2(b)), rows with no `espn_id` (refused), unmapped teams (their text scrubbed) and players, backfilled rows, the clock skew where the newest load is ahead of our fetch, the payload sha256 prefix and path, the ETag line, then "Depth charts: nflverse (CC BY 4.0)". No `--db`: an ingest writes the live DB only (254 item 2(9)'s `--db` is the query command's). Holds the chains' DB lock; a missing DB or an unmigrated store exits 2 before any call. Spec: `docs/specs/availability-record.md` section 9. |
| `availability-nhl-observe` | `--dry-run` | **THE NHL LANDING FEED'S WATCH (ARCHITECT 2026-11-04 13:05 ET, addendum 252 item 3(b); 258 item 2(2), #835 item 2; 2026-11-11, addenda 340 and 341 item 2; lane availability-record): ONE TICK; the host runs it every five minutes, hours 00-03 and 06-23 UTC, as its own unit `sp-availability-observe` (not a chain; enabled only by the architect's block).** It reads the NHL schedule (`https://api-web.nhle.com/v1/schedule/<NHL day>`, parsed by `nhl_goalies.schedule_games`; the NHL day [date 10:00Z, date+1 10:00Z), game types 2 and 3) at most hourly per NHL day (a failed read waits the hour too): support data, NO row; the answer kept under `logs/availability/nhl_api_landing/<NHL day>/` when its bytes changed, `schedule.checked` beside it. Per game starting within six hours before to two hours after now, each poll not yet written (an observation of the source at the game's landing or boxscore URL with that label, scheduled against a start within six hours of the game's current start, addendum 343 item 1: a postponement of six hours or more gets a fresh series beside the old rows) whose time has passed: T-120, T-90, T-60, T-45, T-35, T-30, T-15, T-0 GET `/v1/gamecenter/<gameId>/landing`, asserting `projected_starter` per side from leaders[0] as `scripts/nhl_leaders_order_receipt.py`'s `leader0` reads it (a side with none asserts nothing; neither side: `empty`); T+20 GET `/v1/gamecenter/<gameId>/boxscore`, asserting `eventual_starter` for each goalie with toi above zero as `nhl_goalies.boxscore_goalies` reads it (POST HOC; two in net: two rows, `attrs.two_in_net`; none: `empty`; a boxscore the ingest refuses: `failed`, its payload kept). A poll more than five minutes past its time is a `missed` row with no call; an answer stamped more than five minutes after its time is `late`. Players unmapped (counted, 254 item 2(3)); the team by nhl_goalies' ruled name path (`team_names` through the shared normaliser; the union of what the side's candidates name must be exactly one of the NHL competition's teams, 341 item 2), `source_team` the schedule's own text verbatim; the match by nhl_goalies' link, else `match_game` as it stands (a match two gameIds claim: neither), else NULL with `attrs.source_match_id` = the gameId; a team or the match unmapped makes the observation `partial`. `source_stamp` NULL. A 200 is kept under `logs/availability/nhl_api_landing/<date>/` by its sha256 (never data/). A tick with no poll due takes no lock; with one due it holds the chains' DB lock. `--dry-run`: the window's games and the polls due, no landing or boxscore call, nothing written or kept. No `--db`: an ingest writes the live DB only. Exit 0; 1 on the NHL day's FIRST failed schedule read (one notice a day, 340 item 4); 2 refused (no DB file; the store not migrated since #835 item 2's poll key: run `migrate_availability_record.py`). Spec: `docs/specs/availability-record.md` section 10. |

| `python3 scripts/restore_collided_rows.py` | `--db LIVE (--from COPY \| --from-json EXTRACT) [--ids A,B] [--book-rows refuse\|swap\|keep] [--move-added] [--emit-json PATH] --dry-run\|--apply` (exactly one of the last two) | THE RESTORE (ARCHITECT 2026-11-09, addendum 310 item 4 and addendum 312 item 3; #902), run by the architect's block on the laptop after the key fix is on main, a `.backup` first; the host's after the tag deploys, its rows read first. **The rows are found by PROVIDER ID and season, never by assumed row ids (312 item 3(e)):** for 18216 and 18240, the live file's one NCAA row of season 2026 and the copy's one row of season 2025 (the same row id: overwritten in place), each verified by team names (each file's teams table), kickoff and score against the pinned content; a live 2025 row for the id (already restored, or never collided), two live 2026 rows, or a copy row on another id is REFUSED. `--ids` is optional and must then name exactly the ids found. Exit 0 = planned (dry run) or applied; exit 2 = refused, nothing written. `--dry-run` opens the live DB `mode=ro` and writes nothing to the database file (SQLite may leave its own empty -wal/-shm sidecars beside a WAL file); `--apply` is ONE transaction (BEGIN IMMEDIATE, the live file opened `mode=rw`, which never creates a file; every check read again inside it; the rows written counted before the commit). It writes two NEW 2026 rows in the listing's orientation (Cincinnati home / Utah away 25-49; LSU home / Alabama away 13-31; every score pair swapped, `full_time_result` by the new orientation, `provider_orientation` removed) after checking that no row of any referencing table already sits on the new ids; moves every odds_snapshots / odds / match_stats row of the old ids to the new ids; rewrites the old rows to the copy's 2025 content column for column. **Sides:** a Kalshi odds_snapshots row swaps HOME<->AWAY on the move (3(a), ruled as built; each moved row's side printed before and after). A BOOK HOME/AWAY row (any odds row, a non-Kalshi snapshot) follows `--book-rows` (3(b); default refuse): each is printed with its captured_at (the tables have no written_at) against #885's fix (PR #888, merged 2026-11-08T21:21:44Z): after it, the stored orientation (swap); before it, the provider's, which is the new row's (keep). **Other referencing tables (3(c)):** a row identical in the copy (by its primary key) stays with the restored 2025 row; a row added since the copy is the 2026 game's and MOVES under `--move-added` (default refused; each listed, an ORIENTED one named: it moves as is); a changed or lost row is always REFUSED. availability_observations / availability_assertions are printed, never moved (3(d)). **`--emit-json PATH`** (with `--dry-run` and `--from` only, after every check passes; a NEW path): the two 2025 rows' fields and nothing else (provider id, season, kickoff, status, home and away names, the scores; no row or team ids), its sha256 printed: a receipt, not a database. Run it before the laptop's `--apply` if the host may need it (after the apply the laptop's dry run reads "already restored"). **`--from-json EXTRACT`** (the host, when no daily copy of its own holds the 2025 rows; decided after the host's read): the extract must read the pinned 2025 state; the restored row takes the extract's season, kickoff, status and scores on the live row's own team ids (their names checked), and the game columns the extract does not carry (status_raw, the ht and 90-minute scores, venue, referee, matchday, stage) are written NULL, each printed (law 4); with no copy of the other tables, their rows read as added (`--move-added`), and any availability row is refused. Tests: tests/test_restore_collided_rows.py. |
| `python3 scripts/closing_file_backfill.py` | `[--dry-run] [--mirror DIR] [--receipts FILE] [--exports DIR]` | ADDENDUM 40 ITEM 2, THE BACKFILL (ARCHITECT 2026-10-11; #428), run ONCE on the laptop **before the mirror's next squash, 2026-10-18 06:10Z**. Never opens the database. For every closing receipt whose page went out it finds the export's bytes in the exports-mirror clone's history (`SP_EXPORTS_MIRROR_DIR`, default `logs/exports-mirror`; every blob of every ref) by the receipt's `export_written.sha256`, reduces them to the receipt's Desk rows (its called-off games named in the closing block) and writes `exports/closing_<FAMILY>_<start>.json` by the run's own rule, its modification time the run's page time (`page.at`, else `completed_at`). One line per run: `WRITTEN` / `WOULD WRITE` (`--dry-run`) / `UNCHANGED` / `KEPT` (a file of that name with other bytes is never overwritten) / `SKIPPED` (the run wrote its own) / `NOT FOUND` (bytes not in the history: listed, nothing rebuilt) / `INCOMPLETE` (the bytes lack a Desk row the page carried: listed, nothing written) / `EARLIER` (addendum 41 item 3: the receipts hold a later page for the same family and start; the later page's file is written, the earlier listed). Last line `CLOSING-BACKFILL {...}`. The next mirror push carries the files. |

Per season 2023-24..2025-26 (the game id's first four digits) and game type, from the stored `nhl_shot_events`: it prints the events by type with and without a `goalie_id`, and the exclusions in the frozen xG rules' order (`nhl_xg.prepare`: blocked, shootout, empty net, missing coordinates, …, then kept but no goalie named). It reports the distribution, not the earliest success. One schedule call names the day's games (types 2/3, start in [date 10:00Z, +1 day)). Each game is polled at T-120, T-90, T-60, T-45, T-35 (the declared cutoff), T-30, T-15 and puck drop: `/v1/gamecenter/<id>/{boxscore,landing,right-rail}`, every request stamped by our clock. Per poll and side it records what each endpoint asserted (a starter flag, a starter/probable path, goalies only listed, or none) and the side's identity across endpoints: unique, conflicting or missing. Per game it prints when each identity was first seen and by which endpoint, whether both sides were unique at the T-35 poll itself (usable at the cutoff; a T-35 poll missed or unanswered is not usable and is counted, never filled from an earlier poll), and the missing, conflicting, withdrawn and changed counts before puck drop. A T+20 boxscore read gives the eventual starter, used only to check reliability; it never fills a gap. A poll more than 5 minutes late is missed. `--stored` reads `nhl_goalie_appearances` (mode=ro) and counts rows captured before their game's start. Run on three game days; the consoles go on #105. |
| `python3 scripts/nhl_leaders_order_receipt.py` | `DIR [DIR ...]` (also `PYTHONPATH=<checkout> python3 /tmp/nhl_leaders_order_receipt.py DIR [DIR ...]`) | NHL LEADERS-ORDER RECEIPT (ARCHITECT 2026-11-01 13:15 ET, addendum 200 item 1; lane nhl, the 11-05 step; #105), a script, READ-ONLY, no DB, no network. The directories are the probe's `--save` dirs (`<game id>_<endpoint>_<label>.json`, labels T-120 .. T-0 and T+20; other names counted, not read). Per directory, game and side: the landing page's `matchup.goalieComparison.<side>Team.leaders[0]` (playerId, its name beside it) at every pregame poll T-120, T-90, T-60, T-45, T-35, T-30, T-15, T-0, in poll order; a missing landing file, a file that does not parse, no goalieComparison, no or an empty leaders list, or a leaders[0] with no playerId is `missing at <label>` (reason printed, counted). (1) the status read or its equivalent (field names as served; values of the `--status-value-field` names only; the CurrentSeason read's bare integer year printed as the year, any other bare answer by its type only; a 200 error body refused); (2) the NHL games of `--date` (a UTC day, stated as such; the games as served, each start AS SERVED with its zone or offset as written); (3) the projected-starters endpoint: every field name as served, the projected starting goaltender per team per game, and THE SOURCE'S OWN STAMP (`--stamp-field`, no default, named from the vendor's documentation and quoted with `--stamp-quote`) against the start, BEFORE or NOT BEFORE read from the served values only (a value with no zone read only in `--served-zone`, no default); a stamp missing or unparseable refuses that game; (4) the vendor's terms from `--terms-url` (no default; retention and storage clauses quoted; none given: NOT READ, REFUSED; a page with no retention passage that names the data, a login, welcome or cookie page included (a retention word in page furniture such as "the App Store", "app stores", a "Cancel" button, "your session has expired", "clear your browser cache" or a menu item such as "Home \| Store \| Pricing" does not count), or a retention passage whose bare number the price rule replaced: REFUSED). No Kalshi section (KXNHLGAME untouched). A price-class field prints by name only and no figure prints, a price with no currency sign ("per month", "costs N") or with a currency word ("N pounds", "N British pounds", "N million yen", "N-pound fee", "N złoty", "Rs. N") included (279 item 3(c)). The base URL (`https://api.sportsdata.io`) and the three paths (`/v3/nhl/scores/json/CurrentSeason`, `/v3/nhl/scores/json/GamesByDate/{date_mon}`, `/v3/nhl/projections/json/StartingGoaltendersByDate/{date_mon}`) are VERIFIED defaults from the vendor's OpenAPI 3.1 document (read by the architect 2026-11-07, addendum 282 item 2(b)); the key header and field names are UNVERIFIED defaults the operator confirms on the first run. The probe cannot tell a scrambled trial value from a real one (282 item 2(c)): it reads structure. Exit 0 every read answered, 1 a call failed (first), 2 a read refused. Writes only `--out`, never under a `data/` directory, checked before any call. Run on the LAPTOP on a game day; its receipt goes on #105. |
| `python3 scripts/nba_certification_receipt.py` | `[--competition NBA\|NCAAMB]` · `[--season 2026 --season 2025 --season 2024]` · `--db COPY` | THE NBA DATA CERTIFICATION RECEIPT (ARCHITECT 2026-11-05, addendum 261 items 0 and 1(b); 271 item 3(e)-(f); 272 item 3), read-only, laptop. The served phase (default; opens no DB) reads /leagues once, then /teams and /games per season (7 calls for three seasons, each printing `x-ratelimit-requests-remaining`), and prints per season: status as served, stage and week as served, THE CANC BREAKDOWN (by UTC day, stage, week, FT-twin class, then every CANC row) BEFORE the map's projection, the codes left SCHEDULED counted, game counts with the FT-twin CANC ids excluded (listed by provider id, never deleted), the coverage basis SCHEDULED + FINISHED + POSTPONED, nulls and integrity. `--db COPY` (a `.backup` copy outside data/, opened `mode=ro&immutable=1`) prints the stored counts by status, `status_raw`, FINISHED-with-null-score (must be zero) and the FT-twin ids excluded. A season the provider does not serve is refused (exit 2). Last line `NBA-CERTIFICATION-RECEIPT {json}`. `--competition NCAAMB` (ARCHITECT 2026-11-12, addendum 352 items 2-3): league 116 ("NCAA", by exact served name), the 2026 season ONLY (any other refused, exit 2: 352 item 3(iv)), and THE TEAM-ID STOP in both phases (352 item 3(v)): served, the NCAAMB /teams ids and every /games side id (sync-matches looks each side up by provider id, so a side /teams does not list is still linked where a stored row carries its id) against the NBA's served /teams ids for 2026, 2025 and 2024 (3 more calls; an NBA read that fails, or answers no team id, is a stop, never a pass; with no NCAAMB season read the check is not run; an NCAAMB season answering no team or no game is FAILED, nothing to certify; on the copy, an NCAAMB season with 0 rows or 0 linked teams is FAILED, "nothing stored to certify" (#965, addendum 359 item 3)); stored, the team rows linked to both NCAAMB and the NBA on the copy. Each prints its count, and the ids if any; a non-zero count is `STOP`, exit 3, and comes to the architect. Last line `NCAAMB-CERTIFICATION-RECEIPT {json}`. |
| `python3 scripts/tennis_certification_receipt.py` | `[--tour ATP --tour WTA]` · `--db COPY` | THE TENNIS DATA CERTIFICATION RECEIPT (ARCHITECT 2026-11-06, addendum 269 items 1-3; addendum 284 item 1(b): "on the pattern of the NBA's (served and stored phases)"), read-only, laptop. The served phase (default; opens no DB, no key) prints, per tour, the /series listing's entry (every key, `contract_terms_url` and the fee fields verbatim), the /markets listing per status read to its last page (a cursor left REFUSES), the status and result words as served, THE CONTRACT TERMS before the map (`contract_terms_url`, every distinct `rules_secondary` and `early_close_condition` verbatim, one `rules_primary` per result word: 269 item 3(c), quoted on the PR from this console), the map's projection (both yes/no branches of addendum 296 item 2: (a) the 'yes' market's player named, (b) RESULT_WORD_ECHO, 'Yes' on the yes market and 'No' on the no market, counted per tour), every event left SCHEDULED and every event refused by its reason, THE expiration_value SHAPES of the events left SCHEDULED by WINNER_MISMATCH (addendum 288 item 2(b): one line per shape, every shape printed, the pair of values verbatim, the count, three samples with the 'yes' market's player; the events the kickoff rule refused are read too and counted apart, so the total reconciles with the map), counts by the ticker's year, THE TIMES (every served time key beside the ticker's date; the ruled kickoff, addendum 288 item 1: the counts by kickoff source, the kickoff refusals by label, the events whose kickoff's US Eastern day is not the ticker's date, and the active markets with their stored kickoff ahead of or past the run), nulls (START_UNREAD counted) and integrity. The stored phase (`--db COPY`, `mode=ro&immutable=1`, outside data/) prints the stored counts by season and status, `status_raw`, and the true-zero checks (FINISHED with no winner or no settlement kept, SETTLED_SCALAR carrying a winner, any score stored) and the rows by stored kickoff source and refusal labels (START_UNREAD counted; the refusal label 294 item 13(b) writes counted, addendum 297 item B.12). Last line `TENNIS-CERTIFICATION-RECEIPT {...}`. Writes nothing. |
| `python3 scripts/nfl_results_by_team_receipt.py` | `--db COPY --games-file PATH [--season 2026]` | THE NFL RESULTS BY TEAM RECEIPT (ARCHITECT 2026-11-08, addendum 295 item 5(d), #885), read-only, no network. The stored NFL rows of the season with status FINISHED, from a `.backup` copy outside data/ opened `mode=ro&immutable=1`, compared BY TEAM against nflverse's schedules `games.csv` (github.com/nflverse/nflverse-data, release tag schedules; a local file: #841 fetches the depth charts only, so no fetch is added). A row pairs with the game of the same two team codes (#841's `teams.tla` mapping) within a day of its US Eastern kickoff date; the mismatch list prints our home/away and scores by team beside nflverse's, the orientation (same / reversed) and nflverse's location; unmapped, not in the file, ambiguous and unscored rows are listed, never compared. Exit 0 every stored row compared and no mismatch, 1 a mismatch listed, 2 refused, 3 INCOMPLETE (no mismatch, but nothing compared or some stored rows not compared, each listed: never a clean pass). Last line `NFL-RESULTS-BY-TEAM-RECEIPT {json}`. |

## Hosting — laptop side (scripts, not cli.py)

| Command | Options | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `python deploy/hosting/pull_exports.py` | `--host --dest --transport {auto,rsync,scp}` | Pull the host's `exports/` over the tailnet into **`exports/host/`**, never the laptop's own `exports/` (writer of record through H1b). Host from `SP_HOST_ADDR` in `.env`. Newest-wins by mtime, idempotent, all-or-nothing (an unreachable host places nothing, exit ≠ 0). Receipt: pulled / unchanged / newest `window_24h.json`. On demand first; optional hourly launchd at :10 via `bash scripts/setup_export_pull.sh`. Laptop pulls; push is H2. |
| `python deploy/hosting/compare_exports.py` | `<laptop_dir> <host_dir> --glob --since N` | Parallel-week laptop-vs-host export diff (timestamps masked; game rows keyed on kickoff/home/away; code-version skew only with both `git_sha`s). `--since N` (default 3, 2026-10-01) compares only files whose name date is within the last N UTC days. Undated files (`window_24h.json`) are always compared, the skipped settled files are counted on the header line, and `--since 0` compares everything. Phase 2 of the morning chain (Tailscale). It is the release gate (docs/RELEASES.md). **The cutover PL check only (#420 / #422 RULED, ARCHITECT 2026-10-11):** `--ignore-keys K1,K2` leaves those keys out at any depth and prints them (`desk,desk_meta,line_move,late_news_flag,exported_at`: read off the clock) and REPLACES the timestamp mask: every other key, database timestamps and `match_id` included, is compared (`git_sha` reported on its own line); `--require-predictions` prints the count of rows with a prediction and its probabilities on each side and reads `NO-COVERAGE` (exit 3) when either side has none or nothing was compared (a missing host file). Both are off by default; every other comparison is unchanged. **NOTHING-TO-COMPARE (ARCHITECT 2026-11-11, addendum 334 item 3(a)):** "the verdict for zero compared files becomes NOTHING-TO-COMPARE (neutral, not failure), DIVERGENT reserved for files that were compared and differ": without `--require-predictions`, a run that compared no file reads `VERDICT: NOTHING-TO-COMPARE (0 compared[; N on one side only: …])` (exit 3, as NO-COVERAGE did), whatever sits on one side only; a file on both sides that does not parse stays DIVERGENT, and a run that compared at least one file reads as before. The mirror's Action posts it as state pending. **THE PAIRED KINDS (ARCHITECT 2026-11-11 08:45 ET, addendum 335 item 4(b)-(c), #924):** `--paired-kinds` (default the declared `PAIRED_KINDS`: `fixtures_NHL`) names the kinds both machines produce every day. A kind not on it is EXPECTED ONE-SIDED (`· NAME: only on SIDE (expected one-sided: KIND is not a paired kind)`, counted, never a cause). CLEAN needs at least one paired file compared with rows in its window (its declared rows list and its dated window both read; otherwise `window NOT READ`, NOTHING-TO-COMPARE, addendum 338 item 3), every paired file identical and no paired kind missing on a side. DIVERGENT is a paired file that differs, a paired kind missing on a side for a COMPLETE day (a date before the run's own UTC date), or a pair that does not parse. **336 item 4 (ARCHITECT 2026-11-11 09:35 ET):** a paired kind on one side only on the run's own UTC date (or later) is `awaiting <role>`, NOTHING-TO-COMPARE, ranked above CLEAN; an unpaired kind on both sides prints `unpaired, on both sides: identical` or `unpaired, on both sides: DIFFERS (not a verdict; pair it by ruling if both machines should make it)`, its diffs shown, counted, never a cause. Everything else is NOTHING-TO-COMPARE, an empty window included. A paired file compares only its rows dated the name's day -1, 0, +1 (the others counted as outside the window), leaves out `count`, and masks and counts its host-only columns, every key named and no prefix (336 item 4(1); `host-only columns (market, close_unpriced, kalshi, kalshi_legs, kalshi_bid/ask, away_bid/ask, exec_cost*, fee*, k_track, input_quality, desk, desk_meta; each named, no prefix): N`; a key `_fixture_row` writes that is neither compared nor on the mask fails the test until placed). A summary line `paired kinds (...): N compared with rows in the window · ... · expected one-sided (not a paired kind): N · unpaired, on both sides: N identical, N differ (not a verdict)` follows the counts. `--paired-kinds '*'` is the pre-335 rule (every kind paired, no window, no host-only mask); `--require-predictions` always uses it. **Machine state (#592 RULED, ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 128 item 3):** "the compare treats kind `coverage` as "machine state": listed by presence on each side (one side, both sides, neither), never compared for content, never DIVERGENT, never counted toward or against criterion (a). Printed as its own line ("machine-state files: coverage laptop yes / host no")." Kind is the exports mirror's (the name with its date/time stamp removed), so the dated `coverage_<date>.json` and the latest copy `coverage.json` are both kind coverage: in the window, such a file is never opened, never a `·` or `✗` line, never in the compared or one-side count, and never a verdict (a folder holding only it reads NOTHING-TO-COMPARE); the line `machine-state files: coverage laptop yes|no / host yes|no` is always printed, just before the `coverage:` tally. Presence is read among the files the compare selects (`--glob`, then the `--since` window): a coverage file a narrower glob or window leaves out reads `no` on the line and is counted on the tally (not matched) or the header (settled) instead; the Action's flags (`*.json`, `--since 0`) select the whole dated folder. `cutover-readiness` (a) applies the same rule. **The unit-bearing fields (ARCHITECT 2026-10-30, addendum 182 item 4, Desk policy v1.2, #732):** `order.unit`, `order.text` and `order.contracts`, `desk_meta.unit`, and `pool_usd` at any depth are never compared or printed, in either mode: each machine reads its own pool, so they differ by construction and carry a dollar figure. A mismatch printed whole (an `order` or `desk_meta` object against null) shows them as `<masked>`. Their count prints on its own line after the tally, `unit-bearing fields masked (policy v1.2): N`: every such field in the compared files, a field on either side counted once, the same in both modes (keys `--ignore-keys` prunes included); every other field, the exec figures included, is compared as before. **The shadow-arm keys (ARCHITECT 2026-11-01, addendum 199 item 2, pl-availability-v1):** "compare_exports treats every `candidate_*` key as "shadow-arm keys (host-only, pl-availability-v1)": never compared, never printed, counted on its own line beside the unit-bearing count (#735's pattern), so a real v22 divergence in the same file still prints inside the budget." Every key starting `candidate_` at any depth (the host's PL rows carry `candidate_version`, `candidate_probabilities`, `candidate_missing`, `candidate_reason`), and a results row's `candidate` when its value is the arm (a dict whose `version` is `pl-availability-v1`; another writer's `candidate` is compared), in either mode; `<masked>` in a mismatch printed whole; counted on the line right after the unit-bearing one, `shadow-arm keys (host-only, pl-availability-v1): N`, a key on either side once. **The graded-export keys (ARCHITECT 2026-11-01, addendum 206 item 1, #768):** "a NAMED, COUNTED machine-local class in compare_exports, "graded-export keys (machine-local)": every key of a results row's graded_export object and of the file-level graded_exports block, never compared, never printed as a diff, counted once per file on its own line beside the unit-bearing (#735) and shadow-arm (#761) lines" (each machine grades its OWN closing file, so the file name and run id differ by nature). A key named exactly `graded_export` (a results row's kind, file, run_id, exported_at) or `graded_exports` (the file's block: closing, slate, none, closing_files, slate_files, unreadable), at any depth, in either mode; `<masked>` in a mismatch printed whole; counted on the line right after the rehearsal-arm one (itself right after the shadow-arm one), `graded-export keys (machine-local): N`, every key of each object, a key on either side once (4 per results row, 6 for the block). `graded_export_x` and the row's `graded` block are compared. **The rehearsal-arm keys (ARCHITECT 2026-11-04, addendum 247 item 2(2), nfl-elo-v2):** every key starting `rehearsal_` at any depth (the NFL slate and closing rows' six `rehearsal_*` keys, a results row's and the record's `rehearsal_arm`), in either mode; `<masked>` in a mismatch printed whole; counted on its own line right after the shadow-arm one, `rehearsal-arm keys (host-only, nfl-elo-v2): N`, a key on either side once; the shadow-arm line and its count are unchanged, and the NFL file's top-level `rehearsal` is compared as before. |
| `cutover-readiness` (= `python deploy/hosting/cutover_readiness.py`) | `--mirror DIR --receipts FILE --named FILE --no-parity --fullseason-list FILE` | **#85, ARCHITECT lane 2 (2026-10-04); read-only.** The ruled cutover criteria in one readout: (a) the morning-compare streak from the exports mirror (dates, verdicts, the classes named per divergent line; 5 in a row needed; under each date the compare's `machine-state files:` line, kind coverage listed by presence and never counted toward or against (a): ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 128 item 3), (b) the host's tag, days on it, whether it carries #246 and desk calls emitted per chain (from the host's receipts), (c) the parity harness run on that tag in a scratch worktree. **(T9), an added binding condition (ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 122 item 3(b); #586):** the weekly full-season list the `SP_FULLSEASON_LIST` setting names (or `--fullseason-list FILE`, the host's file read on the laptop), counted through the chain's own parser and str.format (`sp_run.fullseason_resolve`; one competition per line, its `sync-matches` step: the `sync-teams` step before it, #765, is not counted twice): `fullseason list: N competitions — <CODE\|SEASON> · …`, or `fullseason list: EMPTY → NOT-YET`; the setting not set (or `--fullseason-list` given an empty path), the file missing or unreadable, or a line the chain cannot parse or fill (no `\|`, an empty code or season, a brace) are NOT-YET with the reason. An MLB line on the list binds: NOT-YET, with the line named (`⚠ MLB line(s) listed: …`, and `(T9) MLB line(s) on the list: MLB\|2026` in the last line; MLB is excluded by ruling, addendum 122 item 3(a); ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 128 item 1(b)). MLB lines are named once the list parses: an UNPARSEABLE list is NOT-YET on its own and names no MLB line (the one parser refuses the file at its first bad line). Last line `GO` or `NOT-YET — <unmet>` (exit 0/1), with earliest 2026-10-08. Class naming is conservative: only identical, capture timing and the guarded code-version skew are machine-named; anything else is UNNAMED unless `--named` names its date. GO is never the decision. **Run on the HOST, over ssh in the Thursday evening block (ARCHITECT 2026-10-18, addendum 128 item 1(a)):** (b) and (T9) read the host's own receipts and list; the laptop form (`--receipts FILE --fullseason-list FILE`) is for the architect's dry reads only. On the host, run it without `--receipts` or `--fullseason-list` (pass `--named FILE` when a day is operator-named). docs/specs/h2-cutover-runbook.md section 0. |
| `python deploy/hosting/pull_backup.py` | `--host --dest` | Nightly pull of the host's newest daily `.backup` into `~/sp-backups` (sha256 + integrity verified); launchd via `scripts/setup_backup_pull.sh`. |

## Web UI

`python main.py` serves the local browser app (FastAPI on
`localhost:8000`) — dashboard, competitions, teams, matches, and the MLB
card. NFL and cup views are not yet built (backlog U1); the CLI is the
complete interface for those.
The operated surface is the Cockpit, not this app (addendum 276 item 1): a
Claude artifact published separately, whose repo copy is `tools/cockpit.html`
(CLAUDE.md, opening; docs/specs/exports-mirror.md, "Published artifact").

---

## Required external services

| Provider | Used for | Endpoints touched | Env var |
|---|---|---|---|
| **API-Football** (api-sports.io v3) | Soccer: fixtures, teams, injuries, odds, players | `/fixtures`, `/teams`, `/injuries`, `/odds`, `/players` | `API_FOOTBALL_KEY` (required) |
| **MLB Stats API** (statsapi.mlb.com) | MLB schedules, scores, probables, appearances, umpires | `/schedule`, `/game/.../boxscore`, probables feed | none (public) |
| **API-Baseball** (api-sports.io v1) | MLB odds only | `/odds` per game window | `API_BASEBALL_KEY` (falls back to `API_FOOTBALL_KEY`) |
| **API-American-Football** (api-sports.io v1) | NFL teams, games, odds, injuries, rosters | `/teams`, `/games`, `/odds`, `/injuries`, `/players` | `API_AMERICAN_FOOTBALL_KEY` (falls back to `API_FOOTBALL_KEY`) |
| **API-Basketball** (api-sports.io v1, `v1.basketball.api-sports.io`; ARCHITECT 2026-11-06, addendum 271 item 2: THE SOURCE for lane basketball-market-only) | NBA teams and games (market-only; the adapter `api_basketball`) | `/leagues`, `/teams`, `/games` (no `/odds`) | `API_BASKETBALL_KEY` (falls back to `API_FOOTBALL_KEY`; one account key serves every API-Sports product, the plan is per product) |
| **Kalshi** (public market API) | Second market source: MLB (`KXMLBGAME`), NFL (`KXNFLGAME`), NHL (`KXNHLGAME`), NCAA football (`KXNCAAFGAME`), EPL (`KXEPLGAME`), NBA (`KXNBAGAME`, READ on #839); and for tennis the FIXTURE AND RESULTS source too: ATP (`KXATPMATCH`) and WTA (`KXWTAMATCH`), READ on #838 (addendum 269) | series/markets listing | none (public read) |
| **Open-Meteo** | Weather capture/backfill (MLB; NFL is backlog N1) | forecast + archive | none (public) |

Subscription notes: api-sports products are licensed separately —
football, baseball-odds, and american-football each need coverage on
your plan (a bundled key may serve all three; the clients fall back to
`API_FOOTBALL_KEY` where blank). Copy `.env.example` → `.env` and fill.

## Required local components

- Python 3.10+ with: `sqlalchemy requests python-dotenv click rich`
  (CLI core) plus `fastapi uvicorn jinja2` (web UI).
- SQLite (bundled with Python) — `data/sports.db`, created by `init-db`,
  **gitignored and irreplaceable** (point-in-time odds snapshots cannot
  be re-synced). Back up DAILY, and mandatorily before any
  `soccer-refresh` (CLAUDE.md law 5; `.backup` API only):
  `sqlite3 data/sports.db ".backup ~/backups/sports_$(date +%F).db"`.
- `.env` from `.env.example` (gitignored).
